Beginners favour colonies shot at several zooms; `singleP()` raises the share of single-view questions (up to 85%) as ability rises (`FORMAT`, one schedule with the traps, 2026-11-03); a single view counts as +0.5 harder (`SINGLE`). Other views show after the answer.
- **Glossary words** (`app.js` `GLOSS_PAT`, `gloss`, `glossAll`, `termDef`, `showGloss`; beginner review 2026-11-03): a glossary term in a text is underlined (dotted) and opens its definition on a tap (or focus + Enter): the plain-words sentence (`terms[i][3]`) first, then the sourced definition and its source. Glossed: the ID tips and lecture notes everywhere (`tipsList`, `notesList`), the Genera cards (`card`), the wrong-answer line of the quiz ("Your answer, X, would show: ..."), and in the Coral ID Key the tips, the Coral ID Key criteria (the Coral Finder book's features), the next-clue questions and the box / clue buttons (inside a button the word is tappable but no focus stop of its own, `gloss(text, seen, true)`; a tap opens the definition, not the box). Each term is underlined once per text block, at its first occurrence (`seen`; `glossAll` makes one list one block), and the longer pattern wins where two start ("axial corallite", "hooded corallites" over "corallite"). Corallite and Polyp are glossed too, and since the plain genus sheets the 9 terms of `apply_plain_terms.py` `MORE`, and "septal teeth" opens Septa. The first starter card says once "Tap an underlined word to see what it means" (`quiz.js` `introShow`, on the first card whose tips hold a term). `tests/gloss.test.js`. Lecture group titles on Genera are `.gtitle` (they were `.gt`, the glossary class, and looked tappable).
- **Bonus genera** (Pierre, 2026-11-01; opt-in since 2026-11-02; `BONUS` (`core.js`), `isBonus()`, `bonusList()`, `bonusSet()` in `app.js`, `bonusOfferDue()` / `bonusOfferShow()` in `quiz.js`): 5 extra Dauin genera (`DATA.x.gen`): Isopora, Leptoseris, Heliopora, Sandalolitha, Pleuractis (Millepora, first of them until 2026-11-02, is now a slate genus: `BENTHIC` above). Chosen as: 4+ Research Grade iNat observations within 40 km of Dauin, and a slate look-alike named by the Coral Finder (`look`/`lsrc`), or not a hard coral (Heliopora). **Offered once**, when every slate genus has been asked at least once (`asked`; met alone is not enough: an RA who taps Open all genera would otherwise get it a question later, Pierre 2026-11-02), by a card over the question (a prompt like the others, `promptOk`): "5 more genera?", "Not on the IMR coral list, but common at Dauin: you may meet them on dives and in ReefCloud photos.", **Add them** / **Not now**. The choice is `Q.bon` in `reefid-sync-coral-quiz` (synced with the quiz settings; `Q.bonT` = when it was made, so the latest choice wins across devices in `sync.js` `mergeSaved`, and a newer progress reset from another device clears it: Codex review #371; `mergeUsers` keeps it on if either account had it on); ⚙ **Bonus genera On / Off** changes it any time (Off takes them out of the draw, their `P[n]` stays). Players whose bonus genera were unlocked by the former progress thresholds (`Q.bu` 2 or more: Millepora counted first) start with it on; a progress reset clears it (the offer comes again). Once on, they join the genera not met yet and open like slate genera (`openPick()`, `openChance()`, `OPEN`: easiest first, the shaky slope, the opening hold), each on its first-meeting card (kicker "Bonus genus", "Not on the IMR list, but common at Dauin."); no threshold, no weight cap. They are choices and distractors like slate genera (`distractors()` matches look-alikes both ways), duel with their slate look-alike (`duelPick`), have their own `P[n]` (synced), count as "wrongly given" (rule L2), and never count in the progress (`progPct`, the slate tiles, the sync summary). My progress shows them after the slate genera in all three views once on (photo wall: tiles tagged "Bonus"; Slate: a strip under the slate; Groups: a "+" row). ID tips are labelled "ID tips (Coral Finder p. …)", never IMR. `#learn/compare/A/B` accepts Millepora and bonus genera (`gameGenus()`). A two-level bonus genus counts its `medium` photos as close-ups in games (Sandalolitha). Game photos (Millepora too): Research Grade only (`p.gid` = not Research Grade, set by `tools/apply_bonus.py`), Pierre's own always, never multi-coral. Memory (Lab `pairs`) does not use them. Until 2026-11-02 they unlocked one by one with the progress (10%, then every 5%, `BONUS_AT`), weighed at most 4 each and 15% together, and each unlock had a flip card (`unlockShow`): all removed.
- **Photo difficulty** `photoDiff()`: the sheet's hierarchical photo Elo (`?stats=1`, all players, cached in `reefid-photo-stats` with the sheet's `v`): a photo's rating is its genus's offset plus its own residual (sheet v31, `core.js` `GENUS_K`), shrunk towards the genus's offset (`?stats=1` `gd`, `model.js` `PGD`, read only from a sheet that reports v ≥ 31, rule D4) by `PDIFF.W` = 5 answers; a photo nobody has answered is its genus's offset, 0 with an older sheet. It is the difficulty of a photo met for the first time. The Easy / Medium / Hard photo sets that gave the prior until 2026-11-03 are gone (they predicted the answers worse than no prior: docs/LEARNING.md "Hierarchical photo Elo").
- **Photo memory** (`PMEM`, `famOf`, `pgMemW` in `model.js`; `core.js` `MEM`, `memBucket`, `memFam`, `memOf`; sheet v26, Pierre 2026-11-03): a question on photos the player has already been shown (`s.ps`: [shown, right]) is expected easier by the offset of their memory bucket (new 0, shown never right, right once, right twice or more), estimated by the sheet (`?stats=1` `mem`), the old fixed rule as prior with an older sheet. Those offsets are the corals' (the sheet rates only the subject it replays, `SUBJECTS` `replay`): every use reads them through `model.js` `pmem()`, which gives any other subject (fish, until a per-subject `?stats=1`) the prior, so no coral offset touches a fish answer (2026-11-08). The ability step, the global level, the logged `e`, the opening hold and the comeback all use that expectation; a right answer counts in the progress only for what memory does not explain (`pgMemW`); the photo draw weighs a photo already shown e^offset (`photoSet`). Progress saved before it is recalculated once, silently (`core.js` `memRecalc`, `model.js` `memRecalcAll`; genus records marked `s.pm`, kept with `s.pg` by `mergeProgress` and `mergeUsers`). The same call drops a learned mark passed under BKT (a record without `s.pg`), silently since 2026-11-09 (Q332: `model.js` `mxRecalc` no longer calls `mxLost`; the achievements kept; docs/LEARNING.md "Learned under the former model"). `tools/memreplay.js` replays a saved answer log under the old and the new rule. docs/LEARNING.md "Photo memory". The admin "Without me" switch recomputes it without Pierre's answers for Stats only; the games still use everyone's.

### Lab prototypes (`proto/`)
A static mockup for one of Pierre's decisions may also sit in `proto/` as a standalone `.html` page (not a Lab tool: not in `LAB.files`, not cached by the service worker, nothing links to it; it borrows `../app.css` for the tokens and fonts): `fish-sheets.html`, the fish sheets' genus as a block or a page (Q2 of docs/reviews/2026-11-05-fish-sheets-study.md), removed once he has chosen and the real sheet is built.

`lab.js` holds the hub, `LAB.files`, `LAB_V` (cache version), `GRADUATED` (tools promoted into a section, currently `idkey`, `pairs` and `basics`; the Tools page lists them under "Now in the main sections") (the target section of each remaining prototype is in ROADMAP, the module registry row). One file per tool: `basics` (Coral basics: a 7-step guided path for players who never had the coral lecture, described below), `anatomy` (Corallite anatomy), `map` (mind map), `web` (look-alike web), `zoom`, `size`, `elim`, `matrix`, `spot`, `reveal` (Blur reveal), `gallery`, `pairs`, `idkey`. `cards` is not a tool but the live gallery of the app's cards and pop-ups (`#tools/cards/<kind>`: apology, update, feature, genus, tip, bonus, account, ach, toast), each opened through `app.js` `cardShow` with sample copy and nothing recorded (the sign-in card, the achievement toast and the note are the real ones); it was the three-direction mock-up Pierre chose from (2026-11-03, B Medallion; commit f46f11c). A prototype marked `admin: true` is listed in the hub in the admin's browser only (`IS_ADMIN`); its link works for anyone.

**Coral basics** (`proto/basics.js`, styles `.bx-*` / `.bxd` in `proto/lab.css`, 2026-11-03; graduated from the Lab into Learn the same day, Pierre: step 0 of the Learn path, `GRADUATED.basics`, the first Learn tab, the "0 · Coral basics" block of Genera; the Tools page keeps a link; still a tier 5 file). Routes: `#learn/basics` (resumes at the last step: the Basics tab, the Home line and the quiz's offers), `#learn/basics/<step>` (`colony`, `polyp`, `corallite`, `septa`, `teeth`, `walls`, `forms`), `#learn/basics/walls/<view>` (`separate`, `shared`, `valleys`, `nowall`), `#learn/basics/<term>` (a glossary term such as `Coenosteum` opens its step and view), `#learn/basics/words`; every old `#tools/basics…` link lands on the same `#basics…` page (`viewLab`); `|edit` logs photo coordinates on a tap and shows the tap targets. Each step: one photo, Pierre's (CC BY) or an IMR lecture photo (`PH`, coordinates in pixels of the 960 px file, crops `[x, y, w, h]`) under an SVG layer (rings that dim the rest, leader lines) with HTML labels (constant text size), or a drawing of ours (theme tokens, the taught part in `--hl`); one plain sentence; the official line from `data.json` `terms` (IMR Lecture 6 / Corals of the World) with its source; an optional check (`choice`, `draw`, `tap` with hit points, `pick` a photo, `photo` / `form` classification; growth form definitions from `forms.json` `forms`). Once answered, every check shows the app's report button (`repBtn`, mode `basics-<step>`, with the answer and the photos shown; rule U14) and its photos open the enlarged view (blind before the answer). The polyp step shows one large open polyp, *Tubastraea* (IMR lecture photo `imrl-p19_g64`, `POLYP`): mouth and ring of tentacles labelled, nothing dimmed (Pierre, 2026-11-03, found his earlier pick *Galaxea* too busy for a beginner); the day-extended *Goniopora* is only an aside. Every teaching photo is itself the enlarge button (`.bx-zoom`, the app's `ZOOMI` mark; no "Enlarge" text link): the enlarged view shows the whole photo with the step's labels over it and a Labels on/off switch (`app.js` `zoom(list, i, {over})`, a layer `.lb-over` kept on the photo's box by a `ResizeObserver`, its SVG sized to fill it, at fit and at actual size; `ANN` keeps each photo's marks). Rings dim the rest of the photo only lightly (`.bx-dim` 0.22, 2.5 px ring). Step 4 (`septa`) teaches septa and costae together on an IMR lecture photo of *Diploastrea* (`PH.septa`, `imrl-p57_g172`: low cones, each with a small cup whose rim is sharp, short beaded septa inside it and the same blades running on as ridges down the cone outside it; Pierre, 2026-11-03: "if you define septa, you must define costae", and on review: costae are immediately outside the wall, the septa carried on past the rim, never the skeleton between the cups, which an earlier *Astrea* photo pointed at): a dashed rim (`['rim', …]`), hollow rings (`hollow`) on the mouth, on a septum inside the rim (`--hl`, class `sep`) and on the same blade just past it (`--hl2`, the palette accent, class `cos`; `basicsTeach` keeps it between 1.05 and 1.25 rim radii), the drawing in the same two colours with its key (`cupTop('both')`, `sepKey`), and Costae as a term of its own (`more`, with the IMR Lecture 6 slide 13 line). Step 6 (`walls`) shows the four wall types one after the other (Pierre, 2026-11-03: players looked at Separate and moved on): numbered tabs with ✓ for the types seen, a sentence per type, and the step's main button reads "Next wall type: …" until the four are seen (`reefid-basics` `walls`), then "Next: Growth forms"; its check stays folded until then. Step 7 (`forms`) picks a form on drawn icons (`FORM_ICON`, ours, from the IMR definitions in `forms.json`) and shows 2-3 large example photos of it (`FORMS7`, picked by eye among the game photos of that form, different genera, shown in rule P1's order: IMR, then Pierre's, then other observers', `provOrder`; Tabulate has two, all *Acropora*; a photo no longer in `data.json` drops out), each enlarged with its genus and credit. Once answered, the check photos also carry the games' clickable source pill (`photoSrc`, rule P5; hidden on phones), and every photo is bounded by the screen's height (`--h` × `--ar` in `proto/lab.css`, rule U2); `tests/pages.smoke.js` `basicsFit` checks both at 390×844 and 768×1024, and `basicsTeach` (390 and 1280 px) the septa/costae/centre points against the rim, the costae term, the enlarged views with their labels, the four wall types before the next step, and the form icons with their 2-3 large examples. The walls step shows IMR's four wall structures, the fourth (indistinguishable walls) on *Pavona*. Nothing is forced: the stepper jumps anywhere, Next never waits for the check. Per-viewer state in `localStorage` `reefid-basics` (`app.js` `BASICS_KEY`: `last`, `done` checks passed, `walls` seen, `seen` steps, `fin` the time it was done), browser only, never synced, cleared at sign-out with the progress (Codex review #428: on a shared device the next player must not inherit it); **done** = every step seen (the walls step once its four types are; the checks stay optional), which grants the achievement "Coral basics" (`achBasics()`, feat `_basics`, synced with the achievements record), so `app.js` `basicsDone()` (local `fin` or the synced feat) is true on every device of the player: it hides the Home line and the first-launch offers of Coral basics. The words, their step (or Corallite anatomy card) and their plain line are one table in the first tier, `app.js` `BASICS_WORDS` (`basicsRoute`, `basicsListed`; the module's Words list and `window.BASICS` read it), so the glossary popup links the same way before and after the Tools tier is in (Codex review #428): the glossary popup of every underlined term (ID tips on genus sheets, first-meeting cards, quiz feedback, Genera cards; `app.js` `showGloss`, `basicsHref`) ends with a link to its step ("Coral basics: on a photo"), Corallite anatomy, or the Words list (none for a word Coral basics does not have). Every label was placed by looking at the photo (rule W11); the arrangements in the walls check follow the IMR lecture lines of each genus.

### Install the app (`app.js`, 2026-11-08)

Pierre, 2026-11-08: nudge phone players to install the app (an icon on the home screen) instead of opening a link in the
browser. `installNudge` offers one card (`installCard`, `cardShow` kind `feature`, `.unlock.install`, the app's icon in
the medallion) on a touch screen (`pointer: coarse`), from the 3rd visit (`INSTALL.VISITS`; a visit = a load 30 minutes
or more after the last, `installVisit`) or once 10 answers are played in any subject (`INSTALL.ANSWERS`, `answersAll`: a
full session trail), whichever comes first; on Home at a calm moment, like `progress.js` `subjHello` (never over a quiz
question; signed in, after the visit's first sync, `noticeLater`; spaced from the other prompts and never with another
card, `quiz.js` `promptOk`/`promptMark`). Android Chrome and its family: the `beforeinstallprompt` event is held
(`preventDefault`, no bar of the browser's own) and the card's **Install the app** calls its `prompt()`; iPhone and iPad
Safari (`installIos`: no prompt API) get the steps (Share, or ••• first on a recent iPhone, then Add to Home Screen,
then Add) with Safari's icons inline (`INSTALL_ICONS`); any other browser gets no card. The card closes for good however
it closes (but by leaving the page), and never shows once installed (`appinstalled`, an accepted prompt, or the app
opened standalone: `display-mode: standalone` or `navigator.standalone`) or in the installed app. The device record
`reefid-install` (`{n: visits, t: last load, x: closed, in: installed}`) stays on the device (never cleared at sign-out:
it is about the device, not the player). An iPhone's home-screen app has its own storage apart from Safari's, so Safari
cannot know it was installed: its card is closed for good by its button. ⚙ Settings keeps an **Install the app** row
(`#instrow`, hidden in the installed app): the browser's prompt at once where there is one, else the card (the steps,
or the browser menu's Install app / Add to Home screen) without marking anything. Nothing new is logged: the `load`
event's `st` already says installed or not. Tests: `tests/install.test.js` (the decision), `tests/pages.smoke.js` part
`install` (390 px: an Android-like prompt event, iPhone Safari, the installed app, the 2nd visit, a quiz question, the
⚙ row).

### Service worker (`sw.js`)

Registered by `app.js` once every tier is in (https only); it serves the app offline and fast on a poor line. Tested by
`tests/net.smoke.js` (rule W14).
- **Caches.** The shell, `V` = `reefid-shell-vN` (`SHELL`: the page, the scripts of every tier, the JSON the app starts
  with, the fonts, `curation.json`, the Lab files), stamped by the deploy from a hash of what it keeps (rule W15) and
  replaced at each new version (`REPLACES`: every older shell cache, under the old name too). The photos, `imr-photos-v1`,
  which no deploy deletes (at most `PH_MAX` = 2,500 entries, oldest first out). The admin's own code,
  `reefid-admin-files` (`ADM_FILE`: `admin*.js`, `admin*.css`, `autosort.js`, `analysis.js`; one entry per file, never
  player data), which no deploy deletes either.
- **Install**: each shell file on its own, tried up to 3 times; a versioned file (`?v=`) or a font that an older cache
  already holds is copied from it, the other files are asked from the server (`no-cache`, never the browser's HTTP
  cache); then `skipWaiting`.
- **Activate**: photos still in an old shell cache move to the photo cache, the old shell caches go, `clients.claim`.
  Each open app page (`index.html` only) is asked its version (`reefid-ver?`): a page that answers reloads by itself at
  its next calm moment (`app.js` `UPDATED`: the next page change, or back into view unless a question waits for its
  answer, `QOPEN`); one that does not answer within 3 s runs a version from before 2026-11-03 and is reloaded now.
  `app.js` `swCheck` asks for a new version when the page comes back into view (at most every 5 min) and every 30 min.
- **A page no service worker controls** (a browser without one, such as a chat app's built-in browser or some private
  windows, or one that blocks it; 2026-11-08): `swCheck` also reads, at those same moments, the deployed page itself
  (`buildCheck`: the page's own address, `cache: 'no-store'`) and compares its `<meta name="build">` (the deploy's run
  number, `tools/stamp_versions.py`) with the running page's; a higher one counts as a takeover (`UPDATED`, the same
  calm reload, never while a question waits for its answer). Higher, not different (`newBuild`): a host's cache still
  serving the older page would otherwise reload it in a loop. A copy never deployed has no build number and reads
  nothing. Tests: `tests/update.test.js`, `tests/net.smoke.js` check 13 (service workers blocked, the build stamped by
  the test server).
- **Fetch**: photos (`/img/` on the site or on R2) cache first, looked up under both addresses (`twinOf`); from R2 in
  CORS mode, two tries `PHOTO_RETRY` ms apart, a 404 not retried, then the failure goes to the page, which picks the
  next source (`app.js` `photoFail`), and R2 is not asked for `R2_PAUSE` (60 s). Versioned files and fonts: cache first,
  no network once cached. The other app files (the page, `curation.json`, the manifest): revalidated with the server; a
  cached copy is served after `SLOW` (2 s) on a slow line while the network refreshes it. The page's reply never waits
  for its cache write (written in the event's lifetime, at most 30 s).
- **No time limit on Cache Storage**: every reply waits for it (the `soon` limit of #531 was removed on 2026-11-08). It
  was added for the net.smoke step-9 hang, read then as a stalled Cache Storage; that hang was traced on 2026-11-05 to a
  navigation that reached the old worker in the instant Chromium stopped it for a version swap (the test now opens the
  admin on the version already installed, #615), and no Cache Storage stall has been seen.

## Subjects (the taxon model, 2026-11-03)

Pierre, 2026-11-03: fish in the app on the same engine as corals, cleanly, with a Corals / Fish selector (on Home, the header and ⚙ at first; in Tools, admin only, for part of 2026-11-04; since then the subject switch, "Switching" and "Beta" below). A **subject** ("matière") is what the games teach; the engine (`model.js`, `quiz.js`, `progress.js`) never names one, it works on the **current subject**.

- **Registry** (`core.js` `SUBJECTS`, pure data, tested in `tests/subjects.test.js`): per subject `id` (`coral`, `fish`), `dev` (prefix of its device keys), `base` (the sign-in record's fields for its progress last sent / a send on its way: `base` / `pending` for corals, `fbase` / `fpending` for fish), words (`name` Corals / Fish, `one` genus / family, `many`, `what`, `lecture`), `icon` (its icon in the subject switches, the app's and the admin's), `views` (corals: colony, close-up, corallites; fish: one photo), `optin` (the opt-in sets: corals `bon`; fish `c2`, `c3`), `log` (answers to the sheet: corals only), `ach` and `board` (achievements and leaderboard: corals only), `pages` (its Learn / Practice pages: corals every one, `sheet` = a genus sheet; fish `genera`, `sheet` (its family sheets, below) and `quiz`), `sheets` (fish: the address word of each rank of its sheets and the prefix of their ids, `{family: 'f:', genus: 'g:', species: 's:'}`: `#fish/learn/family/Damselfishes`, `#fish/learn/genus/Chromis`, `#fish/learn/species/Chromis%20ternatensis`; `routeOut`, `routeIn`; `core.js` `sheetIdOf(S, id)` gives a sheet id in its one spelling, an old unit id renamed first, t01 → f:Damselfishes, and null for an id that names none of the subject's sheets, so a coral genus sheet linked with Fish chosen stays the corals', `app.js` `subjHas`, `hr`), `progress` (My progress views), `remote` (its photos are iNaturalist's: preloaded all the same). Keys: `subjKey(id, name)` = `reefid-sync-<id>-<name>` (`progress`, `quiz`, `intro`, `intro-offer`, `reset`, `wink`: travel with the player), `subjDev(id, name)` = `reefid-<dev><name>` (`hold`, `comeback`: stay on the device; the corals' `dev` is empty, so their keys are the same as before subjects). The choice: `SUBJECT_KEY` = `reefid-sync-subject` (synced). `admin` {`hash`, `cur`, `pages`, `origins`, `csrc`}: the subject's part of the admin ("Admin back-office", one subject switch); `origins` (where its photos come from, `[key, name, short name]`: corals IMR lecture, Pierre, iNat Dauin / Philippines / elsewhere; fish the same without IMR) and `csrc` (its Candidates sources) fill the one filter bar ("Admin for every subject").
- **What reads the subject's data** (`app.js` `UNITS[id]`): `load()` (fish: `fishLoad`, fish.json + fish-photos.json + fish-game.json, fetched when the subject is fish, else at the first switch; all three in the `sw.js` shell) and `home()`, what start-up waits for when it has one (fish: `fishHome`, fish.json and fish-photos.json only, Pierre 2026-11-09: Home before fish-game.json, its photos first the stopgap's; fish-game.json then comes with tier 2, which `need(2)` holds for the subject's `load()`, so the quiz and the Learn pages open on the curated photos, and the units are built again with them; a device whose service worker already holds fish-game.json, `fishCached`, waits for it at start-up as before; meanwhile `dataPartial` (`UNITS` `partial`) holds `model.js` `domRecalcAll`, the one-time rebuild of an old record's bar, which reads the units' photo difficulties, until the whole data is in; `tests/fishhome.test.js`; `fishBuild` makes `FISH` again whenever `build()` applies a new curation), `slate()` (the units the games teach and the progress counts: `slateOf(DATA)` / the circle-1 entries), `sets` (opt-in sets: units, when due, the card's words, ⚙ row; `bonusList`, `setOf`, `isBonus`, `bonusOfferDue`, `bonusOfferShow`, `bonusSet` are generic), `names()` and `norm` (typed answers: `typeNamesOf` + `typeNorm`, the first word; `fishTypeNames` + `typeNormAll`, the whole name: the entry's names (`core.js` `fishNames`: IMR name, its parts, families, sub-entries, the curated other names `aka` of `tools/fish_source.json`, e.g. Tang, and its names in common use `often`, e.g. Clownfish, right but corrected gently in the verdict line: `fishOften`, `oft`, `quiz.js` `typeStep`), plus, with `SUBJECTS` `typeDrop` ('fish'), each name without "fish" (Trigger, Damsel, Clown), its plural, and the name's own plural or singular, left out when within the spelling tolerance of another entry's name or short form; fish beta QA, Pierre 2026-11-06), `taxon(p, n)` (the photo's own taxon, finer than the unit `n` asked, said in one short line under the verdict after every answer, right or wrong: `app.js` `photoTaxon`, `taxonLine`, `.fbsp`; fish: `fishTaxon`, the species the photo is in play for (`sp`, `FISH.sps`), its first curated common name and its PDF name with the WoRMS name beside it, rule S2; corals: `coralTaxon`, the photo's iNaturalist species only inside the genus asked and only when Research Grade or Pierre's own, else nothing; Pierre, 2026-11-07, 2026-11-08, go 2026-11-09; tests `tests/answertaxon.test.js`, `tests/fish.smoke.js`), `pairs()` (duels), `winks()`, `list()` (the Learn page: `viewGenera` / `viewFishList`), `sheet(id)` (a sheet: `viewGenus` / `viewFishSheet`), `bar(id)` (fish: the page bar of its sheets, `learn.js` `fishBar`; the corals' is `ctxBar`'s own), `help`, `progHelp` (the ? texts, fish only; the corals keep theirs inline), `hello` (fish only: the words of its cards, one per way it opens, below), `about` (fish only: its lines in About and credits, shown only to a player it is open to, `subjMine`).
- **Records**: every unit is read through `GX(n)`: coral genera by name (`DATA.gen`, `DATA.x.gen`), fish entries by teaching id (`FISH.gen`, built by `core.js` `fishGen`; ids and genus names never meet). A fish record has the fields the engine reads on a genus: `look` (teaching ids, sub-entries folded into their entry), `lsrc` (IMR fish presentation), `qt` (the IMR cues), `forms` (its group in the identification key in force, so the first wrong choices come from other groups as coral ones come from other growth forms: `imrFormsOf` returns a record's own `forms`), `group` and `ig` (that group), `fam`, `cm` (√ of its species' Research Grade observations near Dauin: common first), `views.wide`, `levels: 1`, plus `name` (shown: `uname()` = a genus in italics, a fish entry by its IMR name; `unameT()` plain text), `lk` (IMR's words to tell each look-alike apart), `sub`, `phase`, `names` (accepted when typed: the IMR name, the parts of a double name, the IMR and WoRMS families, the sub-entries; a family two entries share, Holocentridae or Serranidae, is "which one?"), `wink`.
- **Identify per subject** (Pierre, 2026-11-04): Identify shows the current subject's tool only, `UNITS[id].identify` (corals `idkey`, the Coral ID Key; fish `fishkey`, the Fish ID Key, coming soon: `home.js` `viewFishKey`), one entry of `MODES.identify` each. `subjPage` sends a bare Identify address to the subject's own tool (a subject switched on Identify shows the other one's), `route` hands a link into another subject's tool with its clues (`#identify/key?form=…`) to that subject, as it does a genus sheet; Home's Identify door follows the subject (a fish photo, "Identify a fish", the Fish ID Key). `tests/fish.smoke.js` checks both ways.
- **Switching** (`setSubject`; Pierre, 2026-11-04, docs/DESIGN.md "Subject switch": two places, one state, both `[data-subj]` buttons built by `subjBtns`: a full-width Corals | Fish row at the top of Home, `home.js` `.hsubj`, and next to the logo in the header on every page but Home, `#subjc` (on Home `route` sets `data-home` on it: hidden with its room kept, its menu closed, a focus on it or on Home's row given to Home's row once drawn; Pierre, 2026-11-08), drawn by `showSubj` as `subjCtl` says: with two subjects a one-click switch `.subjsw` (Pierre, 2026-11-08), shown as one chip `[data-flip]` where its two sides do not fit (under 380 px beside the Sign in pill); with three or more the subject chip `#subjb` with its small menu `#subjmenu`, opened and closed by `subjMenu` (a choice, Escape or a tap elsewhere closes it); a switch used from the keyboard keeps the focus once redrawn; each subject's icon is `UNITS[id].icon`; both shown only while the player has two subjects or more, `subjMine()` = `SUBJECTS.filter(subjOpen)`: every player's since fish opened to all on 2026-11-06, Q158; only the admin's and its group's for a subject with `open: 'group'`, "Beta" below): the subject's data, then `subjectLoad`: `P`, `Q` from its keys, and every file's per-subject state through `SUBJ_HOOKS` (`model.js`: cards seen, opening hold, comeback, genus difficulty, last asked, the photo memory recalculation; `quiz.js`: typed names, questions planned ahead, duel spacing, the visit's trail; the winks shown travel with the player, `reefid-sync-fish-wink`). `inSubject(id, fn)` runs `fn` as if `id` were current and comes back (synchronous; the hooks reload only what is on the device): the sync summary and the achievements on the corals, every subject's merge at a sync.
- **Sync** (`sync.js`): every `reefid-sync-*` key travels already (`progData`), so the fish progress, quiz settings and cards go to the sheet's Data column and progress files. `mergeSaved` merges each subject on its own (`mergeSubject`, inside `inSubject`: reset, opt-in choices with their times, cards, hold, the three-way `mergeProgress` against its `base`); `syncSummary` (the leaderboard %, "By genus") is always the corals'. Sign-in to a known account: corals as before (ANONB delta); fish, never logged, adds everything on the device (`mergeProgress({}, here, sheet)`). Sign-out clears every subject's keys. `core.js` `mergeUsers` (admin account merge) merges each subject's progress and opt-ins on its own. Fish answers are logged to the sheet's Answers tab since sheet v60 (#630, "Every subject's answers, at any rank"), but not replayed (`replay: false`): the photo ratings, confusions and replay stay the corals' (ROADMAP). Reports (`repBtn`) from a fish question carry the teaching id.
- **Full replay** (sheet v28+, `core.js` `RVS`; fish too, `SUBJECTS` `replayed`, its own rules versions `FISH_RVS`, "Other subjects' replays" below, which supersedes what this line says of fish): only the subject the sheet replays is replayed (`SUBJECTS` `replay`: the corals; every subject's answers are logged since sheet v60, "Every subject's answers, at any rank"). `sync.js` `replayAdopt` always runs as that subject (`inSubject`), whatever the subject on screen, so a replay never lands in the fish progress; `mergeSubject` asks for and adopts the replay, and compares rules versions (`RVK`, `reefid-sync-coral-rv`), for that subject only; the fish progress merges with the three-way `mergeProgress` against its own base (`fbase`). Safe because the sheet replays the corals' rows only (`Code.gs` `answerRows_`, Subject column, v60): the day fish is replayed, the fish subject gets its own rules version key. While a visit runs (`SUBJ_VISIT`), nothing redraws the page. The one-tap question and the apology card are the corals' (`pulseDue`, `sorryNotice` wait for `SUBJ.replay`). The sheet's opening hold is the corals' too (`sync.js` `holdAdopt`, as that subject). At sign-in every subject takes the account's copy as its merge baseline, played on the device or not. A wink is decided before a question is taken or chosen (`quizPhoto`), so planned questions and the Again / duel spacing wait. A genus sheet or comparison (`#learn/genus/…`, `#cmp-…`) opened with a subject that has none (Identify's Coral ID Key with Fish chosen) switches to the subject that has it (`route`, `setSubject`); the other way, a subject chosen by the player on such a page opens its section's page for that subject (`setSubject`, `subjPage`), not the hand-off back. `fishLoad` refuses fish data that did not come (`json()` answers `{}`), so nothing empty is cached and the app opens on the corals until a later try. Reports from a fish question keep the teaching id and the remote photo id (sheet v34 `logReports_` accepts digits in a unit); admin To do › Reports names the fish entry and shows its iNaturalist photo (`admin.js` `fishAdmin`, `repUnit`). A saved copy without a subject's keys (saved by an app from before fish) is not an empty record: `mergeSaved` leaves that subject alone (a reset is an empty record with a reset time, never an absent key), and the sheet keeps the saved copy's keys of a subject the saving app does not know (v34, `core.js` `keepSubjectKeys`). Tools › Type the genus stays on the corals whatever the subject on screen (`proto/type.js` `CORALS`; `quiz.js` `typeForm` / `typeBind` take the subject).
- **Beta** (`SUBJECTS` `beta` {kick, line}, fish, the **label**; who sees the subject is `SUBJECTS` `open`, below): **the admin's only from 2026-11-04 to 2026-11-06**, every player's since (Q158, next item) (Pierre, 2026-11-04: "some things clearly aren't ready"; the switches are shown only to the admin until fish opens to players, to a group Pierre picks in the admin, FISH_PATH "Both subjects in one app"). The subject switch ("Switching" above) shows the beta subject's name with a small *beta* tag; ⚙ has no subject row and nobody sees `beta.kick` or `beta.line` (Home's doors only follow the subject on screen, for the admin in Fish). The Tools hub's subject tile and the emoji on the links to Tools that were the admin's switch and way back before the subject switch are gone. A player who is not the admin (no admin token, or ⚙ View: User) is never in a subject that opens to a group unless in its group (next item): `subjOpen(S)` = not `subjGroup(S)` or `IS_ADMIN` or in its group; at start-up a saved beta subject opens the corals (`subjAt`, fish.json not even fetched), `setSubject` and the sheet hand-off in `route` never pick it; the saved choice (`reefid-sync-subject`) and the fish progress stay as they are (still synced and merged through `inSubject`), back on screen if the admin view comes back. Fish has addresses of its own (`#fish/learn/families`, `#fish/practice/quiz`: URL refonte, "Pages and routing"), so a link opens it for the players who may. `tests/fish.smoke.js` checks both sides.
- **Opening** (`SUBJECTS` `open`, `core.js` `subjGroup`; 2026-11-05, ahead of the public fish release): the gate is its own field, apart from the `beta` label. `open: 'group'`: the admin and the subject's beta group (next item); no field or `open: 'all'` (the corals; **fish since 2026-11-06**, Pierre, Q158: "open fish to everyone now", its *beta* tag kept, sheet v67): every player, signed out included. `subjGroup(S)` (`S.open === 'group'`) is what `app.js` `subjOpen`, `sync.js` `groupsSeen`, `admin.js` `groupOn` (the Beta column, row toggle and Add all), `admin-demo.js` and `Code.gs` `groupIds_` (v53) read; `beta` only puts the *beta* tag on the switch buttons and Home's `kick` / `line`. Opening fish to everyone is the one field `open: 'all'`, the *beta* tag kept or not on its own; the sheet then stops reporting that group and refuses to change it, its rows kept in the Groups tab (read, never deleted), so a return to `'group'` finds the same members. `tests/subjects.test.js`, `tests/sync.test.js` (the v48 and v53 groups tests, on fish set back to `'group'`), `tests/adopt.test.js` (the app's group replies, the same), `tests/fish.smoke.js` (6c: fish as it is, open to all: a signed-out non-admin switches to Fish, plays, reloads, signs in in no group, switches back; 6 and 6b: `core.js` served with fish back to `open: 'group'` for the group's own checks), `tests/admin.smoke.js` (the group page on fish set back to `'group'` at runtime; no Beta column or group line when `'all'`).
- **The beta group** (sheet v48; Pierre, 2026-11-04: "fish opens to a group I pick in the admin, for now everyone; not one site-wide switch"): a subject with `open: 'group'` opens to the admin **and to the players of its group**, named by the subject's id: `subjOpen(S)` = not `subjGroup(S)`, or `IS_ADMIN`, or `subjGroups()` holds `S.id`. The sheet keeps the members in a **Groups** tab (Group, Name, Added; `Code.gs` `groupSet_`, `groupMap_`), says in every reply to a signed-in load or save which groups the player is in (`groups: [ids]`, `[]` for none), and the app keeps that list on the device under the account's name (`app.js` `GROUPS_KEY` `reefid-groups` `{n, g}`; `sync.js` `groupsSeen`, also at sign-in before the reload; cleared at sign-out; another account on the device is in no group). An older script sends no `groups`: nothing changes, the subject stays the admin's only (rule D4, the field is the gate). A change shows at once: the Tools tile appears (`proto/lab.js`, for anyone with the beta subject open; its "Admin and its group only." line for the admin only), and a player taken out while on that subject reloads on the corals (`subjAt`), the saved choice and the fish progress kept. Signed out, nobody is in a group. The admin: on the **Players** page of the subject in beta (the admin's subject switch, `#fish/players/list`), a **Beta** column (In / –), "In the Fish beta · Remove" / "Not in the Fish beta · Add" in the row's bar (one click, no confirmation), and above the table "Fish beta: N of M players · Add all K" (confirmed: a bulk action); with a script before v48 a note says what it needs (`admin.js` `groupOn`, `GROUP_SHEET`). Players who sign up later are not in the group until added. `tests/sync.test.js` (the tab, the replies, rename / merge / delete carrying the memberships), `tests/fish.smoke.js` (6b, the app with a sheet stub), `tests/admin.smoke.js` (the Players page).
- **The subject's cards: the fish beta invitation** (`progress.js` `subjHello`, `helloMode`, `helloDue`; the words in `UNITS[id].hello`, one card per way the subject opens, `group` or `all`; Pierre, 2026-11-05: "a proper card the player closes, shown once, at a calm moment", not a toast; 2026-11-06: fish stays open to a group he picks, he alone opens it to everyone, and meanwhile the card is an invitation to test the fish beta, for the players of the group only): `hello.group` is a `cardShow` card (kind `fish`, kicker "Invitation", title "Test the fish beta", buttons "Try fish", which switches to the subject and opens its quiz, and "Later"; Escape is Later) shown only while the subject opens to a group (`subjGroup`) and only to a player in it (`subjGroups()`: not the admin's own access, `IS_ADMIN`); `hello.all` (Pierre, 2026-11-06, Q158: fish opened to every player, the beta label kept): the same invitation ("Try fish, now in beta: …"), to whoever `subjOpen` lets in, only while the subject keeps its `beta` label (`subjHello` `card`); its own seen mark `reefid-fish-hello-all`. Each card once per device under its own mark (`reefid-<dev>hello-<mode>`: `reefid-fish-hello-group`, `reefid-fish-hello-all`), so the invitation never hides a later card; never in the admin's browser (`HAS_ADMIN_TOKEN`), only to a player with at least `HELLO.MIN` (10) coral answers; a player who has played the subject already (on screen, its welcome question answered, or any progress in it) is marked seen without a card (`helloDue`, pure, unit-tested in `tests/hello.test.js`). The calm moment: Home, `HELLO.WAIT` (1.5 s) after it is drawn (`route` and start-up `SETUP 2` call it), still on Home, with no tour, card, toast, ? help, ⚙ or enlarged photo on screen and the prompt spacing met (`quiz.js` `promptOk`; it then pushes the other prompts back, `promptMark`); otherwise it waits for the next Home. Leaving the page closes it unseen. `tests/fish.smoke.js` (11) checks it, `open: 'all'` included (no card).
- **Fish players get** (level 1): the photo quiz on the IMR entries (one photo per question, from one observation), the first-meeting cards and progressive opening, adaptive photos and choices (shape groups, then IMR look-alikes; confusion scores from the player's own answers), "Again" and look-alike duels slipped into the quiz (IMR look-alike pairs), progress per entry with the levels and the silent mastery exam, typed questions accepting the entry's names, the comeback after a break; **every circle on the path and in the progress** (Pierre, 2026-11-06, Q175 and Q177: no ⚙ row; since 2026-11-07 circle 3 by the player's choice, Q204, below): `UNITS.fish` `slate` = the families of circles 1, 2 and 3, `core` = circle 1 (what Open all opens, `model.js` `introLeft`), `gate` = `fishGate` (`model.js` `mayOpen`): circle 2 may open once every circle-1 family has been asked, circle 3 once the progress on circle 1 reaches `FISH_C3` = 50% and the player said yes to its card or in My progress (`UNITS.fish` `opens`, Q204, #703), then the usual weighted opening (`openPick`, IMR-tagged first for "I'm at IMR"); once every unit in play is met and asked and none may open now, a calm card at the break at Next says so and what comes next, once per reason (`quiz.js` `allMetDue`, `allMetNone`, `allMetArm`, `allMetShow`; `UNITS` `done`, absent for the corals; `app.js` `fishNextOf`: `c3` circle 3 not reached, `beyond` circle 3 declined once its offer will not come back (none after a first "Not now", Pierre, 2026-11-12, #851), `all` the families still without a game photo and the Scientific names state, else the exams; seen on the device under `reefid-fish-allmet-<k>` (cleared at sign-out and account deletion with the account's keys, `sync.js` `signOutClear`, rule D7; Codex review #851) and on the account as the TOLD token `allmet:<subject>-<k>`; Pierre, 2026-11-12; FISH_PATH.md "My progress (fish)"); **winks** for every fish player, circle 1 included (Pierre, 2026-11-03: "ça sera rigolo"): a rare, late surprise card (`quiz.js` `WINK`, `winkDue`, `winkShow`: the whale shark from 300 fish answers of the player's record, the thresher sharks from 650, each once per player across devices; Pierre, 2026-11-06; docs/LEARNING.md "Winks") with fish.json's absurd choices and lines, its photo Pierre's ★ Cover of the unit while in play (`coverP`, Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q290: the Thresher sharks' card), else one of the wink's 2-3 chosen ones (`quiz.js` `winkPhoto`, fish.json `wink.photos` from `tools/fish_wink_photos.json`; Pierre, 2026-11-07: never a bad photo), never counted; Learn = the fish families page (`learn.js` `viewFishList`: by the groups of the identification key in force, in its order, only those holding an entry, the key credited in full under the list; cues, look-alike lines; each photo enlarges in the shared viewer, `zoom`); My progress (photo wall, levels, review, mix-ups; no leaderboard rank or achievements). Not for fish yet (the family sheets, genus pages and species sheets are built, "Fish sheets" below), Look-alikes, the Duel and Memory tabs (their hashes open the fish page of the section: `subjPage`), Identify (the Coral ID Key stays, whatever the subject), the leaderboard and achievements (corals), timed mode and size estimation (video ID, EventMeasure) by design.

- **Fish scientific genera** (branch B's first step, docs/FISH_PATH.md "Levels"; 2026-11-04 night, open for Pierre): `app.js` `fishGenera` adds one unit per genus of the circle-1 species of each IMR entry (key `g:<genus>`, `rank: 'genus'`, `one: 'genus'`, `parent` its entry, its entry's group as `forms`, its sibling genera as `look`, the IMR sub-entry's cues when one bears its name, its species and the entry's photos of them) to `FISH.gen`, and `FISH.sci` lists them; the admin's `fishGen` is unchanged. They are an opt-in set of the fish (`sci`, `SUBJECTS` fish `optin`): offered once an entry is at Advanced (Open Water from 2026-11-07, Q205, #702, to 2026-11-08), ⚙ Scientific names; `units()` is the genera of the entries at Advanced plus those already met, `auto()` (played whatever the set's state: `bonusList`) an IMR player's IMR genera (`imrg`: the IMR sub-entry names that genus alone) from their entry's Open Water plus those already met (`app.js` `sciOpen`; `model.js` `imrSaid`: ever answered imr, Q227), `all()` every genus (`setOf`); `units()` from the entries at Open Water instead when the player turned it on by hand before any entry was at Advanced (`Q.sciH`, set by `app.js` `bonusSet` from ⚙ or My progress, synced with the choice; Q240); `rv` the set's rules version, recorded with each choice as `Q.sciV` (synced with it, `sync.js` `mergeSubject`), and `back` the one-time card to a player who turned it on under earlier rules (`quiz.js` `bonusBackShow`). `app.js` `rankOf(n)` ('' for corals and entries): `model.js` `distractors` keeps a question's choices in the rank of the unit asked (for a ranked unit, any unit of its rank may be a wrong choice: `rankUnits`, every genus of the set, met ones first), `quiz.js` `typeMatch(text, rk)` reads a typed answer among that rank's names (Chromis: the genus on a genus question, the Damselfish entry otherwise), the prompt and the typed form use the unit's `one`; genera are never duel pairs (`UNITS.fish.pairs`), never on the families page (`page: false`), never in the families' mean (`progPct`); they count in the fish global score (half a genus's bar is its species' scientific credit: `core.js` `treeScore`, below).
- **Fish global score** (Pierre, 2026-11-04 the formula, 2026-11-08 Q242 the commonness weights and the square-root display; docs/FISH_PATH.md "My progress (fish)", docs/LEARNING.md "Fish: the global score, built"): `core.js` `treeScore(P, T, K)`, one computation over the subject's tree (`core.js` `fishScoreTree`, built with `FISH`, `UNITS.fish.tree()`: the families with their weight and circle, the species packed by genus while they have no unit of their own, `null` common names for a species with none, Q247) with the subject's constants (`SUBJECTS` fish `score`, the one switch): the % shown (`pct`), `raw`, the components (`entries`, `genera`, `common`, `scientific`, `S`, `credit`), the same per circle (`by`) and every unit's own bar (`units`). Species weights `core.js` `rankWeights` (1/√ of the rank by observers near Dauin, `present.dauinU`, ties sharing their mean rank). Shown, and the tree sent, from a sheet reply with v ≥ `core.js` `SCORE_SHEET` (101; `model.js` `scoreOn`, rule D4), the families' mean before. `model.js` `headScore`/`headPct` give the headline (header pill, My progress ring and its three bars `progress.js` `scoreBars`, the fish % achievements, the update card, the leaderboard via the tree sent to the sheet); `progPct` stays the plain mean of a list's bars. The circle-3 threshold reads its circle-1 Entries component (`app.js` `FISH_C3`, `fishC3`).
- **Fish sheets** (docs/reviews/2026-11-05-fish-sheets-study.md; Pierre's answers of 2026-11-06, option B): `learn.js` `viewFishSheet`, read from `fish.json` alone (`core.js` `fishTree`: families, genera, species by id; the families in the key's order; `go(id)`, the sheet an id opens), so they stand whatever the game's units are. **One family sheet per family of the identification key** (`fish.json` `families`, `#fish/learn/family/<id>`), reached from the families page (each entry's name opens the family it was renamed into; its photo enlarges) and from My progress: the English name, the scientific name or names and IMR's name below, its group in the key (F1); my progress on it once the game asks the families (`myProg`, F2); the hero photo, the easiest, most reference-like photo of its species whoever took it (`easyRank` over every game and sheet-only photo of its species, F3); the IMR cues verbatim with their slides (F4); "Don't confuse with" (`.lrow`, the corals' look-alike cards): IMR's look-alike lines with their source, then the families players mix it up with (`dataLooks`), the mix-up count (F5); **the genera as rows** (F6: up to 4 photos, one per species most common first, the species count and how many are "opened for me", the IMR entry name), each opening its **short genus page** (`fishGenus`, `#fish/learn/genus/<name>`, study §4.2: the genus, its family and IMR entry name, my progress once met, the IMR sub-entry's cues, the species WoRMS now puts in another genus, every species with the filter, and the sibling genera IMR gives cues for; the page bar's previous / next go through its family's genus pages); a family with one genus lists its species there, every species with the **"Opened for me" filter, on each time a sheet opens** (`fishTiles`, a `.seg`, `fishMine`; F7: until the game asks species, a species counts as opened once its family is at Advanced or its scientific genus is met, `spOpen`); the juveniles and phases (IMR's phase lines, the sourced juvenile note, the juvenile-tagged photos, F8); report (F10). The page bar's previous / next go through the families the families page shows (`fishShown`: circle 1, the opt-in circles on, both families of an entry renamed into two). A species without a photo shows the one "No photo yet" drawing (`NOFISH`, `.nofish`). **Species sheets** (`fishSpecies`, `#fish/learn/species/<name>`, study §5.1, the automatic part): opened from a species tile (the whole tile is the link) or a single-species genus row; the scientific name first (the PDF name), its common name, "now … in WoRMS" when WoRMS renamed it; its family and genus, "★ IMR most sighted #n", "On the IMR reference list" and "Confirmed near Dauin" or "Not confirmed near Dauin" (`present.near`, rule C8's words); its rank in its family by the commonness index (`cmi`); its photos, the first large, then two more, the juvenile-tagged ones apart (up to 2), the rest behind "N more photos", each with its credit (`easyRank`, the sheet-only photos included); the other species of its genus; the iNaturalist, WoRMS and FishBase links; previous / next through its genus's species. No photo: the drawing and those links. A genus with one species opens its species' sheet and the only genus of a family its family's (`fishTree` go). Not yet: key marks, size and depth, phase sections, look-alike cues (the writing, the facts tool, the phase model: study §8). **Family ID marks and behaviour, habitat, size lines** (study F4b, F9; Pierre's Q7, Q11; 2026-11-06 16:54: published at once, he corrects them in place): `tools/fish_sheets.json` (`families` {id: {marks, life, apart {family id: a line}, src [{t, u}]}}), our own words from the sources each family lists (the FishBase family summaries for circle 1) and faithful to the IMR cues shown beside them, built into `fish.json` families by `tools/build_fish.py` `add_families` (`marks`, `life`, `apart`, `msrc`; `--regroup` applies an edit offline); the sheet shows them under the IMR tips with their sources and a "Report or correct a line" link (`learn.js` `fishMarks`), and the `apart` lines on the look-alike cards for the pairs IMR gives no words for. **A family IMR gives no cue for** (circles 2-3, the Soapfishes; Pierre, 2026-11-08, "court terme, assez urgent"; "On publie") has 1 to 3 ID marks of its own, what to look at on a photo, each checked against two sources at least (its `src`: FishBase, Fishes of Australia, Reef Life Survey, iNaturalist, the Australian Museum), our own words; `app.js` `fishGenera` makes them the family unit's tips (`qt`) under a label naming those sources (`tl`, "ID marks (FishBase, Fishes of Australia)", never IMR's), so the feedback, the first-meeting card, the families page and the cards show them where they show IMR's cues (`tests/fishsheets.test.js`). Where a source differs from IMR, a point goes to `points.json` (rule C5: the angelfishes' spine). **Size and depth** (Pierre, Q6): `tools/fish_facts.py` reads each species' Reef Life Survey page ("Max Size", "Depth"; generic User-Agent, one request every 1.2 s, cached in `tools/.cache/rls/`), then fills the gaps from the FishBase snapshot (`species.parquet`: Length and its type, the depth range; never its web pages), into `tools/fish_facts.json`; `add_families` builds them into `fish.json` species (`size` {len, lt, src}, `depth` {m, src}, each `src` the page the value comes from). The species sheet shows "Up to 10 cm · 1-36 m deep" with the source (`spFacts`; a length type other than total length in words), the family sheet the range of its species' largest sizes (`famSizes`); nothing is shown for a species neither source gives. **Genus common names** (Pierre, 2026-11-07: "some genera have common names. They should be present somewhere"; he corrects them in place): `tools/fish_genus_names.json` (`genera` {id: {common, from, word}}), only where a widely used one exists: `from: imr`, the IMR sub-entry naming that genus alone (Anemonefish, Bannerfish, Unicornfish; not Surgeonfish, three genera), or `from: species`, inferred from its species' common names (every one of 2 or more ends in `word`, no species of another genus of its family does), never the genus name itself (Chromis, Dascyllus); `add_families` checks each and builds it into `fish.json` genera (`common`, `csrc`: its source in words). Shown under the genus page's scientific name (the source under More details), on the family sheet's genus rows and on the species sheet's genus chip; the game's typed answers do not read it. `tests/fishsheets.test.js`, `tests/fish.smoke.js`.

## Names, storage keys and caches

<!-- rename-app: keep-begin -->
**The app's name** (Pierre, 2026-11-03: IMR Coral ID became **IMR Reef ID**, corals now, fish and benthos next; the address and the repository `coral-id-imr` change later, ROADMAP "Domain and repository rename"). The organisation and app names are in one place, `core.js` `ORG` (`short` IMR, `name` Institute for Marine Research, `app` IMR Reef ID, `appShort` Reef ID, the home-screen label): pages built by JS read it (`SHEET_NAME`, the progress file message, the sign-in button's tooltip); the static files that carry the name (`index.html` title, social tags and header; `admin.html` "IMR Reef ID Admin"; `privacy.html`; `manifest.webmanifest` name / short_name; `tools/og/og.html`, then `node tools/og/make_og.js` for `og.jpg`; `README.md` and the docs) are written from it by **`tools/rename_app.py`**. Names of things outside the repo that kept their old name: the Google Sheet "IMR Coral ID – RA progress" and the Cloud project "IMR Coral ID sync".

**`tools/rename_app.py`** applies every mechanical part of the rename (visible name, storage keys, shell cache name, CSP hashes of the inline scripts, Prettier on what it changed), idempotently: after a rebase on branches that still use the old names, run `python3 tools/rename_app.py` and commit the result on its own (`--check` changes nothing and fails if something is left). Lines marked `rename-app: keep` (or blocks between `rename-app: keep-begin` / `keep-end`) are left alone: the code that names the old keys on purpose (below). A fork for another organisation edits `ORG` and runs it with `--from "IMR Reef ID"`.

**Storage keys** (localStorage, sessionStorage): one app-wide prefix, `reefid-`, that names the app without the organisation (a fork keeps the players' keys; `reef-` alone could clash with another app on the shared origin `ppericard.github.io`). `core.js` `KEYS`:
- `reefid-sync-<name>` (`KEYS.SYNC`) **travels with the player**: `progress.js` `progData()` sends every such key to the sheet (Data column) and to progress files, and `loadData()` takes them back. What belongs to one group of organisms carries the group: `reefid-sync-coral-progress` now, `reefid-sync-fish-progress` beside it later.
- `reefid-<name>` (`KEYS.APP`) **stays on the device**: the default, so nothing is sent by mistake.
- Per subject (see "Subjects"): `core.js` `subjKey` (`reefid-sync-coral-progress`, `reefid-sync-fish-progress`, `-quiz`, `-intro`, `-intro-offer`, `-reset`, `reefid-sync-fish-wink`) and `subjDev` (`reefid-hold` and `reefid-comeback` for corals as before, `reefid-fish-hold`, `reefid-fish-comeback`); the choice of subject `reefid-sync-subject`.

| Old name (before 2026-11-03) | New name | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| `imr-coral-progress` | `reefid-sync-coral-progress` | Per-genus progress `P` (see "Learning model"). |
| `imr-coral-quiz` | `reefid-sync-coral-quiz` | Quiz settings `Q` (bonus genera `bon`, ...). |
| `imr-coral-intro`, `imr-coral-intro-offer` | `reefid-sync-coral-intro`, `reefid-sync-coral-intro-offer` | First-meeting cards seen, Open all and the back card's choice (`model.js` `INTRO`). |
| `imr-coral-reset` | `reefid-sync-coral-reset` | Time of the last progress reset (the sheet's opening hold counts from it). |
| `imr-coral-custom` | `reefid-sync-coral-custom` | The genera picked for the custom quiz (`quiz.js` `quizCustom`). |
| `imr-coral-rv` | `reefid-sync-coral-rv` | The progress rules version this device applied (`sync.js` `RVK`, full replay); the sheet drops it under both names from a merged account. |
| `imr-coral-ach`, `imr-coral-board` | `reefid-sync-ach`, `reefid-sync-board` | Achievements, leaderboard choice. |
| (new, 2026-11-08) | `reefid-sync-told` | The one-time notices told on any device of the account (`core.js` `TOLD`, tokens; see "Progress sync", "One-time notices after the first sync"). |
| `imr-coral-theme`, `-palette`, `-cvd`, `-size` | `reefid-sync-theme`, `-palette`, `-cvd`, `-size` | ⚙ Theme, Colours, Colour-blind, photo size per page (`index.html`'s inline script reads the first three before paint). |
| `imr-coral-order`, `-gview`, `-cmpview`, `-lkswiped`, `-pview` | `reefid-sync-order`, `-gview`, `-cmpview`, `-lkswiped`, `-pview` | Genera order and view, Compare view, look-alike swipe hint seen, My progress view. |
| `imr-coral-sync` | `reefid-account` | The sign-in record (`SYNC`: name, code, base, log); never travels (it never did: it was excluded by name). |
| `imr-coral-loaded` (session) | `reefid-loaded` (session) | "Progress loaded from ..." after a reload. |
| every other `imr-<name>` | `reefid-<name>` | Device-only: `reefid-tour`, `-nudge`, `-log0`, `-recalc`, `-prompt-at`, `-pulse`, `-again-told`, `-hold`, `-comeback`, `-link`, `-photo-q`, `-photo-stats`, `-idkey-x`, `-idkey-lay`, `-idkey-pv`, `-idkey-start2`, `-lab-votes`, `-curation-last`, `-curation-draft`, `-curation-base`, `-ach-new`, `-achs-cache`, `-board-cache`, `-board-told`, `-board-sent14`, `-anon-base`, `-anon-id`, `-anon-resent`, `-anonq`, `-dev-id`, `-evq`, `-reports`, `-report-name`, `-photo-flags`, and the admin's `reefid-admin-*` (`-vault` the encrypted token, `-view`, `-session` and `-undo` in sessionStorage, `-sub`, `-cands`, `-cseed`, `-csrc2`, `-diff`, `-kcov`, `-ffilter`, `-qfilter`, `-started`, `-stats`, `-origins`, `-gview`, `-me`). The admin demo prefixes its keys with `demo:`. |

The service worker's messages to the pages follow the same names (`reefid-ver?`).

**Migration on a device** (`core.js` `LEGACY_KEYS`, one table read by both the tool and the devices): `migrateStorage()` is the first line of `app.js` and `admin.js` (after `core.js`, before anything reads a key): each old key is copied to its new name if the new one is absent (a new one already there is kept), then removed; a key that cannot be written (storage full) keeps its old name until the next start. The inline theme scripts of `index.html` and `admin.html` run before it and read both names. A progress file says `app: 'reefid'` (`'imr-coral-id'` before, still read).

**The Coral ID Key rename** (2026-11-04, alpha: no backward compatibility, rule W3): the tool's device keys (`-idkey-x`, `-idkey-lay`, `-idkey-pv`, `-idkey-start2`, the admin's `reefid-admin-kcov`) and the page or tool names stored inside `reefid-sync-size`, `reefid-lab-votes`, `reefid-admin-view` and `reefid-admin-sub` moved once from their old names (`core.js` `migrateIdKey`, run right after `migrateStorage()` by `app.js` and `admin.js`; idempotent, to delete once every device has run it; `tests/idkey.test.js`). Nothing else reads an old name: an old `#finder` link lands on the default page (no redirect). The sheet moved its logged page names itself (v38, `ONCE_`).

**The URL refonte's data move** (2026-11-05, Q53-Q56, rule W3): `core.js` `migrateRoutes`, run by the app and the admin at start-up after `migrateIdKey`, moves this device's data once from the old page names and addresses: the photo size per page (`reefid-sync-size`, keyed by page name: `genera`, `genus`, `pair`, `idkey`, `lab-gallery`, `lab-elim` become `learn.genera`, `learn.genus`, `learn.compare`, `identify.key`, `tools.gallery`, `tools.elim`; `tips` dropped), the events still queued (`reefid-evq`: each one's address, a visit summary's page names) and the photos flagged (`reefid-photo-flags`: where they were seen); idempotent. The translations (`routeRename`: an old address to its new one; `pageRename`: an old page name to its new one) sit between `routes-migration: begin/end` markers, for the sheet's own one-shot over the Pages, Events and Reports tabs (its own PR, once the app is live). Delete them with their callers once every device and the sheet have moved.

**Migration in the sheet** (sheet v33): a player's Data JSON is keyed by these names. Until every device runs the renamed app, an app from before the rename still saves (and reads) the old names. So the app reads either (`core.js` `keysIn` on every reply that carries a player's data: `syncGet`, admin `userPost`; the new name wins) and sends the new names **plus a copy under the old ones** (`withLegacy`, while `LEGACY_SEND` is true: an old app reading a record without its names would merge its progress with an empty one and drop the saved record). `Code.gs` reads either through `dataKey_` (progress for the leaderboard % and the abilities, reset for the opening hold, achievements). Turn `LEGACY_SEND` off with the domain move (ROADMAP). The salt of the code hashes and anonymous ids (`Code.gs` `SALT`, `admin.js`) stays `'imr-coral-id'`: changing it would lock every player out.

**Caches** (`sw.js`): the shell cache `reefid-shell-vN` (`imr-coral-id-vN` before; on activation every other cache with either prefix goes, `REPLACES`), the admin's files `reefid-admin-files` (2026-11-04, `ADM`, `ADM_FILE`: admin*.js, admin.css, autosort.js, analysis.js, one entry per file, a new `?v=` replacing the older; no deploy deletes it, so the first admin visit after a deploy downloads only the files that changed; code only, no player data), and the photo cache `imr-photos-v1`, not renamed: a new name would have thrown away (or copied while the app waits) up to 2,500 offline photos per phone; it gets one with the domain move, whose new origin starts with empty caches anyway. The worker asks the open pages their version under both message names (`VER_ASK`), so a page from before the rename is not reloaded mid-question.
<!-- rename-app: keep-end -->

## Data files

### `data.json` (served source of truth)
Layout (code re-review M5): written only by `tools/datajson.py` `dump()`: one top-level key per line, and one genus per line inside `gen`, `course`, `cand`, `x` and `x.gen` (compact JSON within a line, key order kept, trailing newline), so edits to different genera merge cleanly. `python3 tools/datajson.py` rewrites the file in that layout; `--check` (PR check) fails when it is not. The app only parses it, so the layout does not matter to it.
- `gen{Genus}`: `n` slate number, `fam`, `ig` IMR lecture group, `imr` lecture cues (verbatim), `qt` short ID tips, `sum` short description, `plain` the plain sentence (also on every `x.gen` record; below), `mn` mnemonic, `crit` Coral Finder criteria, `tr` trait ids, `trs` trait ids found in some species only (Pierre, 2026-11-04: e.g. paliform lobes in some Favites; every tool reads `tr` and `trs` through `proto/lab.js` `trHas` (1 / 0.5 some species / 0), `trAll`, `trLabel`, `trFits`, never by genus name: half a match in the Coral ID Key, never eliminated on in the Elimination board, an "In some species" row in the Feature gallery, a ring in the Feature matrix; `tools/apply_some_species.py`, `tests/traits.test.js`), `look` look-alikes + `lsrc` source per pair, `pages` CF pages, `sl`/`rsl` slide numbers, `info` dated "Other sources" notes, `check` points to check with a trainer, `levels` (2 or 3 views), `views{wide,medium,macro}` photo lists, `more` extra photos, `cover`, `cm` how common the genus is near Dauin (a small integer, the geometric mean of its iNaturalist observations and observers within 40 km; written by `tools/commonness.py`, also on the `x.gen` records of Millepora and the bonus genera; used by the opening of genera, docs/LEARNING.md "Common first").
- **The plain sentence** (Pierre, 2026-11-03: sheets clear for everyone, complete beginners included, divers who never had the IMR coral lecture; the scientific IMR text stays for the RAs): `plain` on each genus of `gen` and `x.gen`, 1-2 sentences of at most 30 words in everyday words (shape, texture, size, colour caveats, a comparison), shown in body text with no label right under the photo, before the ID tips and lecture notes: first in the text column of the genus sheet (`learn.js` `viewGenus`, `.idbox`; beside the photo on tablet and desktop) and under the name on the first-meeting card (`quiz.js` `introShow`), by `app.js` `plainLine()` (`.plainw`). It was first a muted "In plain words" line above the photo; Pierre's review the same day: people look at the photo first, then at what is under it, and skipped it. The lines are a synthesis in the app's own words (no sentence copied) of how field guides and references describe the genus underwater, each line checked against at least 2 of them (`tools/plain_sources.json`: Corals of the World factsheets, Reef Corals of the Indo-Malayan Seas, the ReefCloud hard coral genera guide, Wikipedia and a few others), plus our IMR lecture notes and ID tips and the genus's own photos; where a source differed from the IMR notes, the line followed the IMR notes, except four IMR wordings Pierre had corrected after the sources (2026-11-03, `tools/apply_imr_corrections.py` for the lecture notes, mnemonic, `sum` and the old "to check" note, `tools/id_tips.txt` for the ID tips; reasons and quotes in the script and in `plain_sources.json` `_corrections`): Seriatopora corallites "in neat rows", no longer "black", and its mnemonic "Serius Black" replaced by the name itself (Latin *seriatus*, in rows: "Seria-topora": pores in series); Lobactis tentacular lobes "often green, blue or white", not "pale" (Corals of the World); Heliofungia tentacle tips "white or pink"; Herpolitha "free-living like a mushroom coral, but many mouths along the furrow" (the IMR "solitary" and the Coral Finder's "colonial" both hold: one disc, many mouths; Hoeksema 1989 says monostomatous / polystomatous instead), its "to check" note now an "Other sources" note; the glossary's Solitary says the same. Written by `tools/apply_plain_sheets.py`. `tests/plain.test.js`: every genus has one, within the limit; no 5-word run taken from the Coral Finder features; 2+ sources each; a slate genus's line names another genus only when its card comes after that genus's (`starRefs`). `tests/pages.smoke.js` checks that a sheet and the first cards show it after the photo, before the tips, with no label.
- Photo object: `f` file stem in `img/`, `ar` width/height (quiz layout; `r: 1` means remote, see below), `a` attribution, `lic`, `o` original URL, `t` taxon, `own` (Pierre's), `loc` (dauin/ph/world), `src` remote URL when not downloaded (`r: 1`), `gid: 1` when the iNat observation is not Research Grade (candidates; bonus genera, kept out of games), `skel: 1` a coral skeleton photo (rule P6: never shown; `tools/apply_skeletons.py` from `judging/skeletons.json`; `core.js` `applyCuration` drops it from every list, as an excluded photo).
- `cand` = only the candidates `curation.json` names (the ones Pierre added, moved or sent to another genus: the app needs them to apply the curation). The candidates still waiting live in **`cand.json`** (same layout, top key `cand`; Pierre, 2026-11-01: a big backlog must not weigh on every player's download): only the admin loads it (and the app's `?draft` preview). Tools read both with `datajson.load_all()` and write back with `dump_all()`, which re-splits by what `curation.json` names; One-off backlog runs: `python3 tools/fetch_local.py --dauin 30 --ph 20 --ph-all` (raised caps, Philippines for every genus; first run 2026-11-01: 544 → 1825 photos waiting, 1321 observations; Caulastraea, Acanthastrea, Leptastrea, Stylocoeniella and Zoopilus still have none). `python3 tools/fetch_local.py --tidy` brings the lists in line with `curation.json` offline ("Photos" below), `python3 tools/datajson.py --split` only re-splits, `--check` checks both layouts (CI).
- `key` Coral Finder front-page key (growth form → boxes), `imrg` the 11 IMR lecture groups, `traits` corallite clues with sources, `terms` glossary `[term, definition, source, plain]` (`plain`, optional since 2026-11-03: a first sentence in plain words written for the app, shown before the sourced definition, which keeps its source; `tools/apply_plain_terms.py`, which also added Polyp, and on 2026-11-03 `MORE`: the 9 most frequent words of the genus sheets' scientific lines that had no entry, Free-living, Solitary, Separate walls (also matches "plocoid"), Shared walls ("cerioid"), Phaceloid, Radial corallites, Tentacular lobes, Axial furrow, Spinules, with the Corals of the World glossary definition), `tips` the lecture's observation questions, `course` lecture photos, `cmp` lecture comparison images, `cand{Genus}` candidate photos for the admin, `spell` other spellings of a name (name → `[spelling, source]`, e.g. Diploastreidae → Diploastraeidae, WoRMS; Pierre, 2026-11-04: both shown, the IMR one first, by `app.js` `altName` wherever a family is shown; never a per-name branch in code), `x` extra Dauin genera for the Coral ID Key and the bonus genera (built by `tools/build_extra.py`; `tools/apply_bonus.py` re-checks Research Grade and moves multi-coral photos of the bonus genera to `more`).

### `curation.json` (Pierre-owned; never overwrite in a PR)
`genera{Genus}`: `cover`, `feat{view:[f]}`, `hide`, `move`, `add{f:view}`, `out` (not this genus), `ok` (validated), `lv` (2/3 levels); top level: `mont{"A|B":{v,a,b,hide}}` Look-alikes overrides (`v` may be `all`), `quality{f:1-3}`, `gf` growth forms, `exclude{f:reason}`, `flag`, `seen` (dismissed suggestions, reviewed candidates `cand:f`, players' reports marked Done `rep:id` (Stand-by and Dismissed are `reps` below), photos out of the presence rule Pierre kept as exceptions `rule:f` and share-alike photos he kept `sa:f`, To do's Kept), `use{f:'ref'|'game'}` (sheet only / games only; no admin button: Pierre had the Both / Sheet / Game segment of PR #303 removed, 2026-11-01; the field still works if set), `single{f:1}` (photo wrongly marked "several corals"), `crop{f:[l,t,r,b]}` (fractions of the original photo), `rot{f:90|180|270}` (Pierre's rotation, degrees clockwise, applied after the crop: "Photo rotation" below), `tile{form letter:[f]}` (lead photos of the growth-form tiles on the Coral ID Key start, at most 3, 📌 in admin > Growth form), `auto{day:{f:code}}` (the auto-sort's log: what it added, rejected or excluded, the Checks waiting for Pierre, the decisions he undid; code grammar at the top of `autosort.js`, e.g. `aw2B`, `r`, `?aw3T`, `-r`, `?aw3T@t5a1kq` (answered at that time); Candidates "Auto-sort" below), `retest{f:{q:[first, again], fm:[first, again]}}` (Pierre's blind second ratings; his first rating stays in `quality` / `gf`). `fish{add, seen, exclude, out, tags, ease, phase, crop, rot, auto}`: the fish curation ("Fish candidates" below; `out` is Pierre's Not this only). `points{id:{s, n, a{photo key: text}, t}}`: Pierre's state of the points to handle ("Points to handle" below): `s` `wait` (stand-by), `done` (resolved) or `drop` (dismissed), absent = open; `n` his notes; `a` the trainer's answer per photo (key = the photo id, `p<i>` for an empty slot); `t` when he last set the status (ms). A new key rather than `seen`: `seen` is a set of handled flags (value 1) many views read, while a point carries four states and text. `reps{id:'wait'|'drop'}`: the players' reports Pierre set on Stand-by or dismissed (To do › Reports, Q309; "Players' reports" below); Done stays `seen` `rep:<id>`, the one the app reads to thank the player.

Written only by the admin's Publish, which merges key by key onto the latest published file (see Admin).

### Suggestion files (machine-written, Pierre confirms in admin)
- `forms.json`: `forms` (the 7 IMR growth form definitions, single source), `cf` (CF box → IMR forms), `gf` guesses, `gunsure`, legacy `form`. Written by `tools/judging_import.py` and `tools/game_forms.js` in one layout (one key per line, the big `{photo: value}` maps one entry per line; `tests/gameforms.test.js`). **Every game photo should have a growth form**, Pierre's (curation `gf`) or a guess (`tests/photos.test.js` lists the ones without as a warning, never a failure: a candidate Pierre approves without a form arrives with his Publish and must not turn unrelated PRs red): `node tools/game_forms.js` lists the game photos with none, and `--apply FILE` writes the guesses of a growth-form pass (judging/BRIEF.md format, each photo looked at) into `gf` and `gunsure`; the pass of 2026-11-05 is `judging/game-forms.json` (222 photos, mostly candidates approved after the first form pass).
- `quality.json`: automatic quality 3/2/1 from `tools/photo_quality.py`.
- `picks.json`: suggested featured photos.
- `dups.json`: near-duplicates from `tools/find_dups.py`.
- `metrics.json`: objective photo metrics from `tools/photo_metrics.py`, `m{f:[sharp, peak, px, cast]}` (sharpness as the variance of the Laplacian at 960 px, sharpness of the sharpest part, long side in px, colour cast in %), for the live photos (`img/<f>-m.webp`) and the iNat candidates (their 1024 px file, cached in `~/.cache/coral-id-imr/metrics/`, outside the repo). Admin only: tie-breakers and sanity flags of the auto-sort, evidence next to a disagreement, never a decision alone (on the 134 photos Pierre rated, size separates Poor from the rest, AUC 0.90, sharpness at 960 px hardly does, 0.4-0.6). One photo per line. Re-run after a candidate pass (`--cand`) or a big photo change.
- `suggest.json`: imported results of the outside judging agent (`tools/judging_import.py`), its fish candidate pass (`tools/judging_fish.py`, `"fish"`: "Fish candidates" below), and its candidate pass (`tools/judging_cand.py`, `"cand"`: per candidate photo its view, quality, growth form, `best`/`alt`/`dup` within its observation and view, or another genus, and `c`, a suggested crop `[l, t, r, b]` judged at full size and halved toward Pierre's own crops by the import, `CROP_K`). Admin only. `admin.js` applies the view and the dashed quality and form guesses, orders the swiper with `jw` (best ×1, alt ×0.3, dup ×0.04; Good ×1.3, Poor ×0.15; several corals ×0.3; another genus ×0.02; not judged ×1), shows the judgment under the photo, and counts the candidates not judged yet (reminder: the pass runs on demand). The pre-sort sets aside (`aside`, the same rule as `tools/excluded.py` `set_aside`, so the candidate search does not count them as waiting) a candidate judged `exclude` or `cmp`, `dup` (of another candidate or of a live photo of its observation: `judging_cand.py sheets` puts those on the observation's sheet, marked "live"), several corals or Poor, with no `unsure` (rules learnt from 85 photos Pierre rejected, judging/BRIEF.md); a count and Show keep them reachable. Since the auto-sort (Candidates, below) those sure rejects are rejected or excluded in the draft (logged as auto, undoable); what stays set aside without a decision is a sure comparison image, and every sure reject while the keep field is off; a Check, an undone auto decision or a judgment the metrics contradict is never set aside. A suggested crop shows on the candidate (`sugCrop`, `crSrc`), opens in the crop editor, and is written to `curation.json` `crop` when Pierre adds the photo (`ACT.addc`); Full photo over it keeps an explicit `[0, 0, 1, 1]`. `tools/judging_cand.py detail <f>` builds the full-size sheet (whole frame with a 10% grid, three 100% crops) the pass judges quality and crop from.

Precedence: `curation.json` > suggestion files > `data.json` defaults.

### Fish content (`fish.json`, 2026-11-03; read by the app since the fish subject, see "Subjects")
Content of the fish subject on the same engine (decided with Pierre, 2026-11-03). The app reads it through `core.js` `fishGen` (level 1 so far: the teaching entries; see "Subjects (the taxon model)"); its photos, until the fish photo pipeline exists, come from `fish-photos.json` (below).
- **Files**: `tools/fish_source.json` (hand-maintained: the regional identification keys and the one in force here (`keys`, `deploy`), the teaching nodes with their reference mapping, circle, cues, look-alikes and phases from the IMR fish presentation, the species list in PDF order with the cheatsheet rank, the circle-3 sources (`sources`) and the retired ids (`retired`); its `_doc` key explains every field), `tools/build_fish.py` (the build, documented at its top), `fish.json` (its output), `tests/fish.test.js` (structure checks). Only derived lists are in the repo, never the PDF or the slides.
- **Two trees.** `ref` = the reference tree from WoRMS, keyed by AphiaID: `{name (accepted), rank (as WoRMS gives it: no fixed ranks, unranked clades kept, missing ranks skipped), parent, syn (WoRMS synonyms, accepted when typed), inat (iNaturalist taxon id), iname (iNaturalist's name when it differs)}`, from the WoRMS class down to the species. `teach` = the teaching tree, what the game asks: the 27 entries of the IMR presentation (level 1, circle 1) in its order, then the other circles; level 2 = species. Each node: `id` (stable, never the name), `name` (as IMR names it), `fam` (as IMR writes it; `wfam` = the WoRMS families when they differ, e.g. Groupers: Epinephelidae), `group` (its group in the key in force), `level`, `circle`, `src` (imr / pdf / diver / wink), `wink`, `note`, `why` (circle 3: why divers want it), `ref` / `not` (AphiaIDs it covers / leaves out), `species` (ids of the species in the game, in commonness order since 2026-11-04: the most commonly met around Dauin first, below), `sl`, `cues`, `look` ([teaching id, how to tell it apart]), `phase`, `sub` (sub-entries IMR names inside an entry: Chromis / Dascyllus / Pomacentrus / Anemonefish, Bannerfish, Surgeonfish / Unicornfish). A species belongs to the node whose `ref` holds its nearest ancestor (most specific wins), its genus being the genus of its PDF name (Chromis agilis stays under Chromis though WoRMS has it in Pycnochromis).
