- **The game's units: families, genera, species** (Pierre, 2026-11-06 13:04: "the game's units are not the IMR entries"; 13:38: the IMR course is a tag; [plan](reviews/2026-11-06-fish-units-plan.md)). `tools/build_fish.py` `add_families` writes `families` (the identification key's family labels inside its 20 groups, the book's English name, `sci` the scientific names they cover, each game species placed by walking up `ref` to its first label: *Amphiprion* in the Damselfishes, *Diploprion* in the Soapfishes; 28 in circle 1; ids `f:` + the label's letters, `f:MoorishIdol`) and `genera` (`g:<Genus>`, the PDF name's first word, with its `family`), and gives each game species its `family` and `genus`. **The IMR tag**: `imr`, IMR's name, on the family that carries an IMR entry (with its slides `sl`, cues, look-alike lines as family ids, phase lines, juveniles) and on the genera an IMR sub-entry names (Anemonefish = *Amphiprion*, Bannerfish, Unicornfish, Surgeonfish on three genera; Chromis, Dascyllus, Pomacentrus); `imrWith` on a family whose species IMR teaches under another (the Soapfishes: Groupers). **Declared look-alikes beyond IMR**: `tools/fish_source.json` `look` ({src, key, groups of family ids, note}; Pierre, 2026-11-06, from experience: Butterflyfishes–Angelfishes, Snappers–Coral Breams–Emperors, Parrotfishes–Wrasses (some wrasse species only), Soldierfishes–Squirrelfishes–Cardinalfishes, Triggerfishes–Filefishes): every pair of a group a look-alike both ways, applied while its `key` is in force; each family's `lsrc` ({family id: source}, as `data.json` `gen` `lsrc`) names IMR's slide, then the declared source (`IMR fish presentation, slide 10 and Pierre, 2026-11-06`). A family id that is not a family stops the build. They seed the traps and the confusion groups until pooled fish answers exist (rule L7). The game reads them (`core.js` `fishGen`: one unit per family, the starter core the families of `start`'s entries, typed names the book's, IMR's and the scientific ones; `app.js` `fishGenera`: the genera inside their family; `model.js` `openPick`: "I'm at IMR" opens the IMR-tagged units first). Old entry ids saved on a device or in an account (t01) move to their family at every load (`app.js` `unitsRenamed`, `sync.js`: `core.js` `renameUnits`; Groupers keeps IMR's Groupers, the Soapfishes start unmet). `teach` stays in `fish.json` for the tools (`fish_photos.py`, the judging) until they move.
- **Circles**: 1 = IMR core (the 27 entries, their species from the EventMeasure reference PDF "Common Filipino Fishes" and the slides); 2 = seen sometimes on SVS video (barracudas; trumpetfishes, shrimpfishes and sweepers since Q216, 2026-11-08); 3 = opt-in "beyond IMR", **not PDF-based** (opt-in until 2026-11-06; since Q175 every circle is on the path and counts in the progress, circles 2 and 3 opening after circle 1: "Fish players get"; since 2026-11-07 circle 3 opens only once the player says yes, asked by a card at the Q177 threshold and in My progress, counting in the % either way, Q204: `UNITS.fish` `opens`, LEARNING.md "Circles") (Pierre, 2026-11-03): the fish families divers want to recognise, muck diving above all (Pierre's list and Dauin dive guides, cited in `sources`), with every species of them confirmed near Dauin (Research Grade by at least 2 distinct observers within 40 km, or the IMR reference list; until 2026-11-04 also Pierre's own observation there: "Presence" below; until 2026-11-03, at least 2 Research Grade observations within 40 km), in the PDF or not, plus **winks** (`wink`: the whale shark and the pelagic thresher, unmistakable and fun to meet, a light bonus rather than ID training. Presentation, agreed with Pierre: a rare surprise card in the quiz, late and once per player (the whale shark from 300 fish answers, the thresher sharks from 650, Pierre 2026-11-06), one photo (one of its 2-3 most beautiful, `wink.photos`: `tools/fish_wink_photos.json`, written by `tools/build_fish.py` `wink_photos`; those Pierre's curation left in the game, else the unit's easiest; Pierre, 2026-11-07), its answer among 3 deliberately absurd choices taken from our own entries, each with a tongue-in-cheek label (`wink.choices`: [teaching id, label], e.g. "Damselfish (a well-fed one)" for the whale shark), then one friendly feedback line (`wink.right`, `wink.wrong`; the texts of both winks validated by Pierre, 2026-11-03); never counted in the progress, no effect on photo ratings). Silvery/pelagic families are not in circle 1. **A species is in its family's circle** (Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q214: "the circle holds families, so every species of the family"): its teaching node's circle, whatever its source; a species off the IMR list (`src: dauin`, `wink`) comes in only once confirmed near Dauin, as every circle-3 species (`build_fish.py` `needs_near`). Until 2026-11-08 a `dauin` species was always circle 3 (the Dauin lionfishes, boxfishes and cowfishes, batfishes and spadefishes of circle-1 entries, and the other barracudas). **Every species confirmed near Dauin of a game family is in the game** (Q214, 2026-11-08: a search of every Research Grade fish species within 40 km of Dauin, iNaturalist `species_counts` per class, names and families resolved on WoRMS, kept when its first key label is a game family and 2+ distinct observers saw it: 222 species came in, 19 of them PDF species of the new families, 442 to 664 game species, 3 of them with no common name in any reference (Q247)). **The soapfishes are one family, as in the book** (Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q248: "we'll do as in the book: one soapfish family ... with the reminder ... with the groupers for the IMR course"): `f:Soapfishes` (the key's label, Grammistidae and Diploprioninae) holds *Diploprion bifasciatum* and the Grammistidae *Grammistes sexlineatus* and *Pogonoperca punctata*, all circle 1; the Groupers entry's references include Grammistidae so the build files them (the family by its key label), and the family keeps `imrWith` Groupers, without the Groupers' cues. **Families added on 2026-11-08** (Pierre, Q216; Q217 for the dartfishes): circle 2 Trumpetfishes, Shrimpfishes, Sweepers; circle 3 Hawkfishes, Blennies, Triplefins, Convict Blennies, Dottybacks, Eel-tailed Catfishes, Bigeyes, Tunas & Mackerels, Soles, Cornetfishes, Remoras, Needlefishes, Eagle Rays, Cat Sharks, Dartfishes (the key's own label, WoRMS subfamily Ptereleotrinae of Gobiidae: shown as *Ptereleotrinae*), Gobies (the key's Gobies label, Gobiidae and Oxudercidae, without the dartfishes and the wormfishes, Microdesminae, not decided; Pierre, 2026-11-08: "the gobies are indeed in circle 3 ... we said we would put them in circle 3", replacing the `leftOut` entry of 2026-11-03 that kept them out). Each book label is its own family (Pierre, 2026-11-08: "if the book has its own label, it goes under its own label": triplefins and convict blennies apart from the blennies, dartfishes apart from the gobies).
- **Names: EventMeasure / PDF first, WoRMS as an indication** (Pierre, 2026-11-03). EventMeasure's species come from a fixed pick list, probably the PDF's, which may lag WoRMS: the name shown and expected is the PDF's (`name`); the WoRMS accepted name goes beside it (`now`, shown as "now X in WoRMS"), and typed answers accept both (and the WoRMS synonyms). Two PDF species that WoRMS treats as one (Pomacentrus armillatus, P. opisthostigma) stay two entries. Likely slips in the PDF (a spelling WoRMS does not know, two common-name typos) are kept as written and flagged in `check` for Pierre to check against EventMeasure. Only by Pierre's decision is a PDF spelling shown another way: the source row's `shown` (its reason and date in `shownWhy`) is the name shown, expected and looked up in WoRMS, and the source's `name` keeps the PDF's (`tools/build_fish.py` `shown_name`): s278, "Neoniphon argentus" in the PDF, is shown as *Neoniphon argenteus*, the spelling of FishBase, WoRMS and iNaturalist, with no "now" note (Pierre, 2026-11-14); typed, the PDF spelling reads as a slip of it (right, with the spelling step).
- **Presence: confirmed** (rule C8, Pierre 2026-11-04: "the same scientific rigour for everyone, me included"; the rule is defined once in `tools/presence.py`). **Confirmed near Dauin** (`near`, reasons in `nearBy`; `near_dauin`) = `observers`, Research Grade observations within 40 km of Dauin by at least 2 distinct observers, or `imr`, the species is in the IMR reference list (the EventMeasure PDF "Common Filipino Fishes", `pdf` pages: what IMR uses as its reference for the fish seen at Dauin, despite its name). **Confirmed in the Philippines** (`ph`, reasons in `phBy`; `ph_confirmed`) = `observers`, Research Grade observations in the country (place 6873) by at least 3 distinct observers (`phU`, asked only when `rg` >= 3 and nothing else confirms it); `checklist`, at least one Research Grade observation in the country (`rg`) and FishBase's country checklist lists it (Table 1 "present", `chk`; `tools/presence_checklists.json`, names matched through the PDF name, the WoRMS name, iNaturalist's and the WoRMS synonyms): a checklist alone never brings in a species nobody observed; `near`, confirmed near Dauin (so an IMR-list species is in the Philippines: until 2026-11-04, 31 PDF species were "confirmed near Dauin" yet out of the game as "not confirmed in the Philippines"). Pierre's own observations (`ppericard`), two halves (rule S2): **presence**: he is one observer like anyone else, his Research Grade observations count, his Needs ID ones confirm nothing on their own (until 2026-11-04 one of his species-level observations, even Needs ID, confirmed a species in the Philippines and near Dauin); **photos**: his photos identified to the species stay usable as that species even when Needs ID (he names a species only when nearly sure), `own` counting them anywhere. `present` = `{dauin` (Research Grade observations within 40 km of Dauin), `dauinNI` (the Needs ID observations there: such a species is "not confirmed near Dauin", never "never seen"), `dauinU` (their distinct Research Grade observers), `near`, `nearBy`, `rg`, `phU`, `chk`, `ph`, `phBy`, `own``}`. Not used: the number of identifiers (a well-identified rare fish often has only two) and the date (recency says nothing about the identification). **Circle 3, and every species off the IMR list,** also need `near` (Pierre, 2026-11-03; `build_fish.py` `needs_near`): such a species not confirmed near Dauin is out of the game (`out`: "not confirmed near Dauin", "circle 3: not confirmed near Dauin" until 2026-11-08, kept in `fish.json`, taken out of its teaching node's lists, and so out of the stopgap photos, `tests/fish.test.js`). `python3 tools/build_fish.py --near` recomputes `dauinU`, `near` and `nearBy` on the existing `fish.json`, and with them `ph` and `phBy` (they depend on `near`; the national observers are asked when the rule needs them), taking out of the game a species no longer confirmed (the rest of the build unchanged; a species newly confirmed comes in with a full build, which applies the whole rule). `tests/presence.test.js` checks every species' `ph` against its own fields. Run of 2026-11-04 (the rule's first build): 29 IMR-PDF species entered the game, confirmed near Dauin by the IMR list and so in the Philippines, after being out as "not confirmed in the Philippines" (Acanthurus bariene, A. grammoptilus, A. leucocheilus, Naso caesius, Siganus margaritiferus, Amphiprion percula, Chromis agilis, C. xanthochira, C. cinerascens, Neopomacentrus filamentosus, Lutjanus lemniscatus, Lethrinus amboinensis, Seriola dumerili, Pterocaesio marri, Pseudanthias squamipinnis, Epinephelus bleekeri, Scarus frenatus, Macropharyngodon ornatus, Wetmorella albofasciata, Halichoeres kneri, H. chlorocephalus, H. marginatus, Myripristis botche, Ostorhinchus angustatus, O. holotaenia, O. luteus, Cheilodipterus nigrotaeniatus, Parapercis multiplicata, Parupeneus ciliatus; most have no Research Grade observation in the country, so their photos come from abroad once fetched); none left; no species' `near` changed (none was confirmed near Dauin by Pierre's own observation alone); 7 species moved Pierre's Needs ID observations near Dauin from `dauin` to `dauinNI`. Of 467 species, 426 are confirmed by the checklist and near Dauin, 36 near Dauin only, 2 by 3+ observers only, 2 by the checklist only, 1 not confirmed (Ichthyapus vulturis: 2 Research Grade by 1 observer, not in FishBase; out). The same rule decides the coral photos from abroad (`tools/fetch_local.py`'s last tier) and the admin's "out of rule" flag (`presence.json`, "Presence of coral species" below). Run of 2026-11-03 (applied 2026-11-04, admin plan PR 1): with two observers, 30 species went from "confirmed" (one observation was enough) to "seen, not confirmed"; with the IMR list, 108 species became confirmed by it (73 of circle 1, among them those 23; 35 out of the game for other reasons); 5 circle-3 species left the game, each seen near Dauin by a single observer (Brachysomophis cirrocheilos, Ichthyapus vulturis, Echidna polyzona, Ariosoma fasciatum, Turrum coeruleopinnatum; one stopgap photo of Ariosoma fasciatum under Garden eels left with it); none entered.
- **Species**: `{id, name, now, common, ref, inat, circle | out, r (rank: the PDF cheatsheet's 24 most sighted first, then PDF order), cmi (commonness index within its entry, 0-1), present, src, sec (PDF section), pdf, cs, sl, check, photoTaxon}` (`photoTaxon`: "Fish candidates" below). A species not confirmed in the Philippines, or a PDF species whose family is in no circle, is out of the game (`out` says why, no `circle`) but stays in `fish.json`: a future Fish ID Key for EventMeasure must hold every species of the PDF, unfiltered (ROADMAP).
- **Curated common names** (Pierre, 2026-11-04: 1 to 3 per species; none for a species no reference names, which has no common-name unit: Q247, 2026-11-08; published as selected, Q313, 2026-11-08; docs/FISH_PATH.md "Common names"): `tools/fish_common.json` holds each game species' names (the PDF's first, then the main English names of FishBase and iNaturalist when found in both, at most 3, the most shared first; spellings of one name merged into `alt`), every name with its sources; its inputs, FishBase's and iNaturalist's English names per species, are in `tools/fish_common_src.json`; both written by `tools/fish_common.py` (`--inat`, `--fishbase DIR`, then offline; `--check`, run by `tests/fish.test.js`). `tools/build_fish.py` `add_families` (every build path, `--regroup` the quickest offline) writes them into `fish.json`, species `cn`: `[[name shown, other spellings…], …]`. The game reads them through `app.js` `cnList`, `cnShow` (the name a multiple-choice question shows: the least asked, rotating) and `cnTypeNames` (typed: only the listed names and their spellings, a name two species list right for each, a kind word of `vn` known: Q319), for the species level; the genus questions' "common name" nudge (`typeOther`) and the family questions' name index read them too. Tests: `tests/fishcommon.test.js`.
- **The name index** (Pierre, 2026-11-09, Q319, Q320, Q333; [the study](reviews/2026-11-09-shared-common-names.md)): `fish.json` `vn`, the kind words of the species' names shown (razorfish, toby, spinefoot), `[[word, [[family id, role, n, of, [species ids]], …]], …]` (role `k` when at least 2 of the family's game species carry the word or all of them, `w` for one species' word; never a word of a "False …" name or a family's own name), written by `tools/fish_names.py` (offline summary; `--check`, run by `tests/fish.test.js`) through `tools/build_fish.py` `add_families` (every build path, `--commonness` included). The app completes it from `fish.json` itself (`core.js` `fishNameIndex`: `sp` a listed species name, `ss` a species' scientific name, `gs` a genus's, `gc` a genus's common name, each with `sc`, the species it designates) and Pierre's exceptions (`curation.json` `fish` `names`); `fishGen` `scopes` gives the IMR genera and sub-entries their species. `fishTypeNames` reads every name as the family it lifts to, with `sc` when it names part of it only (one key of a family read by two names takes the wider); `typeFits(m, t, sp)` says whether a reading names unit t as the photo shows it (sp: the photo's species, `quiz.js` from `app.js` `photoTaxon`; unknown: read as before). A name of part of the family not designating the photo is a mistake with no unit given (`quiz.js` `typeUnit`), a name several families share is right on each that it designates (`typeMatchIn` `shared`, Q319). Feedback lines: `quiz.js` `typeRead` (`typeSays`, `typeWhose`; "…, not this one.").
- **Commonness** (Pierre, 2026-11-04, approved after reviewing every family's ranking): each species of a teaching entry gets `cmi`, a commonness index within its entry, and the entry's `species` (and its sub-entries') are in that order, so the species path opens the most commonly met first. Three signals: field counts near Dauin (weight 3; `tools/fish_commonness.json` `field`, each source with its citation and licence: CCEF Siquijor 2025 presence per MPA, Ferrandis 2021 Dauin stereo-video counts), the IMR 24 most sighted (`cs`, weight 2), iNaturalist distinct observers within 40 km, 250 km and the Philippines (weight 1; `tools/fish_commonness_inat.json`), shrunk toward the entry's mean with M = 5. Computed by `tools/fish_commonness.py` (`apply()`), written by `tools/build_fish.py` (the full build, `--near`, `--commonness`; `--commonness --check`, run by `tests/fish.test.js`, fails when `fish.json` is not as the index gives it). Method and biases: docs/FISH_PATH.md "Species order: the commonness index".
- **Photos, stopgap** (`fish-photos.json`, `tools/fish_photos.py`, 2026-11-03): for an entry with fewer than 5 very easy curated photos ("Curated game photos" below; before admin plan PR 7, for every entry), the quiz shows **remote iNaturalist photos** (nothing downloaded or committed), as a coral candidate waiting to be downloaded (`r: 1`, `app.js` `img()`): per level-1 entry (every circle, winks too) up to 24 observations of its species in the game, one photo each (the observation's first), species taking turns in fish.json order; Research Grade in the Philippines CC0 / CC BY, then CC BY-NC, then Research Grade anywhere only for an entry left short (no ND nor share-alike licence). **Pierre's own photos** (Pierre, 2026-11-06, Q148): every compatible photo of his observations is in the list, whatever their number, never left out and never boosted (`his_ok`: identified to a species in the game, even Needs ID, rule S2, any licence: his; the Indo-West Pacific box, never captive or Dead; not rejected, excluded or moved with Not this in `curation.json`; not set aside by the judging rubric: a near-duplicate, an unclear subject, out of the water), every compatible photo of an observation, not only its first; they take none of the other observers' 24 places, and the order of the list says nothing (`core.js` `easyRank` orders it). Until 2026-11-06 his observations came first and filled the 24 places (442 of 1,160 photos, the four starter entries his alone). Built on 2026-11-06: 1,740 photos, 573 of them his (of his 644 photos in the game's species: 67 he rejected or excluded as candidates, 3 near-duplicates, 1 unclear subject); the new photos judged on `judging/FISH.md` (those already judged as candidates take that judgement), and the 4 circle-1 entries then under 5 very easy photos topped up to 6 with very easy candidates Pierre has in play (`tools/fish_judge.json` `add`). Photo objects as data.json's plus `sp` (fish.json species id) and `loc` (dauin within 40 km / ph / world). Replies cached in `tools/.cache/fish-photos.json` (git-ignored): `python3 tools/fish_photos.py [--offline | --refresh] [--per 24] [--sheet]`. `tools/fish_judge.py` `write` is the file's one writer (both tools use it). Varied quality on purpose (very good to bad, blue / green water) is the pipeline's goal, not this list's. `tests/subjects.test.js` checks the list (licences, one photo per observation, species in the game, every IMR entry has photos).
- **Sheet-only photos** (`fish-photos.json` `sheet`, Pierre, 2026-11-05: "no exception to the rules for the game"; a circle-1 species with no game photo nor candidate may keep a photo for its sheet): `{species id: [photo]}` for the species of `tools/fish_photos.py` `SHEET` (fish.json ids; add one there), written by `python3 tools/fish_photos.py --sheet` (the game photos kept as they are; a full run rewrites both). **A sheet photo passes every game photo rule** (Pierre, 2026-11-05: "if they don't pass the game rules, we don't keep them"; `sheet_ok`): licence CC0, CC BY or CC BY-NC (Pierre's own: any), Research Grade or Pierre's own identified to the species, taken in the Indo-West Pacific box of `tools/fetch_fish.py` (the Philippines inside it), never captive, never annotated Dead, checked on the observation that holds the photo. Order: the species' iNaturalist **cover** (the taxon's default photo), the taxon's other photos, then its observations in iNaturalist's order (Pierre's own with no priority since 2026-11-06), then **FishBase** (2026-11-05, `tools/fetch_fishbase.py`, the same rules as its candidates, "Fish candidates" below), its main picture first; none, and the species stays without a photo (its future sheet shows a generic fish drawing: ROADMAP "Fish sheets at three levels"). In practice it serves a species iNaturalist files under another one, so the candidate search (by the species' own observations) finds nothing. Two batched requests for all of them (the taxa, then their observations in the box). Photo objects as the game photos' (`f`, `o`, `src` remote, `r: 1`, `a`, `lic`, `t`, `sp`, `loc`, `ar`, `own`) plus **`use: 'ref'`** (sheet only, the corals' curation word), `from` (`cover` / `taxon` / `obs` / `fishbase`), `via: 'fishbase'` for a FishBase photo (`o` its FishBase photo page, `src` its image on www.fishbase.se, the credit "(c) author, via FishBase, some rights reserved (CC BY-NC 4.0)"), `rep` ("Fish candidates" below: the iNaturalist cover, FishBase's main picture) and `note` when iNaturalist files it under another species of fish.json (Pomacentrus armillatus: "Filed under Pomacentrus opisthostigma on iNaturalist, which lumps the two species (as WoRMS does)."; the name shown stays the PDF's, rule S2). **Never in a game**: `core.js` `fishGen` keeps every `use: 'ref'` photo out of an entry's `views.wide` wherever it is listed (so out of `model.js` `photoSet`, the cover, `app.js` `fishGenera`'s genus units and the winks: a unit with no game photo is never asked) and returns them by species as `sheet` (`app.js` `FISH.sheet`), for a species sheet to show: **no screen shows them yet**. Tests: `tests/subjects.test.js` (never a game photo nor a cover, licences, credit, the lumping note), `tests/fishphotos.test.js` (the rules and the order), `tests/fish.smoke.js` (never drawn in the real app). Run of 2026-11-05: *Pomacentrus armillatus* has the *P. opisthostigma* cover (Research Grade, Sabah, CC BY-NC); *Chromis agilis* (cover from the Maldives), *Siganus margaritiferus* (cover Needs ID), *Halichoeres marginatus* and *H. chlorocephalus* (no allowed photo in the box) have none; other photo sources for them are Pierre's decision (*C. agilis* and *H. marginatus*: candidates from their Pacific taxa since Q354 b, 2026-11-14, "Photo taxon" below). Run of 2026-11-05, later (Pierre: FishBase becomes a fish photo source; this photo his pick): *Halichoeres chlorocephalus* has J. E. Randall's FishBase photo of the holotype, photographed alive underwater at Madang, Papua New Guinea, before it was collected (`Hachl_m0.jpg`, FishBase's main picture, CC BY-NC 4.0; `note` says so); FishBase has nothing usable for the other three (*Chromis agilis*: the Maldives and Mauritius; *Siganus margaritiferus*: Sydney, and one with no place; *H. marginatus*: no place, or the Red Sea), nor for *Pomacentrus armillatus* (all rights reserved).
- **Curated game photos** (`fish-game.json`, `tools/fish_game.py`, admin plan PR 7, 2026-11-05): the photos Pierre put in play in the admin's fish Candidates take the stopgap's place. **Other species in view** (Pierre, 2026-11-09, Q323): a photo whose count in force is other species one could take for the subject (`k`), or the legacy `m` not sorted yet, is never in play, his adds included: the count in force is his tag, his legacy `m` refined by the judging pass's `o` or `k` (`core.js` `fishCount`, `fishOther`; `tools/fish_game.py` `count_of`, `other_out`, `refined`, which writes the sorted count into the game record's `jt`); `core.js` `fishIn` and `fishPhotos` (the stopgap's photos too) read the same (his tag at once in the admin; the players' curation has no tags, `tools/curplay.py`, so the app reads the game record's `jt`), and the admin lists his adds so held out apart (Photos › Tree, "Other species, out of play", with Keep in play: count `o`). **The photos in play by themselves** (Pierre, 2026-11-06 15:16 and 16:20, Q180 and Q199: fish candidates enter the game automatically once judged on the rubric, treated exactly like the photos he adds; every fish photo in play is hosted on R2, whatever its route in; his curation only removes or corrects): `tools/fish_game.py` `in_play` adds to his adds (1) every candidate judged sure (`cand_sure`: `autosort.js` `fishSure`'s add, i.e. no `unsure`, keep `best` or `alt`, on a sure judgement, `judged_sure`: valid tags, any ease 1-5, never count `x` nor flag `a`, and a count an automatic route may put in play, `core.js` `FISH_COUNT.play`: one fish `1`, several of its kind `s`, or other species in view with no risk of confusion `o`; never `k`, other species one could take for the subject, nor the legacy `m` until sorted: rule P3, Pierre, 2026-11-09, Q323) he has not decided on (added, rejected, excluded, moved), its judged tags, ease and phase, the pass's proposed crop applied as the auto-sort would write it into his draft; (2) the units' reference photos (`tools/fish_refs.json`) judged sure on the same rubric as a candidate, whatever their reference level (Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q212: "we bring them in like candidates"; until then only the reference level, `ref_level`, entered): `tools/fish_judge.json` codes through `tools/fish_judge.py` `cap`, then `judged_sure` (a reference photo has no keep or unsure field: the reference-photo batches only write codes; other species one could take for the subject, an unclear subject or a fish out of the water stay out), unless the same photo is already in play (always out); one on an observation already in play (another photo of it) enters once judged against that observation's photos, like a candidate (Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q218: "judge them with the others, see the duplicates, take them out"; `tools/fish_judge.json` `keep` {photo: best | alt | dup}: best or alt in, dup or not judged against them yet out; the game draws one observation a question, rule P4, so its photos share one draw; before, any photo of an observation in play kept it out); the reference level still orders them (`core.js` `refLevel`, `easyRank`, the draw's `calib.ref`); never the same photo twice (`pid`). Both are listed with `auto` (`cand` / `ref`; a reference photo keeps `from`), downloaded, cropped and written in the four sizes like his adds, and uploaded to R2 by the same workflow. **In play at play time** (`core.js` `fishIn`, used by `fishPhotos`; `fish_game.py` `fish_in` its copy, `tests/fishgame.test.js` checks they agree): his add (`fishOn`), or an `auto` photo whose photo id (`fishPid`) he did not reject, exclude or move with Not this (`fishGone`). **Plan of 2026-11-08** (Q212): 456 more reference photos in play (ease 4: 23, 3: 377, 2: 56; 107 from FishBase) over 239 species, 263 of them in circle 1, 3 in circle 2, 190 in circle 3; one species gains its first very easy photo (*Labrichthys unilineatus*), three of circle 3 their first of ease 3 or more; about 161 MB more on R2; 174 more below the level stayed out because the same photo or its observation was already in play (on 2026-11-08, after Q218: of the 1,381 reference photos judged sure, 1,088 in play, 286 the same photo as one already in play, 7 a photo Pierre decided against, none waiting on its observation). The Candidate photos workflow also runs on a change to `tools/fish_game.py`, so such a change is downloaded and uploaded at its merge. **Batches**: at most `MAX` (5,000; `FISH_MAX`, `--max`) downloads a run, the reference photos first, then the starter entries' photos, circle 1's, the rest; what is left comes at the next run, and the game lists only photos already on R2 (the original stays the fallback, `core.js` `photoAway`). FishBase's mirror `www.fishbase.se` is an allowed host (the candidates and reference photos name it since 2026-11-06). **Plan of 2026-11-06** (`python3 tools/fish_game.py --plan`): 8,308 photos in play over 438 species (Pierre's adds 3,822, candidates judged sure 4,077, reference photos 409), 61 from FishBase, about 2.9 GB on R2 (33,232 files); the workflow's run of that day downloaded his 3,822 in 9 minutes. The stopgap list (`fish-photos.json`) stays only as the fallback of the entries still short of 5 very easy photos on R2 (18 of circles 2 and 3 on that plan), and goes once none is. **In play** (`core.js` `fishOn`; `fish_game.py` `fish_on` its copy, `tests/fishgame.test.js` checks they agree): `curation.json` `fish.add` names the species, the photo is neither rejected (`seen`) nor excluded, and its Not this (`out`), if set, names the same species; the top-level `exclude` (by name or photo id) too. **Download** (the Candidate photos workflow, `tools/fetch_approved.py --fish` after each Publish): each photo in play is fetched from its original in `fish-cand.json` (`src`: iNaturalist's photo hosts or `www.fishbase.de` only; FishBase asked up to 4 times, 5 s, 10 s, 15 s apart, as it answers 500 at times, and after the others, one at a time), Pierre's crop applied (`fish.crop`; `[0, 0, 1, 1]` = none), saved in the four sizes by `tools/photos.py` `save()` into `r2-upload/`, never `img/` (it has an online original: R2 only), uploaded by `tools/r2_sync.sh r2-upload` before the commit, so `fish-game.json` never names a photo R2 lacks; a photo already downloaded is kept, downloaded again when its crop changes (`cr`, `iv` bumped, `app.js` `ver`); one that fails is left out until the next run; one no longer in play leaves the list (its files stay on R2, unused). `python3 tools/fish_game.py --plan` prints what a run would download, the expected R2 volume and the entries left with the stopgap, offline. **Record**: `{f (the candidate's name, fish-<species>-cand-<id> or -cand-fb-<stem>), o (its observation, or the FishBase photo page), src (the original: core.js photoAway's fallback when R2 fails), a, lic, t, sp (the species it is in play for, after a Not this its new one, `t` then its `fish.json` name, WoRMS's else the PDF's, never the identification Pierre corrected: Codex review #614), loc, own, ar, e (fish.ease, else suggest.json fish e), jt (fish.tags, else the pass's), ph (its phase as autosort.js fishPhase), cr, iv, via, rep}`, no `r`, so `app.js` `img()` reads it from R2. **Layout**: by teaching entry (the level-1 entry whose `species` holds the species), entries in `fish.json` order, an entry's photos by its species order, the easiest, then by name (never Pierre's own by priority, Q148; the app orders them with `core.js` `easyRank`); one photo per line, and an unchanged run writes the same bytes. **In the game** (`core.js` `fishPhotos(stop, game, cur)`, what `fishGen` takes; the app's `fishBuild` passes `CUR`): an entry with `FISHPH.MIN` (5) very easy curated photos (ease 4+, `model.js` `easyFirst`'s photos; Pierre's minimum of 2026-11-04, `tools/fish_judge.py` `WANT`) plays on them alone, but for the stopgap's photos of any scientific genus its curated photos do not cover (the genus as `app.js` `fishGenera` reads it, from `fish.json`: no genus unit loses its photos when its entry switches; none on the curation of 2026-11-05; Codex review #614); with fewer, the stopgap's photos join them, minus every photo Pierre rejected, excluded or moved as a candidate (matched by photo id, `fishPid`: before PR 7, 12 stopgap photos he had rejected were in the game) and the photos or observations its curated list holds. Pierre's curation applies at once, before the workflow runs again: a curated photo shows only while in play (an Out of play, a Not this, a Reject takes it out at the app's next curation read). With no curation loaded, the lists as they are. The admin's To do indexes both lists (`admin.js` `fishAdmin`), so a report on either finds its entry. A FishBase game photo shows its source pill after the answer (`app.js` `photoSrc`: author · FishBase ↗). Tests: `tests/fishgame.test.js` (the pipeline on a fixture with a stub host: only photos in play, four files, the crop, FishBase retried, the same bytes on a rerun, a recrop, an Out of play; `fishPhotos`), `tests/photos.test.js` (rejected, excluded and Not this photos never in the game), `tests/fishbase.test.js` (no FishBase game photo Pierre did not add), `tests/fish.test.js` (codes and the rubric's caps where the ease is the pass's; 5 very easy photos per circle-1 entry in the game). **First run** (after the merge, on the curation of 2026-11-05): 3,822 photos of 438 species (3 from FishBase, 541 Pierre's own), 15,288 files, about 1.35 GB on R2 (361 KB a photo, four files: the mean of 24 photos already there); `fish-game.json` about 1.26 MB (110 KB gzipped). 29 entries then play on curated photos alone (the 27 IMR entries, Frogfishes, Seahorses & pipefishes); 18 entries of circles 2 and 3 keep the stopgap's beside theirs (fewer than 5 very easy curated photos: Barracuda, Ghost pipefishes, Dragonets, Seamoths, Scorpionfishes, Stonefishes, Flying gurnards, Stargazers, Jawfishes, Snake eels, Morays, Garden eels, Rays, Reef sharks, Porcupinefishes, Jacks, and the winks Whale shark and Thresher sharks).
- **The file's short form** (Pierre, 2026-11-09, the performance pass): a record's `o`, an iNaturalist observation, is written as its id alone, and its `src`, the original on iNaturalist's open-data bucket, as its extension alone when the photo is the record's own (the id in its name); FishBase and any other address stay whole (`tools/fish_short.py`, written by `tools/fish_game.py` `dumps`; `--rewrite` writes the file again in it, after a merge that took another copy). 5.29 → 3.58 million characters, 503 → 426 KB gzipped. Every reader gives the records back whole first: `core.js` `fishGameRead` (the app's `fishLoad`, the admin, the sheet's fish photo pools, `tools/ratedworld.js`, the tests and `tests/photostub.js`), `fish_short.full_doc` (`tools/fish_game.py` `load`, `tools/photos.py` `records`); a file in the whole form is read as it is (`tests/fishshort.test.js`). A page of an older version meeting the short file (a page left open across the deploy, whose own `?v=` request reaches the server when its worker has no copy) keeps every photo (its `src` is still set, and R2 serves it), but its fallback to the original and its observation link are broken until it updates (rule W3); a sheet script from before reads the same photo pools (an observation's id groups its photos as its address did).
- **Pierre's choices per family in the app** (2026-11-08: his ★ Cover choices, published, never reached the app): `curation.json` `fish` `genera` `{unit id: {cover, feat: {species id: [f]}, ok}}` (the admin's Tree page, `autosort.js` `fishPick`) is applied after the build (`app.js` `fishBuild` → `fishCurate`, run again whenever the curation is): **★ Cover** becomes the unit's `coverP`, which `cover()` reads first (as a coral genus's), so the Learn families list, the family sheet's hero and its look-alike cards, the first-meeting cards (Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q289), My progress, a wink's surprise card (Q290) and Home show it (Home's landscape tiles and the Identify door's art only when it suits them, `home.js` `tilePhoto`, Q291), while it is among the unit's game photos (`views.wide`, matched by photo id: `core.js` `fishPid`), else the default cover (below: the most seen species' easiest photo; `rankPhotos` never takes his pick back); **Feature** becomes `featP` `{species id: [photo]}`, the photos still in play of that species in his order, which lead that species' photos on the sheets (`learn.js` `fishSpPhotos`), as the admin's page shows them (`fishOrder`). **A genus's and a species' ★ Cover** (Pierre, 2026-11-13: "choisir les couvertures des genres et les couvertures des espèces") sit in the same map under their `fish.json` ids, `{cover}` only (`g:Chromis`, `s091`), set on the Tree tab's node pages (since 2026-11-14: ★ Cover on a genus's or a species' own page sets that node's cover; the bar's ★ Family | ★ Genus | ★ Species switch of #858, the stopgap Pierre accepted on 2026-11-13, "Ok. Mais ça ne sera pas vraiment générique si on commence à multiplier les niveaux", went with them; the rank words read from `core.js` `SUBJECTS` `sheets`, broadest first, never a list of our own: `admin.js` `fuRanks`, `fuRankOf`, rule S1; ★ Cover elsewhere, a report or Health, sets the family's), each genus and species heading showing its cover in force (ringed when it is his pick), the tile marked (Family / Genus / Species cover) (`admin.js` `renderFishUnits`); Publish counts them apart, rank by rank (`pubSummary`: fish family, fish genus cover, fish species cover). **Another subject** (a nudibranch or plant key, or the corals at more than one rank) needs only data: its ranks in `SUBJECTS` `sheets`, its tree on its unit page (the fish page's `fishUnits`: units, then nodes, then leaves), and its covers read by node id at build (as `fishCurate` does from `fish.json`); the keys, `fishCover`, `fishDrop`, the switch and the Publish summary take any rank. The corals today have one rank (the genus: their covers are `curation.json` `genera[n].cover`) and no unit page of this kind, so no switch. In the app a genus unit (circle 1) takes its cover as its `coverP`, as a family (`cover()`: its Learn card, first-meeting card, My progress); a genus with no unit and a species get it in `FISH.covers` `{id: photo}`, while it is in play as a photo of that genus or species; `learn.js` `genCover` (a genus's cover: `coverP`, else `FISH.covers`) leads the genus's row on the family sheet (then the first photo of its other species) and its card among the sibling genera on a genus sheet (the genus sheet has no hero), and `fishSpPhotos` puts the species' cover first (then his featured photos, then the game's order): its tile, the first photo of its sheet. **A juvenile photo is never a cover**, at any rank, picked or not (Pierre, 2026-11-13: "Un juvénile ne devrait probablement jamais finir en couverture"): juvenile = its phase word (`autosort.js` `fishJuv`: `fishPhase`, his phase and tags, else the pass's, else the source's life stage; in the app a record's `ph`, which `tools/fish_game.py` writes the same way, or its tags' juvenile flag: `app.js` `juvP`). In the admin ★ Cover is off on a juvenile tile (its title says why); a pick already on one is ignored (`fishCoverOf`, the headings) and flagged ("cover ignored" on the tile, "N covers on a juvenile" in the bar), its button then only taking it off. In the app `fishCurate` ignores such a pick (no `coverP`, no `FISH.covers`) and every default skips juveniles while the unit has another photo (the card's cover in `fishCurate` and `rankPhotos`, the family hero and look-alike cards, the sibling genus cards: `seenCover`; `adultFirst`: a species' one photo `spPhoto`, Home's landscape tiles `home.js` `tilePhoto`); a unit of juveniles only keeps its first; the species sheet shows its adults first, the juveniles apart (`learn.js` `spSheetPhotos`). A photo leaving play (Remove, ×, Not this, a count that takes it out, Out of play and Reject in Candidates: `autosort.js` `fishDrop`) leaves every cover and featured list it was in, one Undo step. Neither touches the games' photos or their order. `ok` (✓ Validated) is the admin's own. A candidate he adds and picks at once shows in the app once the Candidate photos workflow has put it in `fish-game.json`; until then the card keeps the cover as before (tests: `tests/fishcovers.test.js`; `tests/fish.smoke.js` the Learn cards and the family sheet's hero).
- **The default cover of a node with leaves** (Pierre, 2026-11-13, Q339 (b): "ok" to distinct observers rather than observations; 2026-11-14 on #864: among the species that have a reference photo, his ★ species cover first; ROADMAP "The Tree tab"): when he has picked no cover for a family or a genus, it is a photo of its leaf (for the fish, the species) **most seen around the instance's centre by distinct observers** (below, "The instance's centre") **among its leaves with a reference-level photo** that is not a juvenile (`core.js` `refLevel`), ties broken by the observations there, then by the easy order; then **his ★ cover of that species** when he picked one in play (never overridden: "mon choix était le meilleur au moment avec ce que j'avais sous les yeux"), else that species' easiest reference photo in the game's order (`core.js` `easyRank`: the judged ease or the players' ratings, the licence breaking ties); a node with no reference photo under it falls back on the most seen species with an adult photo (his ★ cover of it, else its easiest adult photo); never a juvenile while the node has another photo; a node whose species carry no presence keeps the easy order's first, as before. The species most seen is read from `fish.json` `present` (`nearU` distinct Research Grade observers and `nearN` observations within the centre's radius, under their first names `dauinU` and `dauin` until the build writes the generic ones: `app.js` `nearSeen`, `autosort.js` `fishNear`). One rule in two copies, the app's `app.js` `seenCover` (the card's photo: `fishCurate` without his pick, `rankPhotos` on the rated order; `learn.js` `nodeCover` over the species' photos in play, the sheet-only ones only when none is: the family sheet's hero, its look-alike cards, a genus sheet's sibling genus cards) and the admin's `autosort.js` `fishCoverOf` (the family's and the genus headings' default thumbs on the Tree page; the species records it reads registered by `admin-fish.js` through `fishSeenFrom`, since `admin.js` calls it with four arguments); `tests/fishcovers.test.js` holds them together on fixtures and, on the real files, checks that every unit without a pick shows its most seen species in the app and the admin alike (on 2026-11-14's files: 49 of 180 card photos changed, none losing the reference level; 28 units keep his pick; 18 nodes have no reference photo and take the fallback; Chromis: *C. viridis*, 16 observers, instead of *C. retrofasciata*, its "Most met here" line still following the commonness index until the centre rename). A species' own cover is unchanged (one leaf: his pick, else its easiest photo); his node picks stay as they are (the default never replaces a manual choice); the genus row's stack on a family sheet (one photo per species, commonness order) and Home's landscape tiles (a shape that suits the tile) do not read the default cover. **Corals**: a coral genus is both the unit and the leaf, so the rule leaves its cover as it is (rule P1's source order, then `applySlots`); it applies to coral nodes above the genus (a family) once the Tree tab has them, which first needs each genus's distinct observers near the centre written at build (`presence.json` holds coral species' presence in the Philippines, `ok`, `rg`, not counts near the centre; `tools/coral_presence.py` already asks iNaturalist for the counts around the centre and would keep them). The counts are computed once, at build (rule L13); the app only compares them.
- **Identification groups: the regional key** (Pierre, 2026-11-04; rule S3). Fish groups come from the identification key of the deployment's region, never from a list of our own: `tools/fish_source.json` `keys` holds the keys (`id`, `region` {name, area}, `cite` the full citation, `short` its short form, `src`, `groups`: `n`, `title` exact, `pages`, `labels` [label as the key prints it, [WoRMS accepted names it covers], note]) and `deploy` the deployment's region and the key in force (here the index of the 20 identification groups of *Reef Fish Identification – Tropical Pacific*, 2nd ed., which the IMR course follows, transcribed from Pierre's screenshots of its index; its 10 IMR shape groups are gone). `build_fish.py` gives each teaching node the group of the first key label met walking up WoRMS from each of its references (a subfamily label wins: Parrotfishes over Wrasses) and fails, naming the node, when a node in the game (any circle, winks aside) has no group or spans two, unless the source makes it explicit (`keyGroup` {key id: n}; null with `keyWhy` while a node waits for Pierre, printed by the build; none waits now: the clingfishes, not among the index's family labels, left circle 3 on 2026-11-04). A label the book does not print is added only by Pierre's decision, its note saying so: *Thresher sharks* (Alopiidae) in group 20, Sharks & Rays (2026-11-08, Q232: seen on the reefs in the Philippines, at Malapascua above all), so the thresher wink has a group too. `fish.json` `key` = the key in force without its labels (`fishGen` `key`, `groups`); the app shows the key's numbers and titles, `cite` on the families page and `short` everywhere else (one edit changes both). `python3 tools/build_fish.py --regroup` re-applies the key to `fish.json` offline from its own reference tree (after an edit of `keys`, `deploy` or `keyGroup`, or to settle a merge of `fish.json`); `--regroup --check` (run by `tests/fish.test.js`) fails when `fish.json` is not as the key gives it. A region with no key has no group rule (`deploy.key` unset); a key for another region is data only.
- **Starter core and judged photos** (Pierre, 2026-11-04): `fish.json` `start` (from `tools/fish_source.json`) lists the circle-1 entries met first (`fishGen` marks them `first`, `model.js` `openPick` opens them before any other); `dimorph: true` on a teaching entry whose presentation phase lines say the sexes or colour phases differ (Parrotfish, Anthias). `juv` on every circle-1 entry: its juvenile class (A, B, C, mixed with `early` / `late` genera or species, ns), `trait`, Pierre's `obs`, `note` and sources (`src`, `by`), from a research note of 2026-11-04; `tools/fish_judge.py` caps the ease of a juvenile photo unless its entry is A or its species is in `early` (judging/FISH.md). `tools/fish_judge.json` `add` holds the photos a judging pass added for entries short of very easy ones (from `fish-cand.json`). Each judged photo of `fish-photos.json` carries `e` (ease 1-5), `jt` (its tags) and maybe `c` (a proposed crop `[l, t, r, b]`, never applied), written by `tools/fish_judge.py` from `tools/fish_judge.json` (rubric: `judging/FISH.md`; `tools/fish_photos.py` re-applies them on a rebuild, `--check` tells whether the file is current); `fishGen` orders each entry's photos with `easyRank` (Pierre, 2026-11-06: the easiest first, by the judged ease, then the most reference-like by their tags, `refScore`: the reference flag `r`, then profile, whole fish, good light, no colour loss, sharp, large, one fish; never by who took them) and makes the first the card's `cover`, so the families list, Home and the cards show it too; the app ranks them again on `model.js` `photoDiff` (`app.js` `rankPhotos`, at load and with each new photo stats reply), so the players' ratings take over once fish answers are logged (sheet v58 embeds this `core.js`, its behaviour unchanged); `model.js` reads `e` in `photoDiff` (prior) and `easyFirst` (very easy photos only until Open Water: a temporary bootstrap until fish answers are logged) and `easyTrust` (docs/LEARNING.md "Fish: the starter core and judged photos").
- **Rebuild**: edit `tools/fish_source.json`, then `python3 tools/build_fish.py` (prints the PDF names WoRMS has changed, the likely slips, the species out of the game, those confirmed by Pierre's observations only, WoRMS / iNaturalist mismatches and a PDF-section cross-check; `--dry` writes nothing), then `node --test tests/fish.test.js`. Every WoRMS and iNaturalist reply is cached in `tools/.cache/fish.json` (git-ignored), so a rerun is offline and identical; `--refresh` asks again (names and counts move), `--offline` never asks.

### Fish candidates (`fish-cand.json`, 2026-11-03, merged 2026-11-04; admin page: fish Candidates, admin plan PR 5a)
Candidate photos for the fish subject, to be curated by Pierre in the admin (fish Candidates, admin plan PR 5: "Admin for every subject" below), on the model of the coral candidates (Pierre's decisions, 2026-11-03 and 2026-11-04). Built on the `fishphotos` branch of 2026-11-03 and merged without its admin button (the subject switch replaces it).
- **Fetch**: `tools/fetch_fish.py` (documented at its top; tests `tests/fishphotos.test.js`, offline fixtures). Per species in the game of the circle-1 entries (`--circle` for the other circles), iNaturalist only, never captive, never an observation annotated Dead. **Identity**: Research Grade at the species level, or Pierre's own observations identified to the species even when Needs ID (rule S2, marked `ni: 1`). **Licences** (Pierre, 2026-11-03; corals keep NC): only CC0, CC BY and CC BY-NC; never share-alike (it would block a later commercial reuse) nor ND; each photo keeps its licence (`lic`), so a commercial version could keep only CC0 / CC BY and Pierre's own. **Abroad = the Indo-West Pacific only** (Pierre, 2026-11-03: the region of the ID books IMR uses; other oceans have other colour forms): a bounding box, `IWP` = 30° S-30° N, 90° E-170° E (from the Andaman Sea and Sumatra to Vanuatu and New Caledonia, from Taiwan and the Ryukyus to the southern Great Barrier Reef; out: the Maldives, Sri Lanka, the Red Sea and East Africa, Fiji, Samoa, Hawaii, French Polynesia, mainland Japan), passed to every search abroad and checked again on each observation's coordinates (`in_iwp`), Pierre's own included. **Order** (`select`): Pierre's own observations first for every species, whatever their licence (asked for every species, not only those `fish.json` `present.own` counted at its last build, so a species he identified since gets them too; Codex review #463); then, for a species confirmed near Dauin (`present.near`, "Fish content" above), photos from anywhere in the Philippines and the Indo-West Pacific, not by distance; for any other species, the Philippines first, nearest first (within 40 km of Dauin, within 250 km, the rest of the country), and abroad only for a species confirmed in the Philippines (`present.ph`, rule C8) once the Philippines are exhausted. **Variety on purpose**: no votes order and no quality filter (poor photos stand in for SVS video frames): the least represented observer and iNaturalist life stage first, then the licence as a tie-break (CC0 / CC BY before NC: "Photo rights tie-break" below; Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q284; until then a bucket before the distance and the variety), then a fixed hash order of the observation. A photo that belongs to several iNaturalist observations is taken once, and an observation left with no photo of its own takes no room (Codex review #463; 3 such duplicates were in the first file). **Amount: the cap weighted by phases** (Pierre, 2026-11-05, Q36: "as long as it holds, we hold"; before, 20 observations per species): **30 photos for a stable species, +10 per extra phase, at most 60** (`cap_of`; `BASE_CAP`, `PER_PHASE`, `MAX_CAP`). The extra phases are juvenile, intermediate phase (IMR's "intermediate", "sub-adult"; FishBase's initial phase), male or terminal phase, female (`PHASES`), counted when recorded (`species_phases`, each with what records it): the IMR presentation's phase lines and `dimorph` mark of the species' entry (`imr`), the juvenile classes (`juv`: class A or B, or the genus or species named `early` / `late` in a mixed entry), a judging tag on one of its photos (`judged`: the juvenile flag `j` or the rubric's `phase`, candidates and game photos), the iNaturalist Life Stage or Sex annotation of at least 2 distinct candidate observations (`inat:N`, `MIN_ANNOT`: one stray annotation is not a phase), FishBase's life stage on its underwater photos (`fishbase:N`). On the 442 game species (2026-11-05): 200 at 30, 172 at 40, 36 at 50, 34 at 60 (16,720 photos at most). **Each extra phase holds 10 of them for photos of that phase** (`phase_room`: the room of a phase = 10 minus the photos it keeps of it, by `photo_phase`: the judging tags first, then the annotation `st`; a short room is shared out one photo at a time), the rest is free. **The search fills each phase first** (`fill`): one search per phase iNaturalist can filter (`INAT_PHASE`: Life Stage Juvenile, Sex Male, Sex Female; `term_id` / `term_value_id`) for every species with room in it, 20 taxa a request, paged by `id_above` (`phase_obs`, rule C7), the Philippines first, abroad (Indo-West Pacific) as for any photo from abroad; then the free room and the rooms of the phases iNaturalist cannot filter (intermediate: the judging tags say which they show) from the general searches. A phase's room iNaturalist cannot fill stays open for that phase (FishBase next, or a later run): never adults in a juvenile's place. The cap counts usable photos (added, in by the pre-sort, or waiting; what is rejected, excluded or sent elsewhere, or set aside for sure by the pre-sort, frees room). **Every photo of an observation** (Pierre, 2026-11-08: no limit per observation any more, `PER_OBS` 4 until then): an observation brings all its photos, also one already among the candidates at the next run (but one with a photo Pierre sent elsewhere), and its photos count against the cap by weight (`obs_cost`, Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q226, option b: "better for learning on the species already full"): its first usable photo 1, each further one `EXTRA_W` (0.5), no cap per observation; a short room cuts an observation to what fits (`obs_fit`). On 2026-11-08 this freed 544.5 photos of room over 286 of the 442 species, 63 of them full before (offline `--refill --plan`: 442 short species instead of 410, 4 at their cap instead of 35). **`--topup`** (`topup`, run by every `--refill` first, over all the species of its circles, whatever their room): the observations fetched under the old limit (`OLD_LIMIT`: exactly 4 candidate records, none sent elsewhere; 504 on 2026-11-08, about 260 photos more on the 2026-11-04 cache for the half of them it holds) are asked again by id, 200 a request, and bring their other photos; one with exactly 4 photos on iNaturalist is asked again at each refill (about 2-3 requests a run), so one observation of many photos takes less room than as many observations (one of 20 photos takes 10.5), and once judged its near-duplicates free their room (`sure_out`); the judging pass marks an observation's near-duplicates (`keep` `dup`, judging/FISH.md), whose contact sheets never split an observation (`tools/judging_fish.py` `pack`) and show first, as context, a photo of it Pierre already added (`added`; then at most one new `best`, none needed; Codex review #715). The phase searches abroad open when the Philippines leave a phase's room short as `obs_cost` counts it. One function for both tools: `species_room` (cap, phases, photos kept per phase, room per phase). iNaturalist API v2 with only the fields used, about one request a second (`tools/inat.py`); the general searches of the species with at most 150 observations in a search (`species_counts` says) are asked together, `taxon_id=a,b,c`, read to their end, and each species' share answers its own search exactly (`prefetch`, `Net.virtual`; a species with more keeps its own search; 70 requests instead of 204 for a refill of 40 species on a made-up world, #784); every reply cached, trimmed, in `tools/.cache/fish_photos.json` (not in the repo): a rerun is offline and identical, `--refresh` asks again, `--offline` never asks, `--plan` prints offline per species its cap, phases, photos kept per phase, room per phase and what a run would add per source and phase (`"caps"`: species per cap; `"cached": false` for a species whose replies are not in the cache), `--only s091,t05`, `--cap N` one fixed cap for a test or a one-off. First live run with phases, 2026-11-05, three species (22 requests): *Pomacanthus sexstriatus* (juvenile, cap 40) +18 photos, 9 of them juveniles; *Chlorurus bleekeri* (all four phases, cap 60) +30, 4 male and 9 female (iNaturalist has one Research Grade juvenile of it worldwide: its juvenile and intermediate rooms stay open); *Thalassoma lunare* (juvenile and terminal male, cap 50) +29, 9 juveniles and 7 males; FishBase had nothing more to add for them. Hosting: ROADMAP "Hosting capacity (watch)".
- **Photo taxon** (Pierre, 2026-11-14, Q354 b): a species whose photos are best taken from another iNaturalist taxon (the Pacific form iNaturalist splits off while the IMR list keeps the older name) carries `photoTaxon` `{inat, name, note, src, why}`, written in `tools/fish_source.json` and copied as is into `fish.json` by `tools/build_fish.py`. Every photo search goes through `fetch_fish.py` `photo_taxon(s)` (that taxon, else the species' own `inat`): the general and phase searches, the covers (`--rep`), the top-up, and `tools/fish_refs.py`'s reference photos. Everything else stays the species' own: its IMR `name` on screen (rule C1), its `inat` for presence (rule C8: the IMR-named species' evidence; both species are confirmed near Dauin by the IMR list either way), commonness and the sheet's iNaturalist link; the candidates are the species' (`fish-<id>-cand-…`), their `t` iNaturalist's name. The admin says it under the species' name on its family page and on its Candidates card (`admin.js` `fishPtx`: "Photos from *X* (iNat), Pacific form; IMR name kept"). Each one is also a C5 point for an IMR trainer (`points.json`). Now: s088 *Chromis agilis* from *Pycnochromis pacifica* (1313512), s244 *Halichoeres marginatus* from *Halichoeres annularis* (1474879).
- **Automatic refill** (Pierre, 2026-11-06, Q171: when a fish unit runs short of candidates the tools fetch more on their own; "no more candidates is no more candidates"; rule W20; `fetch_fish.py --refill`, tests `tests/fishphotos.test.js`): the "Candidate photos" workflow's `fish` job, after each Publish (and on manual dispatch), after the coral `photos` job, commits `fish-cand.json` and `tools/fish_refill.json`. **Short** (`shortness`): a species under its cap (the room of `species_room`: the Q36 cap net of what Pierre rejected, excluded or sent elsewhere and of the pre-sort's sure set-asides), or with fewer than `EASY_NEED` (3) observations holding a very easy photo (ease ≥ `EASY_E`, 4: `model.js` easyFirst's `PDIFF.EARLY` and `PICKD.VARIETY`) in play or sure to come (Pierre's adds, the pre-sort's sure adds `sure_add`, the mirror of `fishSure`'s adds, the game photos of `fish-photos.json`). Only room is refilled: a species short of easy photos at its cap stays as it is (the cap holds; its waiting candidates need the pass or Pierre), listed as `at_cap` by `--refill --plan`. **Which**: at most `REFILL_MAX` (40) species a run (rule C7: about 6 requests each, plus the batched phase searches), the shortest first (short of easy photos with nothing waiting for the judging pass, then the largest share of the cap missing, then teaching order); the first runs work through the full refill at the Q36 caps (440 species, about 8,000 photos) about 40 species a Publish. **How**: `run` with the rules above (Research Grade or Pierre's own, the licences, never captive or Dead, the Philippines first, abroad the Indo-West Pacific only and only for a species confirmed in the Philippines; observations already in its list, photos already used anywhere and excluded photo ids are never taken again, so never what Pierre rejected, excluded or marked Not this); each general search is asked afresh (no stale cache) and read on (`pages`, `id_below`, up to `GEN_PAGES` = 5 pages of 200) while it brings fewer usable photos than the room; then FishBase (`fetch_fishbase.py`, offline) fills what iNaturalist left and `--rep` tags the covers, for the refilled species only. **Exhaustion** (`exhausted`, `tools/fish_refill.json` `end` {species: {at: day, parts}}): a part of the room ("free", the general searches; `juvenile`, `male`, `female`, the phase searches) still open after the run whose searches were read to their end (`ends`), or which got nothing, is searched to the end that day; the species is skipped while every open part of its room is in a record less than `RECHECK_DAYS` (30) old (a part opened since, e.g. a juvenile room, is searched); a species filled loses its record. **Judging**: nothing is judged in the workflow (the pass is visual): the new candidates wait in `fish-cand.json` for the judging pass (`python3 tools/judging_fish.py todo`; the full `tools/brief.py` prints how many wait), whose sure decisions the admin's fish auto-sort applies at the next load, then Pierre's Publish (rule A1). First live check (2026-11-06, scratch copy): the 5 species with no candidate found nothing and were noted; *Abudefduf vaigiensis* +19 (10 juveniles), not noted.
- **FishBase** (Pierre, 2026-11-05: "the reference platform for fish, photos well linked to the species; if their licence is compatible, integrate it, fully"; `tools/fetch_fishbase.py`, documented at its top; tests `tests/fishbase.test.js`): a second source of candidates, with the same rules and the same curation (a FishBase photo enters as a candidate, judged on `judging/FISH.md`, and reaches a game only through Pierre's Publish; `tests/fishbase.test.js` fails on a FishBase game photo he did not add). **Source: FishBase's own data, never its pages**: the table `picturesmain` of the rfishbase snapshot (huggingface.co/datasets/cboettig/fishbase, v25.04, CC BY-NC, the FishBase team's export for rfishbase and the FishBase API), its underwater photos of every game species kept in `tools/fishbase_src.json` (one species per line: file, FishBase name, author, licence field, main picture, coordinates, country, locality, life stage, holotype / paratype). FishBase's robots.txt (2026-11-05) allows every user agent but AI-training crawlers (`Content-Signal: search=yes, ai-train=no, use=reference`), fishbase.org sits behind a browser challenge, and no terms-of-use page was found; so nothing is scraped: the photos stay on FishBase's www.fishbase.se mirror (`src`, remote `r: 1`, never committed; www.fishbase.de answers browsers' image requests 500 since 2026-11-06) and are downloaded only when they enter the game (admin plan PR 7). **Rules**: picture type "underwater photo" only (never "photo", mostly specimens, nor tank, aquarium or public-aquarium photos, nor drawings); licence: only what FishBase states as CC BY-NC 4.0 (its `Copyright` field "non-commercial use", "free use" or the public-domain flag, whose photo pages all show CC BY-NC 4.0: the stricter of field and page is taken; "permission required" and empty, all rights reserved, never), so every FishBase candidate is `lic: cc-by-nc`; the Indo-West Pacific box on the photo's coordinates, or, without them, a country wholly inside it (`IWP_COUNTRIES`), abroad only for a species confirmed in the Philippines; the identification FishBase's own (taken as given, like Research Grade). **Order and amount**: the cap of `fetch_fish.py` (`species_room`, Q36; one FishBase photo = one observation), run after it so FishBase fills the room iNaturalist leaves: each phase's room first with the photos FishBase labels with it (juvenile, initial phase, terminal phase, male, female), then the free room (a phase's room left unfilled stays open); within each, FishBase's main picture first, then the nearest. Records as the iNaturalist ones (`f` `fish-<species>-cand-fb-<file stem>`, `o` the FishBase photo page, `t` FishBase's name, `st` from FishBase's life stage) plus `via: "fishbase"`. First run (2026-11-05, circles 1-3): 158 candidates for 130 species (53 FishBase main pictures; 1502 underwater photos of 412 game species in the snapshot, 522 of them with an accepted licence), among them 4 for *Halichoeres chlorocephalus*, which had none (J. E. Randall, Madang, Milne Bay, Alotau: the holotype, a paratype, two more); most species were full, so most get 1 or 2. Judged so far: the 4 of *H. chlorocephalus* (`judging/fish/s238.json`: three sure, one unsure, maybe a juvenile); the other 154 wait for the judging pass (`python3 tools/judging_fish.py todo`).
- **Reference photos** (`rep`, Pierre, 2026-11-05: a photo a reference source uses to represent the species "is easy to identify, so it should come early in the quiz for a beginner", and suits the sheets): `rep: "fishbase"` on FishBase's main picture of the species, `rep: "inaturalist"` on a candidate that is its taxon's iNaturalist cover (default photo; `fetch_fish.py --rep`, 30 taxa a request: 28 candidates on 2026-11-05), on candidates and sheet-only photos. The judging adds the flag `r` to the photo's tags and one ease point (`tools/fish_judge.py` `cap`, `REP_BONUS`; the caps applied after it; `judging/FISH.md` "Reference photos"), so once Pierre adds one it reaches the beginner path through its ease (`model.js` `easyFirst`, `photoDiff`, the card cover); `python3 tools/judging_fish.py rep` re-applies the flag to the judged candidates of `suggest.json` after a new tag. The game photos: **the units' reference photos** (Pierre, 2026-11-06: covers and first photos must be reference photos, whoever took them): `tools/fish_refs.py` (iNaturalist v1 taxa, 30 a request; v2 observation searches with only the fields used: the 200 most faved Research Grade observations in the `IWP` box, then the 200 most faved anywhere while a taxon photo is missing, then an id window around the missing photo's estimated observation id, `obs_of`, since iNaturalist has no search by photo; cached in `tools/.cache/fish_refs.json`) writes `tools/fish_refs.json`, kept by species, the photos iNaturalist shows for each game species (its cover and every taxon-page photo whose licence the rules accept, never ND: Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q229 and Q231), circle-1 genus and family (the first) that pass the game photo rules on their observation (one outside the box is listed under `away` with its rough region, never taken, while Q230 is open) (a genus's or family's: of a game species of the entry), plus FishBase's photos of each species that pass its rules (its main picture first, up to 3; Pierre, 2026-11-06 14:02: FishBase enters automatically when its licence allows); `tools/fish_judge.py` `add_refs` puts each judged one into its entry's game photos (`rep: "inaturalist"` / `"fishbase"` on a source's reference photos, `from` `cover` / `taxon` / `fishbase`, `via: "fishbase"`); a FishBase photo shows a "FishBase ↗" source pill after the answer (`app.js` `photoSrc`). They stay in play even on an entry that plays on Pierre's curated photos alone (`core.js` `fishPhotos`). **Reference level** (`core.js` `refLevel`: a `rep` photo judged 4-5, or any photo judged 5 with the reference look) comes first in `easyRank` (covers, each list's first photos) and weighs `PICKD.REFW` (3) in the draw when never seen (`model.js` `photoSet`). Tested in `tests/fish.test.js`, `tests/subjects.test.js`.
- **`fish-cand.json`** (admin only, like `cand.json`; players never download it, not in the `sw.js` shell): `{"v": 1, "cand": {species id: [photo]}}`, one species per line (`tools/datajson.py` `dump` layout), species in `fish.json` order. Photo records have the coral candidates' shape: `f` (`fish-<species id>-cand-<iNat photo id>`), `a`, `lic`, `o`, `t` (iNaturalist's taxon name), `own`, `loc` (dauin / ph / world, world = abroad in the Indo-West Pacific), `src` (the original, shown straight from iNaturalist), `r: 1`, plus `st` (iNaturalist Life Stage / Sex annotations: juvenile, adult, female, male; FishBase's life stage, with initial and terminal for its phases; a hint for the phase, and the phase of the photo for the cap until a judging tag says otherwise) and `ni`. Nothing is downloaded and no photo is committed; the photos Pierre adds are downloaded (R2, the four sizes) when they enter the game (admin plan PR 7). Run of 2026-11-04 (`CAP` 20, iNaturalist asked again with the photo dedupe; the 2026-11-03 cache was gone): 8667 photos, every photo once, of 322 species (311 of circle 1, all of them but Pomacentrus armillatus, whose only iNaturalist observations are also P. opisthostigma's, which WoRMS lumps with it; and 11 circle-3 species of circle-1 entries), 20 observations for 315 species; thinnest: Pomacentrus geminospilus 1, P. opisthostigma 2, Pervagor aspricaudus 7, Myripristis amaena 10, Halichoeres binotopsis 13, Lethrinus semicinctus 17; CC BY 6208, CC0 1166, CC BY-NC 1293, of which Pierre's own 512; Dauin 528, the rest of the Philippines 1612, abroad in the Indo-West Pacific 6527 (every circle-1 species is confirmed near Dauin, all but 2 by the IMR list, so their free photos come from the whole region); 2.6 MB (200 KB gzipped). The first run (2026-11-03, `CAP` 30) had 12767 photos, 3.8 MB. **Second run (2026-11-04 night, `--circle 1 --circle 2 --circle 3`)**: the 24 circle-1 species brought into the game by the one presence rule (#496: on the IMR list, so confirmed near Dauin, photos from the whole region) and every species of circles 2 and 3 (91) were searched: 12073 photos of 437 species, 20 observations for 417 of the 442 game species with an iNaturalist taxon; still none for *Chromis agilis*, *Halichoeres chlorocephalus*, *H. marginatus*, *Pomacentrus armillatus* and *Siganus margaritiferus* (no usable Research Grade photo in the Philippines or the Indo-West Pacific under the licence rule); CC BY 8102, CC0 1448, CC BY-NC 2523, Pierre's own 643; Dauin 800, the rest of the Philippines 2214, abroad 9059; 3.6 MB.
- **Curation**: `curation.json` `fish` (admin plan PR 4, 2026-11-04; written only by the admin and published by Pierre's Publish, merged key by key like the rest, `merge3`): the coral key names under one key, `add{f: species id}` (in play for that species), `seen{f: 1}` (rejected), `exclude{f: reason}`, `out{f: species id | 'x'}` (Not this: the photo is that species' candidate, or out of every species; in play for a species when `add` names it and its `out`, if any, names the same, "Fish Candidates" below), `names{name: {family id: 'off' | 'on' | 'often'}}` (Pierre's exceptions to the name index, Q319-Q320: not that family's name, that family's, that family's and gently corrected; read by `core.js` `fishNameIndex`; no admin editor yet, ROADMAP "Fish common names"), `tags{f: jt}`, `ease{f: 1-5}`, `kept{f: set}` (a judging re-judge's proposal he answered Keep mine, by its set: `q308`, the light re-judge; admin Photo review › Light proposals) and `kept{node id: 'cover:<his photo id>><the rule's photo id>'}` (a cover proposal he answered Keep mine, the pair of photo ids, `core.js` `fishPid`; admin Photo review › Cover proposals), both left out of `curation-play.json`, `phase{f}`, `crop{f: [l, t, r, b]}` (the `judging/FISH.md` rubric's fields), `auto{day: {f: code}}` (the fish auto-sort's log: `a` proposed in, `r` set aside, `?` a Check, `-` undone, `@t` the time of Pierre's answer). Coral keys are never touched (`tests/fishcuration.test.js`). **`out` is Pierre's "Not this" only** (Pierre, 2026-11-04: the iNaturalist experts can be wrong, he must be able to say so): written by his click (admin plan PR 5b, before any fish photo reaches the game in PR 7), never by an agent or the auto-sort; it wins over an add, and a photo he marked so is never proposed again (`fishRun` skips every photo he decided; `fetch_fish.py` keeps it known, so never fetches it again). Publish writes `fish` with its maps sorted and empty ones left out (`admin.js` `fishOut`) and counts its changes (photos in play, set aside, not this species, fish auto-sort decisions). `fetch_fish.py` honours `add`, `seen`, `exclude`, `out` and the top-level `exclude` (by iNaturalist photo id) for its room count.
- **Fish auto-sort** (admin plan PR 4; `autosort.js` `fishRun`, `fishCompare`; admin `fishAutoRun`): at each load of the admin by someone who can publish, when `suggest.json` `fish` has judged candidates (only then is `fish-cand.json` fetched), the sure judgments (`fishSure`) go into the draft's `fish` part exactly as Pierre's clicks would write them, one Undo step, sent by his Publish: adds fill each species up to its target (`FISHT`: 8 observations, 12 for the PDF cheatsheet's 24 species, `fish.json` `cs`; at most 20), one observation counting once, the easiest first, then the most reference-like (`core.js` `refScore`), whoever took the photo (Pierre, 2026-11-06; Pierre's own came first before), the rest waiting as candidates; rejects are `seen`. A tag, ease, phase or crop Pierre gave stays his. About 1 candidate in 20 (`autoCheck`) is logged as a Check, not applied (his answer comes with the fish Candidates page, PR 5a). Agreement on keep (`fishCompare`: answered Checks and corrections of applied decisions), through the coral `autoState` (90% over the last 40, after 10): off = nothing applied, Checks go on. **Undo one by one** (rule A1; Codex review #479): the Auto details (Candidates) list the fish auto decisions (the latest 30, then All), each with Undo (`fishAutoUndo`, `autosort.js` `fishUndo`): what the run wrote leaves the draft, and only that: the add while it still names the candidate's species, and the fields its log code lists (`a` + `t` tags, `e` ease, `p` phase, `c` crop: a value Pierre gave before the run was never written, so never listed; any admin action that sets one of these fields later goes through `fishOwn`, which takes the field off the list). Nothing is compared with the pass's judgment, which a new judging pass may replace. The log marks the decision undone (`-`): never applied again, counted in no agreement. Emptied maps stay as `{}` in the draft, so the Publish merge removes these entries only, never what another device published in the same map.
- **Fish Candidates** (admin plan PR 5a, `#fish/photos/candidates`, `admin-fish.js` `renderFishCand`; the pure rules in `autosort.js` `fishQueue`, `fishPlay`, `fishAdd`, `fishReject`, `fishExclude`, tested in `tests/fishcand.test.js`): the coral swiper's twin, one candidate a screen. **One phone screen** (Pierre, 2026-11-06: at 390 × 844 the photo, its species and the decision buttons without scrolling, the demo banner included): the swiper's height is the screen under where the page starts (`--sw-top`, measured in `admin.js` `start` under the header, the tab row and the demo banner), the toggles on one line (a long chip ends in …), the Auto line folded behind its chip (`autoChip`, open or closed remembered in this browser, `reefid-admin-autoline`), the photos in play in one row that swipes sideways; the coral swiper shares all but the tiles. **Queue**: every candidate Pierre has not decided (added, rejected, excluded, Not this; an auto decision he undid comes back), **the thinnest species first** (observations in play over the species' target, `fishTarget`; its photos together), within a species the pass's best photo of an observation, another angle, not judged, a near-duplicate, then the easiest, then a reference photo (flag `r`), whoever took the photo (Pierre, 2026-11-06: his own photos are never forced; they came first before). **Stops at target**: a candidate that would add an observation to a species at its target waits (the chip "N at target" shows them; another photo of an observation in play still comes); a Check never waits. The pass's sure rejects the fish auto-sort did not apply (its keep off) are set aside (chip "N set aside"); "N not judged yet" reminds of the pass. A segment **All | Checks**: the Checks alone, **blind** (no judgment line, no suggested Add, no suggested crop; a Check tag), answered with the same buttons, after which a toast gives the auto-sort's answer. **Card**: the photo (remote, iNaturalist or FishBase; Pierre's crop, else the pass's; "Photo not loading" when its host fails), the species (scientific name, common name, IMR entry), its source (FishBase named), the iNaturalist / FishBase life stage (`st`) and link; above the bar the pass's judgment (`suggest.json` `fish`: keep, the rubric's tags in words, only what makes a photo harder, its phase, unsure fields, note, crop suggested) with its ease as a chip (the difficulty prior); **In play n/target** with the species' photos in play (most of them the auto-sort's proposals) as small tiles, each enlarged with **Out of play** (Pierre's ✕ Reject on it: off the species; a correction when the auto-sort proposed it, so it counts in the agreement). **Buttons**: ✕ Reject (← key; `seen`), Skip (→; this tab only), + Add (1; dashed when the pass would add it): in play for the species with the pass's tags, ease, phase and crop written beside it where Pierre has none (as the auto-sort writes its adds, so every photo in play carries its tags and ease for the game, PR 7), and the × on the photo (`exclude`, for good). Each click one Undo step in `DRAFT.fish`, sent by Publish; never a coral key. **Auto**: the fish auto-sort's line above the card (in, set aside, Checks to answer, keep agreement and whether it is on), folded behind the **Auto** chip at the end of the chips' row as the corals' (below), and its Details below it (disagreements, every decision still standing with Undo, `fishAutoUndo`), here and no longer on the coral Candidates. The species' teaching entry comes from `fish.json` `teach` (`fishLoad` `entry`). **PR 5b, on the same card** (`autosort.js` `fishSet`, `fishTag`, `fishNotThis`, `fishState`, `fishOn`, `fishPhase`): **rows** under the judgment: Ease (5 easy … 1 hard) always, Phase (`judging/FISH.md` words: none, subadult, intermediate, terminal, female, male) only for an entry whose phases differ (`fish.json` `teach` `phase` lines or `dimorph`), and behind **Tags** (folded, remembered in this browser, `reefid-admin-ftags`; unfolded, the swiper keeps its one screen: the photo gives up room down to about a quarter of it and the tag rows scroll inside their own panel, Ease, the other rows and the decision buttons in view (Pierre, 2026-11-06)) the seven rubric tags and the Juvenile and Camouflage flags (the tool's `p` and `r` kept as they were); Pierre's value pressed, the pass's dashed until he confirms or changes it (never shown on a Check); each click writes `tags` (the whole code with that tag changed), `ease` or `phase` (`''` = none, over the pass's) through `fishOwn`, so an Undo of the auto decision leaves his value; the card he is setting stays on screen until he decides, skips or moves it. **Crop**: the coral crop editor (`cropEditor(p, {start, save})`) on `fish.crop` (Full photo over a suggested crop kept as `[0, 0, 1, 1]`). **Not this** (Pierre's click only, never an agent's): a select with the other species of the entry first, then every game species (`fishLoad` `game`: a circle, not out), and No game species; `out {f: species id}` makes the photo that species' candidate (its add and rejection go with it; the card says "from" its first species, which it can go back to), `out {f: 'x'}` takes it out of every species. **A photo is in play** for a species when `add` names it, it is neither rejected nor excluded, and its `out`, if any, names that same species (`fishOn`: the convention the game reads, PR 7;
