A value Pierre already gave (rating, form, crop) is never replaced; a photo he decided (added, rejected, excluded, sent elsewhere) or that is already logged is never touched. **Sure rule** (`autoSure`, from the pass's fields, no `unsure` at all): best of its observation in its view, Fair or Good, view and form given → added to that view (`cellView`, only the views `capViews` allows) with quality, form and suggested crop; `dup`, several corals or Poor → rejected; `exclude` → excluded; `alt`, another genus, `cmp` → Pierre's. A sure judgment the metrics contradict (`metFlag`: Good under 1000 px or among the 5% softest candidates, Poor among the 5% sharpest) is left to him with the reason in the judgment line (3 candidates on 2026-11-03), and is never set aside. **Caps**: adds fill each genus × view up to `capOf` colonies, by source priority first (rule P1, `oIdx`: Pierre's own before other observers, so his Fair photo takes the last slot ahead of another observer's Good one; Codex review #400), then Good first, then the sharper (`metrics.json`), the rest waits in the full cell as candidates; decided again at each load. **Checks** (`autoCheck`: FNV-1a of the name, 1 in `AUTO.every` = 20, the same on every device): a sure candidate drawn is logged `?…` and not applied; it comes up in the swiper with a **Check** tag, blind (no judgment line, dashed guesses, suggested view or crop; its Crop editor starts from Pierre's own crop or the full photo, and saving the full photo keeps an explicit no-crop so the add does not write the suggestion), even in a full cell or set aside, drawn with weight 1; after his answer a toast gives the agent's. **Agreement** (`autoCompare`, `autoState`): per field (keep, view, quality, form), each answered Check counts (agree or not), and every later correction of an applied or undone decision counts as a disagreement (a decision left alone counts nothing); view, quality and form only when both added the photo, quality only when Pierre rated it. One comparison set per photo, from its latest log entry (`autoIdx`): two devices' drafts that logged it on different days are both kept by `merge3`, and are not counted twice. "Not this genus" on a photo counts as not kept even when Pierre then adds it in the genus it went to (the auto only decides a candidate in its own genus; Codex review #400). **Remove** on the genus page of a photo the auto-sort added also marks it rejected (`seen['cand:f']`, as ✕ Reject: it stays with the genus page's candidates, off the Candidates queue; one Undo step), so it counts as a Keep disagreement; Remove of any other photo is unchanged (Codex review #400). The comparisons are ordered by **when Pierre answered or corrected**, not by when the auto decided: each click that changes his answer on a logged photo writes the time into its code (`@t`, Unix seconds in base 36; `saveDraft` → `autoWhen` → `autosort.js` `autoSigs`, `autoStamp`), so a Check of an older run he answers today is among the latest comparisons; entries without a time (logged before the times were added) keep the log's order (day, photo), before every timed one; the Publish summary counts decisions, not these times. Over the last `AUTO.win` = 40 comparisons, after `AUTO.n0` = 10: a field under `AUTO.min` (keep 0.90, view 0.85, quality 0.73, form 0.80; reasons in `autosort.js`) is off: keep off = nothing applied; view off = no add; quality or form off = added with that field left as the dashed guess (logged `.`). Checks go on while a field is off, so it comes back on when it recovers. **Visible and undoable**: an "auto" tag next to the source of each photo it added (`srcTag`); the Auto line above the card (added, set aside, Checks left, agreement and n per field, off in red; folded behind the **Auto** chip at the end of the chips' row, marked "off" when a field is, so the photo and the buttons fit one phone screen: Pierre, 2026-11-06), and Details below it: the table (agreement, n, threshold, on/off, Pierre's own retest agreement), the disagreements (both answers, Check or correction, the agent's note while `suggest.json` has it, the metrics: sharpness percentile among candidates, px, cast), every auto decision, newest first (the latest day, and within a day the latest run: a second load the same day used to list that day's first 30 decisions, none of its own), each with **Undo** (`autoUndo`: removes what it wrote unless Pierre changed it since, marks it `-` so it is never applied again, the photo is a candidate again, one Undo step). An auto exclusion keeps its candidate entry through the candidate refill (`tools/excluded.py` `drop_excluded`), so its Undo brings the candidate back too; if the entry is gone anyway (not in the loaded candidate files), the toast says it cannot come back to Candidates instead of claiming it is back. The whole run at load is one Undo step. **Retest** (test-retest, the ceiling the agent is compared with): once every `AUTO.retest` = 25 candidates decided in the swiper in a tab, a photo Pierre rated himself (not by the auto), never retested (fixed order per photo), comes back blind with a **Retest** tag: quality, and growth form when he gave one; Done writes `retest` only. On 2026-11-03 (1622 judged candidates waiting, all sources): 614 added (168 Good, 446 Fair), 396 rejected, 25 excluded, 65 Checks (42 adds, 23 rejects), 193 sure adds waiting in full cells, 3 stopped by the metrics.
- Performance: `photos`/`own`/`cands` walk every genus and are memoized per draft state, kept from page to page: `render()` clears them only when the draft differs from the one they were computed for (`memoFresh`), and photos or candidates sent to another genus are indexed once per state (`sentTo`); code that changes `DRAFT` and then reads them without `saveDraft()` or `render()` must call `memoReset()`. Replays of the whole answer log (`playSplit`, `levelMargin`, `confusionOf`) run once per log loaded (`ofAns`). **Sheet reads** (2026-11-04, Pierre: admin pages slow on his phone): every read of the sync script goes through `sheetRead(kind, go)`, so a page drawn again while a read is on its way, or another page, reuses it instead of asking again (kinds `ans`, `stats`, `users`, `pages`, `events`, `reports`); ↻ and the account actions call `sheetAgain(...kinds)` (forget, drop the read on its way, its late reply ignored), a forced reload `sheetDrop`. The landing page's reads leave as soon as the token is known (`sheetEarly`, by address: Players the player list and the answer log, a Journey also the pages opened, To do the log, events and reports, …), in parallel with the GitHub token check and `start()`'s data files, which they do not need; a refused token drops them. Health and the Journey ask for the pages opened together with the answer log. `status()` computes what it reads from the draft (changes, suggestions, cells over the cap) once per draft and published file, To do's Health line once per log, photo ratings, draft and "Without me" choice (both in `MEMO`). `tests/admin.smoke.js` checks the reads through the demo's call log (`admin-demo.js` `DEMO_CALLS`). Nothing read from the sheet is stored on the device (Pierre, 2026-11-04). The tiles of Quality, Growth form and By difficulty use `content-visibility: auto` (thousands of tiles). Measured on 2026-11-03 (ROADMAP "Done recently").
- **Enlarged photo** (lightbox: `zoom()` steps through a genus's photos (through `zoomSet()`, which To do › Points uses for a point's photos) with ‹ › from the genus pages, Photo review, Photo stats, Health, Errors and Hidden; `zoomOne()` shows one photo from Excluded, To do's flags, Candidates and the auto-sort; both captions come from `lbCap()`): genus (a link to its sheet, `./?draft#g-<Genus>`, new tab) · view or Candidate / Not this genus · the photo's credit · its source · its file name. An iNaturalist photo (`o` an `inaturalist.org/observations/` URL) shows its iNat taxon and author and an **iNat observation ↗** link to `o` (new tab, `noopener`), as the games' `photoSrc()` (rule P5; Pierre, 2026-11-03: he opened his own photo that everyone misses to check its identification on iNaturalist and found no link); an IMR lecture photo shows its slide, with no link (no public page). `tests/admin.smoke.js` checks the link on Health's photo, both lightboxes and a candidate.
- **Points to handle** (Pierre, 2026-11-05: he settles most open points himself and never reads a text list): Inbox › To do › Points `#inbox/todo/points` (`renderPoints`) lists the points of **`points.json`** (repo root, written by agents, never by the admin: `kinds` {key: label} and `points` [{`id` stable, `kind` trainer / names / sources / content, `subj` coral / fish, `at` date added, `title` the list's line, `q` the question in plain words, `ctx` context lines, `ask` the answer field under each photo, `photos` [{`f` a photo id of `data.json` / `cand.json`, with `t`, `a`, `o`, `src` as a fallback copy} or {`none`: why a slot is empty}], `src` [{`t`, `u`}]}]; the file's order is the priority; neutral wording, rule C1; its shape checked by `points.js` `pointsProblems`, `tests/points.test.js`, and a point with a problem left out of the admin). Loaded once at start (`points.json?v=1`, stamped at deploy). Pure logic in **`points.js`** (admin only: `PT_STATUS`, `pointsOrder`, `pointAge`, `photoKey`). The list: Open first, Stand-by below (muted), Resolved and Dismissed folded with their counts (the Kept style); each row title, kind (Fish for a fish point), age; the Points view carries the open count. A tap opens the point's page `#inbox/todo/points/<id>` (`renderPoint`; the address only in `POINT_R` / `pointHash`), made to show a trainer from a phone: the question big, the photos large (one column on a phone) and numbered, **blind** (no name or origin until the bar's **Names**; the enlarged photo, `zoomSet()`, steps through the point's photos with the number and credit only, the full `lbCap()` once Names is on), a text field under each photo for the answer (`a`), **Notes** (`n`), and Resolved / Stand-by / Dismiss, plus Reopen once handled, one click (toast, Undo); Context (folded) holds `ctx` and the sources. Notes and answers reach the draft 0.8 s after typing stops, when the field is left, and before any redraw (`ptFlush`). Everything goes through the draft and Publish (`curation.json` `points`), never written by a script (rule A1). Corals side only (one list for both subjects).
- Genus status: `needs()` lists what is missing (3 featured per view, non-macro cover…); ✓ Validated is Pierre's button.
- Needs (PR #198): per genus × view, why the game pool falls short (few colonies, few good ones, too easy for players' level, repeated photos, mix-ups), ranked.
- **Coverage › Games** `#inbox/coverage/games` (was Game coverage; `renderCov`, `covOf`, `gamePool`; Where to look above the grid, `lookBanner()`, the same ranking as the full list, `needsOf()`): game-usable photos per genus × view (field + IMR lecture photos; no book, multi-coral, hidden or sheet-only photos; Poor ones count), coloured by distinct colonies (0 / 1-2 / 3-4 / 5-7 / 8+), with an "excl." line and tooltip listing what was left out and why. `covOf` is shared with the Candidates swiper. **Cap per genus × view** (Pierre, 2026-11-01: "10 good representative photos per genus × zoom would already be great", then 20 the same day: "the goal is to learn the genus, not the photos"; 40 since 2026-11-06, Q187, after the photo exposure study): `CELL_MAX` = 40 colonies in the games (the Auto pass fills a view up to it). A Key-only genus's cap is `CAP.sheet` = 6 colonies (Pierre, 2026-11-03: the most its sheet and the Coral ID Key show of a view), in its Colony and Close-up views (`capOf`, `capViews`). Game coverage's rows are the game genera (`gameOf`, Millepora and the bonus genera included, tagged "not IMR"; until 2026-11-03 a `!DATA.gen[n].x` filter left them out, as on Where to look, the over-cap warning and Health's "Genera that don't take off"). Full cells get a dark outline here (orange over the cap), a "N full" count and, over the cap, a "⚠ N over the cap" count, also on the Games and Sheets & ID Key views, as ⚠ on the Coverage tab and on the To do group (`OVER`, `overCap()`; Pierre, 2026-11-02: "at least a warning"); a Key-only genus's cells over its cap are counted on the Sheets & ID Key view and outlined there. The swiper's full test (`cellFull`, `candQueue`) uses each genus's own cap. In the swiper, `candQueue` marks a candidate `full` when its guessed view (`cellView`: `vg`, else its view) has `CELL_MAX` colonies and its colony is not one of them; full ones leave the queue (a line counts them, "Show them" brings them in for the tab, `CFULL`) and come back when the cell drops below. Adding to a full view (`cellFull`) opens `renderRepl()`: one tile per colony of the cell, in `retireOrder()` (Pierre, 2026-11-02, semi-automatic for the most variety): the suggested colony first, outlined, with its reasons (Poor quality; a difficulty the cell already has, within 0.5 of another rated colony; many answers, so little left to learn from it; on a tie, an observer with many colonies in the cell), colonies with fewer than 3 answers held back (still being rated), and last the ones to keep, never suggested (a photo among the first 6 of the view, i.e. the sheet's references; IMR lecture or Pierre's own photos; the only colony of a growth form); a click sets that colony's photos of the view to sheet only (`use: 'ref'`, still on the genus sheet) and adds the candidate, in one Undo step; "Add anyway" goes over the cap. Nothing is ever removed automatically. `tests/admin.smoke.js` checks the queue and the replacement.
- **Coverage › Sheets & ID Key** `#inbox/coverage/sheets-id-key` (was Sheet & ID Key coverage; `renderKcov`): what sheets and the Coral ID Key can draw from, IMR genera plus the extra Dauin genera; every photo not hidden or multi-coral; colour = distinct colonies with a non-Poor photo; plus growth forms and species seen.
- Both Coverage views sort Thinnest / Thickest / A-Z, or by a view column (click again to reverse).
- **Recent sessions and who is playing now** (Pierre, 2026-11-03): a card at the top of Players › Activity › Recent sessions `#players/activity` (`recentSessions()`), the last `RECENT.N` = 10 sessions of every player, newest first, after the player filter All / Others / Me: player (by name, linked to their Journey, when signed in; else the anonymous id, linked to `#players/list/anon/<id>`, as on Players), Last (as Players' Last column; the row tinted and "N playing now" in the card title when the last answer is under `RECENT.NOW` = 5 minutes old), answers, right %, length (first to last answer), start time, and modes with counts: quiz (look-alike duels slipped into it included, as on Players), custom (the custom quiz, `quiz|s…`, its typed answers included), duel (the Duel tab, `quiz-duel…`), typed (a `|t` mode, the typed answers once they are in the quiz; ROADMAP "Free-text answers"); "as of" = when the answer log was read. A session has **one definition**, `sessionsOf()` in `admin.js` (one player's answers cut where two are `HEALTH.GAP` = 30 minutes apart or more), used by Stops, the Journey and Recent sessions (the Journey cut at "more than" 30 minutes until then). The names come from the player list (`loadUsers`, read with the answers when Activity opens). **↻** next to the title reads the answer log and the player list again (the whole Answers tab, `readAnswers`, not cached by the script: several hundred kB and a few seconds of script time once the RAs have played). **Live** (Pierre, 2026-11-08: "Les deux", the two parts of the light-read proposal): while Activity is open and in view, every `LIVE_READ.MS` = 60 s, the sheet's light read (`admin.js` `liveArm`, `liveRead`; `Code.gs` `live_`, `POST {readLive: 1, token}`, sheet v110, admin token only, no script lock, nothing written) brings the Answers tab's last `LIVE_N` = 50 rows with a player, as `readAnswers` serves them (`answerOut_`, a surprise card's left out), each player's last answer time (`last`, by admin id, from the Activity tab's QUERY while it holds the script's formula, else from the Name, Time and Mode columns; a deleted account's and an archive's left out) and the tab's row count (`n`, the window `k`); `liveMerge` (pure, between `// livemerge: begin` / `end`, tested in `tests/live.test.js`) appends the rows not held yet (known by time, unit shown, answered, mode and photos, not by player id) and the page is drawn again ("as of" the read); when the rows held can no longer be completed from it (a row held under another player id: answers claimed at sign-in, a rename or a merge; no row of the window held while the tab has more rows than it; a player's last answer more than a second after the last held; an account that left normal operation since the log was read, the reply's `roster`, `Code.gs` `rosterAt_`, later than the log's own; a player of the rows held with no last answer any more: rule D7, a deleted account's answers never stay on the page; Codex review #775), the whole log and the player list are read again in the background (`loadStats`, `loadUsers`, the page kept on screen; a read that fails keeps the log held and the timer runs on), and a signed-in player of the log missing from the player list reads it again (`liveUsers`: at each light read until the list holds them; a failed read of the list, after a full read too, keeps the last good one and is tried again at each light read until one succeeds, `liveUsersDue`); an unfolded session's saved copy follows its player's new answers (`savedClear`, as ↻). Hidden (another tab, a phone asleep) or another page: no read, the timer stopped (`visibilitychange`); back in view, a read at once when the last is over a minute old. Before sheet `LIVE_SHEET` (an older script answers `input`): no timer, ↻ by hand (rule D4). Script cost: one light read a minute while Pierre has the page open, reading two small ranges (the 50 rows and the Activity tab's one row per player; the token check kept 10 minutes, `isAdmin_`), so by our estimate (not measured on the live sheet) well under a second of script time each, and no wait on the script lock for anyone. **How fresh "now" is** (`sync.js`): a signed-in player's answer goes with their progress about 3 seconds after it is given (`syncSoon`: `save()` after each answer, one timer restarted at each change, then a load and a send, `syncOnce`), so it is in the sheet within about 10 seconds; a device not signed in, the same since 2026-11-08 (Pierre: "Les deux"): its answers queued (`ANONQ`) go `ANON_SEND.MS` = 3 seconds after each answer (`anonSoon`, one timer restarted at each answer, `sendAnon`, at most 100 per request; until then when 10 were waiting or 20 seconds after the last answer), when the app is hidden or closed, or when back online; the sheet's caps are as before (`anon_`: 600 answers per device and 6000 for every device not signed in every 6 hours, `SECURITY.md`). About ten times more anonymous requests than before (one per answer instead of one per 10): each holds the script lock for its own write (`anon_`: the Id column read to skip resent answers, the rows appended), as each signed-in save already does once per answer, so, by our estimate (not measured on the live sheet), the lock's other waiters (saves, events, reports; `LOCK_MS` = 20 s) wait a fraction of a second more per anonymous answer, and the Apps Script quotas that apply (30 simultaneous executions; web app requests count in no daily trigger quota) stay far below their limits at a class's pace. Times are the device's own clock when it answered (`a.t`): answers played offline arrive later with their real times, a phone set to the wrong time shows wrong. The demo (`admin-demo.js` `live()`) has Carla playing now (24 answers over the last 15 minutes, 2 typed, 8 in the custom quiz) and a device not signed in that stopped 12 minutes ago, every other demo answer at least 2 hours old; `tests/admin.smoke.js` checks the Last column (sort, "now", one tap), the card (10 rows, newest first, Carla playing now with quiz, custom 8, duel and typed, the device 12 minutes ago by its id) and ↻.
- **A session unfolded** (Pierre, 2026-11-05: "what the player made of the session"): a tap on a row of Recent sessions (or Enter on it) unfolds its summary in a row under it, on the same page, a second tap folds it (`SOPEN` in `admin.js`, by player id and first answer time, so it stays open across ↻ and redraws; several can be open; the player's link still opens the Journey). Drawn by `sessionSummary()` in `admin-journey.js` from `sessionFacts()` (pure, between `// sessfacts: begin` / `end`, tested in `tests/session.test.js`; thresholds in `SESS`): a sentence first (answers, length, right against the expected, genera met for the first time, Open all, genera learned, the most progress, the most misses and what they were taken for, the misses in a row at the end, achievements), then the Journey's KPI tiles (start → end, length, answers, right with the expected share, progress % before → after) and its chart (`journeyChart`, with `opt.w` / `opt.h` for its smaller box and `opt.min` for its scale: right answers over the last `SESS.WIN` = 10 of the session against the expected, on a scale from `SESS.PAD` = 5 points under the session's lowest point, rounded down to 10, up to 100% (Pierre, 2026-11-05: 0-100% squashed it), a dot at each new genus's first question, ★ at a genus learned; under 5 answers, the answers as dots), then **New genera** (first asked in the session; "card": its card showed in it, not asked yet), **Events** (session k of N and the time since the last, Open all or the offer declined, the back card's choice, the first-launch question and its fallback cards as the Journey's Cards list words them (`jStart`), genera learned (the Journey's rule: the last right `|x` question of a genus learned now), mastery exam questions, typed answers, `SESS.RUN` = 10 right or more in a row, ending on `SESS.END` = 3 misses or more), **Most improved** (the genera whose bar rose `SESS.GAIN` = 2 points or more, at most 3: the player's answers replayed with today's rules up to the session's first answer, then through it, `sessProg`, `core.js` `replayProgress` with `start`, as `progOf` replays: the sheet's photo memory and confusions, the saved copy's reset and first-launch answer; a genus learned now counts as learned from the time its exam was passed), **Difficulties** (genera missed `SESS.MISS` = 2 times or more, pairs shown → answered 2 times or more, the photos of the questions missed, hardest first by the photo Elo, as Health's tiles: tap to enlarge; each says "recognised by N%", the share of starting players (level 0, Elo 1000) who recognise it without guessing, 1 / (1 + e^d) of its photo Elo d, whatever the question's format (`admin.js` `recognised`, `recPct`: one helper for every admin page that shows a photo's difficulty; Pierre, 2026-11-05, in place of the signed Elo, which read as good when positive), and a photo everyone misses by Health's rule (counted on players), `healthBad`, adds "hard for everyone"; both in neutral text: the colours of difficulty come with the admin-wide difficulty display, a later PR), **Achievements** (`reefid-sync-ach` times within `SESS.EDGE` = 5 minutes of the session's answers; `savedOf` now keeps that key) and **Journey →** (`#players/list/anon/<id>` for a device never signed in, which has no saved copy: no cards, achievements or learned genera). A signed-in player's saved copy is read when their session is unfolded (one `userData`; the page draws again when it is in, "…" until then), and read again by ↻ (`savedClear`, as on Players and the Journey: Codex review #557). The panel is held at the left of the table, as wide as the visible part of its scroll box (`--sw`, set by `bindStats`), as a Players row's actions (`.adm-sess`, `.adm-urow` / `.adm-udet` shared with Players); phones and tablets stack its parts in one column, desktops (from 1024 px) put the chart and Journey → on the left and the lists beside them, with smaller photo tiles (Pierre, 2026-11-05: several sessions open one under another). The demo gives each made-up player achievements dated at the answers that earned them, and Carla 25% progress in the session she is playing now, where the photos of the questions she missed are ones everyone misses (enough earlier misses of the devices not signed in, several distinct players, to pass Health's rule, `everyoneMisses`, taken from their own answers, never the last 8 answers of the device whose recent session Activity's checks read); `tests/admin.smoke.js` unfolds and folds her session at 390 and 1280 px (tap and Enter).
- **Activity over time and play hours** (Pierre, 2026-11-04: Players › Activity = "who plays and how much"): a card under Recent sessions (`activityCard`): answers, active players and new players (whose first answer falls in the period) per day, ISO week (from Monday) or month (the switch, `SF.per`; gaps filled, the last 60 periods; `activityOver`), then the answers by hour of the day (`playHours`, local time). Tested in `tests/activity.test.js` and `tests/admin.smoke.js`.
- **Health** `#inbox/todo/health` and **Stops** `#analyses/learning/stops`, on each subject's side (the fish's from 2026-11-08: its answers, `rowsOf`; its units by name, `repUnit`; its sheets opened from its sheet page, `fish.learn.genus` for a family, genus or species sheet alike since `core.js` `pageName` logs every sheet in the corals' words, `sheetVisit`, the corals' from the summary's genera as before) (`statsHealth()` draws both, `healthBad()` and `healthGenera()` shared with the red line of To do › All, thresholds in `HEALTH`; Pierre, 2026-11-02: most RAs will not send feedback, so the admin reads what the answers already logged say): no new data, anonymous ids only, counts. One role per admin page (Pierre, 2026-11-04): Health (To do: is something wrong?) keeps what needs action, Stops (Analyses › Learning: what we learn) the rest. **Health** (on each subject's side: the fish's from 2026-11-08, its photos from `fishAdmin` with every tool of their family's page, `fishRepTile`, as the corals' with their genus page's, `coralRepTile` (Q309, 2026-11-09; the To do tiles' grid, one a row on a phone), counted on their answers alone since the sheet rates the corals' photos only, and its slate's units with the scientific genera its answers met (`g:Chromis`, each in its own row; the slate holds families only), `healthBad`, `healthGenera`, `healthUnits` with the subject; To do › All's red line stays the corals'): **Photos everyone misses**, counted on players, not answers (Pierre, 2026-11-05: one player missing the same photo six times, or a new photo one player answered, is not "everyone"): answered by `HEALTH.PL_MIN` = 3 distinct players or more, `PL_MISS` = 2 or more of whom, and a majority, miss it (under `PL_LOW` = 50% of their own answers on it right), and either shown in `PH_MIN` = 5 answers or more with under `PH_LOW` = 40% right (the minimum kept on purpose: on the real log of 2026-11-05, photos answered once by each of 3 players, 2 wrong, came in without it, which happens by chance at the game's ~80% target) or a photo Elo at least `PH_FAR` = 1 above the median of its genus's rated photos (with `PH_MIN` = 5 rated answers): maybe mislabelled or bad; each tile gives right %, answers and the players who miss it out of those who answered it; thumbnail opens the lightbox (`data-zf`), the genus name its page. One rule for Health, To do's Health line, the session summary's "hard for everyone" and the demo data built for it: `admin.js` `photoAnswers`, `missedBy`, `manyMiss`, `everyoneMisses` (between `// everyone: begin` / `end`, tested in `tests/health.test.js`), used by `healthBad`; **Genera that don't take off** (per slate genus: players who met it, median of their right rates on it, Stuck = players with ≥10 answers on it under 50% over their last 10). **Stops**: players, sessions, sessions per player and answers per session; **Where players stop** (a session = one player's answers less than 30 min apart, `sessionsOf()`, one whose last answer is more recent is still going and not counted as ended; answers per session 1-5 / 6-10 / 11-20 / 21-50 / 51+, sessions per player, how sessions ended (misses in a row on the last answers, sessions of ≤10 answers against longer ones: frustration shows as short sessions ending on misses), the genus and game of the last question of the short ones); **Genus sheets opened** (below, Pages opened). The answers and players per day that Health showed for 30 days are Players › Activity's Activity over time (day view). Works in `?demo` (the made-up players play in sessions of 5-40 answers about 30 seconds apart; the devices not signed in answer at random hours, mostly one answer a session). `tests/admin.smoke.js` checks both pages' sections and no sideways page scroll at 390 and 1280 px.
- **Visits** `#players/visits`, Players › Visits (Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q301-Q304, on the study of activity outside the quiz; `admin-visits.js`): one scrolling page from the Pages rows (`loadPages`, the pages-opened summaries of sheet v30; `pagesRows` keeps the Browser column, `ua`) and the Events `load` rows (`loadEvents`, the last 3000 events), joined to the answer log (the ids with answers, any subject) and the player list (names); `sheetEarly` reads all four. `visitsOf` (pure, tested in `tests/visits.test.js`) groups the summaries into visits (one visit = one visitor id, or the device for a summary with no id, and one visit id; its parts added up; its time its first summary's), every subject's (Pierre, 2026-11-09: one page shared by both subjects, as the list of players; before, the subject on screen's pages only and a visit in both counted on each side): its pages under their logged names (`core.js` `pageSubj`: a page drawn with Fish on screen is `fish.<page>`), **marked with the subjects whose pages it opened** (`subj`: Corals, Fish or both, counted once) and **as each subject sees it** (`on`: its pages, its time, a game of its, and the genus sheets for the replayed subject only: they are data.json's genera, `sync.js` `pvGenus`); **Subject** (`visitSubj`): All (the default, every visit whole) or one subject, its visits only, each counted with that subject's pages (as the page per subject was), still marked with every subject; **a game** = a Practice page (quiz, custom quiz, duels, Memory; Q302: the Lab is not one, `visitGame`); **who**: Signed in or Not signed in with answers, Never answered (no answer in any subject, signed in or not); **the load row** of a visit, the device's latest one sent within its first part and never after its first summary (`VISITS.NEAR`, `VISITS.SLACK`: `sync.js` sends the load row first; Codex review #777), each joined once, gives the first page (`p0`) and installed (`st`; else the device's habit); a genus sheet keeps how each summary reached it (per source counts, the card's "From" the most frequent); an Events read that failed is said above the numbers; **the device** (`devClass`, Q304): a phone from the Browser column ending in "mobile", a tablet estimated from the median window width `w` of the device's load rows (a mobile browser `VISITS.PHONE` = 600 px or more, a computer's browser from 600 to under `VISITS.DESK` = 1024 px: an iPad's Safari presents itself as a Mac), else a computer; approximate, and only for devices still in the Events read (said in the help). Filters on one row (`VF`, kept as `reefid-admin-visits`): Subject All (default) \| Corals \| Fish (each with its switch icon), Period 7 d \| 30 d (default) \| All, Visits **Without a game** (default, Q303) \| All visits, Who Everyone \| Signed in \| Not signed in \| Never answered, Device All \| Phone \| Tablet \| Computer (`visitsPick`). Then five numbers (`visitKpis`: visits, "of N" in all when without a game, visitors, never answered, median visit, on a phone) and the cards: **Visits over time** (Day \| Week from Monday, `visitPeriods`, the last 60; visits, visitors, and the hour of the day; on All visits the visits with a game stacked in a pale tint above those without), **Pages opened** (grouped Home, Learn, Identify, Practice, Progress, Other, a page of each subject its own row in its subject's words, `vPage`, marked with its subject's icon on All, `vLab`, `vMark`: the Coral ID Key and the Fish ID Key apart; visits, share, visitors, median time in a visit that opened it, total), an **ID Key** card per subject (every subject with visits on All, the one filtered otherwise: visits, visitors, median time, first page, by who; "Steps inside the Key: later", ROADMAP C3), **Genus sheets** (the replayed subject, on All or on it alone: the 8 most opened, visitors, time, reached from), **First page** and **Device** (class × installed app \| browser tab \| not known), **Visitors** (newest first, each row and each unfolded visit marked with its subjects, `vMarks`; the 12 most recent then Show all; a tap unfolds their last 8 visits, `VOPEN`; the name or anonymous id opens their Journey on the subject filtered, on All the one they spent most time on, the link naming it, `data-own`: `visitorSubj`, Codex review #836). The demo (`admin-demo.js`) adds Ben and Carla coming back to read, two devices not signed in opening the Key, and 12 devices that never answered (staff on computers, an iPad, an Android tablet, two on the Fish side), each with a load row; `tests/admin.smoke.js` checks every card, the numbers, the filters (Subject among them), a visitor unfolded, Show all, the shared page (the switch inert, both subjects' visits and marks, `#fish/players/visits` landing on `#players/visits`) and the help, at 390 and 1280 px.
- **Confusions** `#analyses/difficulty/pairs`, Analyses › Difficulty › Pairs (`statsConf()`, 2026-11-01; under Players › Activity until 2026-11-04): pairs of genera with offered together, mixed up, rate, direction (A→B, B→A) and the same score the quiz uses for traps (prior shown as Look-alike / Family / Other; the app's slightly higher start for a shared growth form is not shown); a pair no source declares that qualifies as a look-alike from the data (`core.js` `dataLook`: `CONF.N` 20 offers or more, score `CONF.TRAP` or more; 2026-11-04) says "look-alike from the data" in its Starts as column, counted in a KPI: the duels, the Again and the genus sheets treat it as declared; then the photos, colonies (iNat observation `o`) and species (`t`) most often answered wrongly, with what they were taken for. Counts come from `confusionOf()` (`core.js`) over `?answers=1` rows; answers logged before v10 (no choices) only appear under "Before v10".
- **Difficulty in words** (Pierre, 2026-11-05, Q19 and Q26: the signed raw numbers, "+1.44", read as good): one helper and one colour scale for the whole admin, `admin.js` between `// recognised: begin` and `end` (tested in `tests/rec.test.js`). A **photo** reads as **recognised by N%** (`recBadge`, `recPct`): the share of starting players (level 0, Elo 1000) who recognise it without guessing, σ(−d) = 1 / (1 + e^d) for its photo Elo d (the format-free part of the model, p = 1/k + (1 − 1/k)·σ(θ − d): the guess floor 1/k depends on the mode and is left out on purpose; d = 0 → 50%). A **genus** (any grouping) reads the same for a typical photo of it, its difficulty offset (`?stats=1` `gd`, sheet v31, kept in `GD`; the median of its photos when the sheet serves none). Five bands on the whole % shown (`REC`, `recBand`): ≥75 very easy, 60-75 easy, 40-60 middle, 25-40 hard, <25 very hard, as a pill (`.adm-rec.rec-<band>`, tokens `--rec-*`, docs/DESIGN.md), "19%" alone where a column, a card or a label beside it says what it is; the raw value in its title (a tap shows the title in the toast on a touch screen, `$v` click listener). A **player** reads as their **Elo** (`eloText`: 1000 + 200 × level, ± the 95% margin of `levelMargin` where it exists), the level in the title. Charts on the difficulty axis keep it (easier on the left) with % labels at its ends and the bands as a coloured line under it (`recScale`). "vs expected" (points against the game's expectation) keeps its sign. The method is in the admin "?" of every page that shows a reading (`REC_HELP`, `REC_PAGES`). Where: Photo review › By difficulty (each tile), Photo stats › Difficulty (KPIs: median photo, middle half, hardest genus; the distribution's axis and bins; Views and Sources "Recognised"; each genus and its dots and lanes), Health and a session's summary (photos everyone misses: "recognised by N%", and "harder than its genus" when its photo Elo is `HEALTH.PH_FAR` above its genus median), Players (Elo, Not signed in: Elo), a Journey (Elo), Analyses › Learning › Answer log (Genera: the median player Elo on each), Coverage's Where to look ("too easy": players' Elo against the hardest photo's reading, a badge in the full list's chip, words in the banner's titles), Candidates (the colony to retire: "recognised by N%, like another colony", a badge in the suggestion, words with the raw value in the tile's title), the one-tap tables' level bands (Elo below 900, 900-1100, above 1100). The one-tap tables' "Difficulty" column keeps its sign: it is the players' own felt difficulty (−1 too easy to +1 too hard), not a model difficulty. `tests/admin.smoke.js` checks every view of every tab, Coverage's full list and the demo journeys: no signed raw number in the text (seconds, points and that felt difficulty aside), every badge in the band of its %, no "recognised by N%" as plain text outside a badge; a new view inherits the check. The admin follows the app's ⚙ Colour-blind (`reefid-sync-cvd`, read at start like the theme).
- **Admin tables never scroll sideways for one long cell** (Pierre, 2026-11-03, a phone screenshot of Players): every `.adm-tbl` cell stops at `--cut` (9 em on phones, 16 em from tablets; `admin.css`) and ends in "…", its full text in the title (set on hover by one listener on `#view`, or written by `cut()` / `pname()` for names); player names are capped by `.adm-cut` itself (an inline block, which every browser honours), genus links are never cut (known length), error messages wrap within a wider cap (13 / 36 em). Tables with many columns still scroll, column by column.
- **Every account counts** (Pierre, 2026-11-04: his account is a real player's): no page leaves anyone's answers out (`playerRows` = the whole log, `rowsOf` a subject's, `photoStats` = the sheet's `?stats=1`); the "Without me", "All / Others / Me" and "This is also me" switches of before are gone, and the leaderboard's "hidden" (Board −1) changes only the public leaderboard. "you" marks the account this browser is signed in with (`isMe`, `MEID` = the anonymous id the sheet derives from the signed-in sync name), a mark only. `tests/everyone.test.js`.

### Admin for every subject (plan, 2026-11-04)
Pierre approved (2026-11-04) one pooled admin for corals and fish, reached in 12 small PRs that each keep the coral admin working (checked by `tests/admin.smoke.js`); the plan, its order and Pierre's decisions are in docs/ROADMAP.md "Admin for every subject". The model: **subject → group → unit → (sub-unit) → section → observation → photo**, with **facets** (tags) on each photo, **origins**, a **target** and **states** (candidate → in play → hidden / excluded / rejected / not this). Each subject describes itself (an admin part of `core.js` `SUBJECTS`: its words, groups, sections, facets, origins, target, data and curation keys); the engine never names a subject. Corals: units = genera, sections = views (Colony / Close-up / Corallites). Fish: units = the book's families (the units plan of 2026-11-06), each with its genera, sections = **species** (curation at the species level from day one), one whole-fish view, and what varies is a facet: phase, framing, quality, colour loss, several species, licence. One `curation.json` and one Publish: fish choices under a `fish` key with the coral key names inside; coral keys stay where they are (rule A1). One **Corals | Fish** switch in the header for everything (To do included), each side showing its own to-do count; coral addresses unchanged, fish pages under `#fish/`. Done so far: PR 1, the fish candidate pipeline ("Fish candidates" above). **One filter bar** (PR 3): every photo page draws its filters with one component in `admin.js`, `FB` (`origins`: the origin chips, with counts and short names on the genus bar or as a Show row; `views`: the view filter, a segmented control on the genus bar or chips on Photo review; `unit`, `check`: Photo review's unit select and check boxes; `sources`: the Candidates sources), filled from the subject on screen (`SUBJECTS` `views`, `one` / `many`, `admin` `origins` and `csrc`), and wires them with `bindBar` (one state per filter, shared by every page as before: `SHOW` in `reefid-admin-origins`, `GVIEW`, Photo review's `QF` / `FF`, `CSRC`); the coral pages' DOM is the same as before it, step by step (genus page, view filter, origin chips, Quality, Growth form, Candidates sources, Hidden, Excluded, at 390 and 1280 px). In review: PR 5a, the fish Candidates swiper, and PR 5b, its tags, crop and Not this ("Fish Candidates" above).

**The generic tree** (`taxtree.js`, 2026-11-13; Pierre: "un système plus générique qui peut fonctionner quel que soit le nombre de niveaux"; docs/ROADMAP.md "The Tree tab"): pure functions with no DOM and no subject (rule S1), loaded by `admin.html`, tested in `tests/taxtree.test.js`. A classification is a list of nodes `{id, name, rank (any word, or none), up (its parent's id in that classification, none for a root), …}`; one parent per node and per classification (the key, later WoRMS: each its own tree), groups (IMR entries, circles, look-alikes) are tags read as filters, never extra parents. `ttBuild` builds the children index once (siblings in the order given) and reports what is wrong (a duplicate id left out, a parent not found or a cycle: the node becomes a root), never fixing it silently; `ttPath`, `ttUnder`, `ttLeaves` (the leaves: the subject's playable rank), `ttRoll` (a sum over the leaves at every node, once), `ttFirst`, `ttNearest` (the nearest node on the way up a test accepts: a node page's unit); `ttShown` (a filter: the nodes a tag accepts, what leads to them and what is under them), `ttRows` (the rows on screen from what is open and shown, with the ARIA level, position and set size) and `ttKey` (the WAI-ARIA tree keys). `ttFromRef` builds the nodes of a classification from a reference table (a parent-pointer table keyed by the reference's own ids, `{key: {name, rank, parent}}`) above the subject's leaves: each leaf keeps its own id (two leaves may share one reference), each ancestor becomes one node once with the id and rank word the adapter maps (`o.id`, `o.rank`), and a leaf with no reference or one the table lacks, a parent the table lacks (the node becomes a root) and a cycle (through `ttBuild`) are reported. It never assumes a number of levels or rank names: a subject's adapter turns its references into nodes (the fish key's: `admin.js` `fuTreeOf`; WoRMS's: `fuWormsOf`, ids `w:<AphiaID>` above the shared species ids).

## Analyses (2026-11-04)

Pierre, 2026-11-04: "I love this kind of multifactor report. Weekly would frustrate me: every 1000 new answers is much better… a new section in the admin with several sub-pages, with visualisations, reports, graphs, tables." Then, the same day: **precomputed, not on the phone**: the stored report is the primary source. Pierre, 2026-11-05: "Recompute here" on every screen too ("my phone is powerful; there is no reason it can't run it"), in that tab only.

- **One report per subject** (Pierre, 2026-11-08: each subject's analyses refitted every 1000 of its answers, "fish when the fish analyses exist"): the subjects with `core.js` `SUBJECTS` `analyses` (the corals, then the fish), each from its own answers only (`core.js` `subjRows`), stored apart (sheet v113, `analysis.js` `AN_SUBJ_SHEET`) and read by the admin's Analyses on that subject's side of the Corals | Fish switch (rule A4). The fish report's units are what its questions ask, the families and scientific genera as the fish tree has them (`f:Damselfishes`, `g:Chromis`); its photos have one view and no photo features in the analyses yet (`SUBJECTS` `analyses` `photos` false: `tools/analyses.js` `metaOf` gives none), so What makes a photo hard is not on the fish side; the views drawn from the live answer log (`admin.js` `LIVE_STATS`) read the fish answers there, not the report: Stops (`statsHealth`), Answer log (`statsPlayers`: the families and genera by name, an IMR sub-entry with its IMR name, its Conditions by rank and, once players type, typed against picked, `logConds`; no Elo column, the sheet sending the corals' levels only) and Pairs (`statsConf`: a pair starts as Look-alike when its families are declared look-alikes or it is two genera of one family, `fishUnitRec`, else Other; no compare page, a name opens its family in Tree; its photos enlarge alone and its observations are iNaturalist's, `SUBJECTS` `admin` `obs`; on both sides the typed mix-ups apart from those before v10, `noChoiceMixups`; `tests/answerlog.test.js`), and its mix-ups carry no view effect (`analysis.js` `confusions`: a view never shown is left out).
- **Where it computes.** `tools/analyses.js`, run by the GitHub Action `.github/workflows/analyses.yml` every hour (minute 23) and by hand (Run workflow, *force*), for each subject in turn (`sheetRunAll`; another subject's only from the script that stores one report per subject, else it logs so and leaves it: rule D4). It asks the sheet for the analysed subject's answer count (`analysesGet` with `head`, cheap: the corals', each subject counted apart from sheet v105, `core.js` `subjCounts`) and does nothing until that subject's answers reach a round thousand (1000, 2000, 3000, …) the stored report does not hold yet (Pierre, 2026-11-08: every 1000 answers of the subject, the fish answers never moving the corals' analyses) (`tools/analyses.js` `plan`: the stored report's newest milestone is its answer count rounded down to a thousand), unless the analysis code changed (`analysis.js` `AV`, part of the stored report) or *force*. Then it reads the answer log (`readAnswers`), fits every analysis at every milestone, reusing the stored milestones whose answers and photo features are unchanged (fingerprint `fpOf`: count, last time, right answers, players, `AV`, a hash of the photo features), and stores the report (`analysesPut`). Both calls carry the admin token from the repository secret **`ANALYSES_ADMIN_TOKEN`** (setup: HANDOVER.md "Analyses report"); without it the job says so and stops, green. The script prints counts only (the Actions log of a public repo is public); the report goes to the sheet only: no artifact, no cache, nothing committed.
- **Milestones.** The first 1000, 2000, 3000, … answers of the log in time order: round thousands only, never shifted by the count of the first report, none before 1000 (Pierre, 2026-11-05; until then the stored report also held all the answers, "All 1933"); each is fitted alone, so the views show how every estimate and its interval moved (As the answers came in). They are kept as history: each is one more look at the same growing log, not a new study, so nothing is marked "new since the previous milestone" (the stats study of 2026-11-05, §5.3; LEARNING.md "Multifactor analyses", Multiple testing). The switch shows the round milestones (the last 5), the newest selected; the status line beside it: the subject, the newest milestone fitted, the subject's answers since it and the next one ("Corals: fitted on 2,000 answers · 729 since · next at 3,000"; "3,000 due within the hour" once its answers have reached it; `admin-analyses.js` `anStatus`), counted as the head and the Action count them (`subjCounts`: the head's `counts` from v105, else the answer log the page reads). **Recompute here** alone adds all the answers so far as one more point, labelled "now (N answers), this tab only" (`report` with `live`: the report's `live` names it), never stored. Bootstrap draws: 999 for the latest milestone (the newest round thousand), 199 for the earlier ones while their answers add up to 60,000, none beyond (`report`; `node tools/analyses.js --boot N --boot-history N`); Recompute here's live point takes as many as the newest milestone.
- **The report** (`analysis.js` `report`): `{AV, made, answers, M: [milestones], R: {milestone: {n, players, at, fp, B, bse, mt, A: {rt, zoom, photo, fmt, conf, learn, back, sess, who, tdiff}}}}` (`B` its bootstrap draws, `bse` 1 for normal bootstrap intervals, `mt` `{m1, m2, a, q}` the number of primary and secondary tests and their levels; `AV` 4); each analysis has `effects` (`k`, `label`, `est`, `lo`, `hi`, `unit`, `sesoi`, `n`, verdict `v` after the multiple-testing correction, `need`; when tested, `fam` 1 primary / 2 secondary, `p`, adjusted `pa`, `alo` / `ahi` its interval at the level it faces; an SD also `lu`, its lower bound before the cut at 0), its typical values `typ` (`p` the share of right answers, or of stops in Sessions, of mix-ups in Confusions; `g` the questions' mean guess rate; `s` the median seconds: what the plain reading is read against; `AV` 2) and its own tables; from `AV` 5, `ent` (effects hard to tell apart, `analysis.js` `entOf`: `k` the effect keys, `r` the correlations of their estimates and `x` of their conditions in the answers as lower triangles, `vif`; Formats: each format's guess rate against its difficulty only; from `AV` 13, with 200 bootstrap draws or more, `rb` the correlations over the draws and `nb` their count) in every analysis with two effects or more from one model, in Learning from `AV` 8, `wright` (the Wright map, `wrightOf`: `th` [id, place, answers, SE], `g` [genus, difficulty, answers, SE, and from `AV` 11 the lowest and highest log-odds beyond guessing the model gives a player on it, each at their own practice and slope: what "every player" is read from], `mid` the typical player's place, `m` logit(0.8); each SE with the intercept's and slope's uncertainty) from `AV` 11, `gcurve` (each genus with practice, `gcurveOf`: `lt` the typical player's practice, `lc` the practice's centre, `m` 0.8, `g` [{k, n, players, `sl` its own slope [est, lo, hi], `at` recognised beyond guessing today, `b` [answers from, n, right, fitted] by practice, `line` its own intercept and slope, `flat`}] lowest `at` first; Learning's random slopes add `sd_gslope` and `sd_pslope` to its effects, and Player profiles a `slope` behaviour), from `AV` 12, `chk` (Can we trust this model?, `checkOf`: `n`, `players`, `cal` [answers, answered, predicted, from, to] in 5 bands, `qq` [probability, quantile residual], `ll` {d, lo, hi, p, players} or null; Answer time's, `checkLmm`: `qq` [normal quantile, residual]) in Answer time, Enlarging, What makes a photo hard, Mix-ups, Learning, After a break and Sessions, and `res` (a second skill?, `resOf`: `lam`, `var`, `null` [median, 95th percentile] of the first axis's share in simulated logs, `sims`, `g` genus scores, `p` player arrows; descriptive, no verdict; a deleted account's answers in the fit, never as a player); `who` from `AV` 7 (Player profiles, `profiles`: `feats`, `who` [{k anonymous id, n answers in the window, low, v the behaviours, z their z-scores}] in the tree's order then the greyed rows, `m` Ward's tree, `cols` and `cm` the behaviours' order and tree, `pca` {var, load, pts}, `k`, `sil`, `groups`, `stab` {loo, boot, runs (compared), of (players), kept, draws, at, stable (1, 0, or null when no run could be compared)}, `close`, `far`; no effects, so outside the multiple-testing correction), from `AV` 9, `tdiff` (Time and difficulty, `analysis.js` `tdiff`: `timed`, `photos`, `bins` [{k, label, n, y right, E expected}] by how fast others answered the photo right, `agree` {photo, photos, genus, genera} the correlation of time intensity and difficulty, `genus` and `slowest` [{k or f, n timed answers, t time intensity in log seconds, d difficulty in logits, + = harder}]), from `AV` 10, `ix` in Zoom, Confusions and Learning (Does it depend on …?, `ixTest`: [{k `view` | `pairview` | `fmtpractice`, `df`, `W` the Wald chi-square, `pm` / `pb` the model's and the bootstrap's p, `draws`, `b` / `se` the product coefficients, `inm` all inside the margin, then `fam` 2, `p`, `pa`, `v`, `need` from the correction; its levels: `lv` (enlarging: per view `off`, `on`, `d` [est, lo, hi] on the log-odds against the app's expectation; format: per format `pts` [answers, est, lo, hi] beyond guessing), or `views`, `typ` and `pairs` [{k, off, mix, v: [view, offers, picked, est, lo, hi]}] (pair × view, on missed questions)}]; `compact` keeps the tests, not the levels), and `map` in Confusions (the mix-up map, `mixMap`: `g` the genera in the dendrogram's order, `m` its merges, `fam` the families, `cells` [shown, answered, offers, mix-ups, shrunk rate] in that order, `base` a typical pair's rate, `across`, `left`; from `AV` 13 `stab` each family's mean best Jaccard over the bootstrap draws, `draws`, `at` 0.75); `compact` drops both from the oldest milestones with the other tables. Players are the sheet's anonymous ids; the admin shows the names from Players (`USERS`), else P1, P2…. `compact` keeps it under the sheet's cap: the oldest milestones lose their tables first, then every other older milestone goes (the first and the last 5 stay), then, if need be, all but the latest; the script refuses to store a report still over the cap.
- **Sheet v35** (`Code.gs` `analyses_`): POST `{analysesGet: 1, token, head?, subject?}` → `{ok, subject, answers, counts, stored: {answers, made, av, size, at, roster, subject} | null, report?}` (v105: `counts` each subject's answers, `core.js` `subjCounts`, its rows with a player, as `readAnswers` serves them; `answers` the asked subject's, else the stored report's, else the replayed one's; before v105 `answers` was every row of the Answers tab, fish included, so each hourly run read the whole log to count the corals'); POST `{analysesPut: 1, token, report}` replaces the whole report in the **Analyses** tab (row 2 its meta, then 45,000-character parts; 2.5 MB at most). Sheet v113: one report per subject, each in its own tab (`Code.gs` `anTab_`): the corals' (the replayed subject's) in **Analyses**, the fish's in **Analyses Fish**; `analysesGet` reads the tab of the `subject` asked (default the replayed one), `analysesPut` the tab of the report's `subject` (none: the replayed one), and either refuses a subject the script does not know (`input`); each stored report is stale on its own (v55). Admin token only (`isAdmin_`). The admin waits for v35 (rule D4): an older script answers `input`, and the views just say "No report yet".
- **The admin** (`admin-analyses.js`): reads the stored report of the subject on screen once per load (`anLoad`, `ANREPS` by subject; another subject's only from the script that stores one per subject, else "No report yet": rule D4) and only draws it, so it opens at once on a phone. On another subject's side its words for the units are that subject's (`SUBJECTS` `analyses` `one`, `many`: "family or genus", "families and genera") and each unit is named, not shown by its id (`anWords`, after drawing), its look-alikes its own (`anGen`), and its Overview ends on one line naming the Analyses views it does not have (`anNotHere`: "Not for fish yet: Difficulty › Photos, …"). Recompute here runs on that subject's answers. A milestone switch (the last 5), the badge, the report's date. Each view: counts; **What changes the result**: one dot-and-range chart per outcome (chance of a right answer, seconds to answer, chance to end the session, chance to pick a wrong genus, right answers against the app's expectation, success of a pure guess), labelled in words, the dashed line at the typical value, then the table Compared / What it does / Verdict; **How much they differ** (players, genera, photos, pairs) in words; the analysis's own charts and tables, with plain column names; **As the answers came in** (each result on its own scale at every milestone). **The plain reading** (Pierre, 2026-11-04: he could not read "logit", "SD player" or "Photo enlarged +152%"; `analysis.js` `PLAIN`, `plainOf`, tested in `tests/analysis.test.js`): every effect has a name saying what is compared ("A poor-quality photo instead of a good one"), a sentence with its direction and size for a typical player and photo ("More likely wrong: about 68% right instead of 80%", converted from the model's scale with the analysis's `typ` and, for a format, its own expected chance and guess rate), its likely (95%) range, a verdict coloured the same way on the pill, the dot and its range (▲ Helps, ▼ Hurts, ● Clear when neither good nor bad: time, the app's own calibration; – No clear effect; … Not enough data yet), after the multiple-testing correction (primary effects under Holm, the others under Benjamini-Hochberg: LEARNING.md "Multifactor analyses"), and, when clear, what it suggests for the app (→). A primary effect carries a *primary* tag, an exploratory one an *exploratory* tag when it reads clear (a lead, not a finding); the verdict's tooltip gives its family and adjusted p (the unadjusted one beside it), never the page itself (rule A2). The Overview's Milestones card lists each milestone (answers, last answer, players, clear results) as history, with no "new" count and no chart of clear results over time. **Visual first** (Pierre, 2026-11-04: "very visual; I can't scroll through everything"): the Overview draws every result as a diverging bar (`divBars`: the change from the typical value, on the outcome's own scale, its likely range, its value in a column on the right, coloured by verdict, the topic under its name), one chart per outcome (a right answer, the time to answer, ending a session, mixing up genera, the app's own expectation, the success of a guess); each view does the same for its own results, its spreads as ranges. A bar of controls under the header: **Show** (all, clear, helps, hurts, not enough data), and a jump list of the page's cards; above every chart and table of results the same **Sort** control (Pierre, 2026-11-05: most negative first (default) or most positive first, by the signed change, a result with no clear effect or too early to tell sorted among the others by its estimate; by topic on the Overview, a sub-heading per analysis; by name: `anSort`, `anOrder`), the Overview's topic (the analysis a result comes from) on a grey line of its own under each name; each list shows only its clear results (verdict `P.v === 'clear'`) until its toggle shows the others in place (`anFold`, kept per page and list in `more`; Show "Not enough data" shows them all); every card folds on its title. The words ("In words", "Every result in words" with a sparkline of each result over the milestones), the milestones and "As the answers came in" are folded by default. These choices are kept per page in this browser (`reefid-admin-anf`, a viewer's convenience). On a phone each result is a block (compared and verdict, then what it does). Definitions and methods are behind the admin "?" (rule A2). **Effects hard to tell apart** (a folded card in each analysis, `anEnt`) and the **Mix-up map** (Difficulty › Mix-ups, `anMixMap`; the whole matrix from 600 px up, on a phone the dendrogram as a list with a band per family that opens its block, the chosen family kept per page in `mfam`): the multifactor visualisation study of 2026-11-05 (`docs/reviews/2026-11-05-multifactor-viz-study.md`, Pierre's Q70-Q77), methods in LEARNING.md; descriptive, not tests, so outside the multiple-testing correction. **Does it depend on …?** (`anIx`, `AN_IX`: a card in Enlarging photos, Genera mixed up and Learning with practice; the study's §5 item 6): its question as its title, its answer in one line with its verdict (Yes, clearly / No, every difference small / Not enough data yet, with the answers needed: rule A5), then what enlarging changes view by view (`dotRange`), each pair's chance that a miss goes to the other genus by view against a typical pair's, and each format's curve against four choices' (small multiples, `smallLines`), each with its table; exploratory tests (one each, Benjamini-Hochberg), not on the fish side for the views while its photos have one. **Each genus with practice** (`anGcurve`, Learning › Practice; the study's §5 item 7): the flat genera and the three furthest below 80% in words, one small chart a genus (right answers by the answers already given, against the model's prediction; `smallLines` up to 5 a row, 2 on a phone), sorted lowest first, and a table (recognised today, what twice as many answers gain, its likely range, a *flat* tag); folded on a phone. **Can we trust this model?** (`anChk`, folded under each of those analyses; the study's §5 item 8): in words, how far the predictions of players left out are from what they answered, whether the quantile residuals lie on the line, and, for Enlarging and After a break, whether they predict new players better than the app's own chance; the two charts (answered against predicted by band, the quantile residuals; Answer time: its residuals against a normal curve) and the bands' table. **Players and genera on one scale** (the Wright map, `anWright`) and **A second skill?** (the residual biplot, `anSkill`, folded on a phone) on Learning › Practice. **Player profiles** (Learning › Profiles, `#analyses/learning/profiles`, `anProfiles`; Pierre, 2026-11-05, Q70-Q74): a heatmap of players × behaviours ordered by Ward's tree (on the left), the greyed rows under 100 answers last, each name linked to the player's journey; the groups and the stability line; the closest pairs and the most different player in words; the map of players (`biplot`) beside it on a desktop, in a card of its own below (folded on a phone, its 8 points furthest from the centre labelled); a table of every number. Admin only (Q73): the report keeps anonymous ids, the names come from Players in the page, the Action prints counts only, the demo and the tests use made-up players. **Sessions in figures** (`analysis.js` `sessDesc`, the session study of 2026-11-04; `desc` in the Sessions result, also on a thin one; `AV` 3): every session (newest first, at most 300: player and their session number, start, minutes, answers, games, right, right − expected, new genera, comeback answers, the last 3 answers, hours since the player's previous session, still going), when sessions start (Manila hour), right answers along a session (by fifth, sessions of 20+; and the last 3, 4th-10th, 11th-20th answers from the end of closed sessions of 30+; right − expected with a bootstrap range over sessions) and the typical time by answer number (photo quiz only), how sessions end (on a miss, on 2+ misses, on a genus met for the first time, against the overall rates; the last answer's game), the last 10 answers of a session against the first 10 of the next by the length of the break (under 3 h, 3-12 h, 12-36 h, over 36 h; the app's expected chance on both sides), players coming back after their 1st, 2nd, 3rd session (a session that ended less than a day before the log ends counted neither way), and first sessions against later ones. Counts and means only; one session definition (`prep`: more than 30 minutes without an answer). **Recompute here** (a quiet link on every screen, phones included: Pierre, 2026-11-05; a 44 px tall touch area on touch screens): the same module in a Web Worker (`new Worker('analysis.js?v=N')`) on the answer log the page reads (`ANS`) and the photo features of the draft (`photoMeta(DATA, DRAFT, …)`), so the page stays responsive, with 199 bootstrap draws read as normal intervals (`B: 199, Bh: 0, bse: 1`: about 2 minutes at 5,000 answers on a desktop, where the Action's 999 percentile draws take about 9; the stored report's milestones are reused when their answers and photo features match, the others keep the model's own interval only); the button shows its progress (the milestone and analysis being fitted) in place of its label, no other warning; the result is kept in that tab, never sent anywhere, and the stored report stays the primary source.
- **The module** (`analysis.js`, plain JS: browser global `ANALYSIS`, a Web Worker's message handler, Node `require`): `prep` (formats from the mode, sessions, exposures, the previous answer on a genus, Again after a miss), `lmm` and `glmm` (crossed random effects), `fmtFit`, the eight analyses, `verdictOf`, `boot` (cluster bootstrap over players, warm-started refits), `PRIMARY` / `pOf` / `holm` / `bh` / `correct` (the multiple-testing correction, applied by `runAll` to every milestone), `milestones`, `report`, `compact`, `photoMeta` (view, quality, sharpness, source of each photo from `data.json`, `curation.json`, `quality.json`, `metrics.json`; the view is the one the games use, through `core.js` `applyCuration` and `applySlots`), `synth` / `demoLog` (synthetic answers with known effects, for the tests and the demo). Methods: LEARNING.md "Multifactor analyses".
- **Demo** (`admin.html?demo`): seven more made-up players (`ANALYSIS.demoLog`, about 2,200 answers on the real photos) and the report computed by the demo itself in a Web Worker, as the Action would (no bootstrap); answering as the script that stores one report per subject (`?demo&v=N`, N = `AN_SUBJ_SHEET` or above) a fish report too, from the same seven players' made-up fish answers (`ANALYSIS.synth` with `subject`, about 1,200 on the fish units with the most photos), kept out of the demo's answer log so the other fish pages stay as they were; `?demo&v=30` answers as a script before v35. The demo's answers are the same on every load of one commit (seeded draws; a seeded stream never feeds a random-comparator sort, which Chromium calls a varying number of times: `analysis.js` `shuffle`), and what the smoke reads in them is built in, not left to the draws: photos everyone misses in Carla's live session (the questions with photos the signed-in players rated first, so Health shows a difficulty in words; a question the donors cannot bring under the rule gives its answers back), Ben's saved copy reading another % than the replay, a signed-in session besides Carla's among Recent sessions (`admin-demo.js`).

## Progress sync (Google Sheet)

- `app.js` `SYNC_URL` points to an Apps Script web app; code in [`tools/sync/Code.gs`](../tools/sync/Code.gs). RAs sign in with a name + 4-digit code (no Google account; the code only prevents accidental overwrites). Where (Pierre, 2026-11-01): the header "Sign in" button (`#signb`, shown by `showProg()` while signed out, or as "Not saved" when `syncTrouble()`, which opens ⚙) opens the sign-in card (`signinNudge(false, false)`); the account line (`#sync`, filled by `bindSync()`) and "Backup or reset progress" (`bindProgFile()`, `#reset`) live in ⚙ (`#settings`), never on My progress.
- **One-time notices after the first sync** (Pierre, 2026-11-08: a device still holding an old copy of his account showed him again, two or three times over three devices, the cards and toasts he had seen on another). For a signed-in player, the one-time cards (the update notes `recalcNotice`, the learned mark taken away `mxLostNotice`, the rules versions' cards `rvNotice` with the apology `RV_SORRY`, the opening apology `sorryNotice`, a subject's card `subjHello`, the leaderboard note `boardNotice`) and the achievements check (`achCheck`, `achRecheck`: their toasts, given and taken back) wait for the visit's first sync (`sync.js` `noticeLater`): when its pull and merge are done (`noticesGo`, from `syncPush` / `syncPull`'s end, whatever the reply: offline, an error or a refused code shows them at once from this device's state), or after `NOTICE.WAIT` = 20 s at most (a line so slow the reply has not come). Each is then decided on the merged state: the progress and achievements of every device, and **the notices told on any device of the account**, `reefid-sync-told` (`core.js` `TOLD`, `toldUnion`: tokens `recalc:<note id>`, `rv:<version>`, `mx:<unit>@<its s.mx>`, `sorry`, `hello:<subject>-<mode>`, `board`, `allmet:<subject>-<reason>`; and `left:<subject>`, the custom quiz's reminder, below), written when a card is closed (`toldAdd`, with a save), merged as the union at each sync (`toldMerge` in `mergeSaved`) and in an account merge (`mergeUsers`), cleared at sign-out with the account's keys, deleted and archived with the account's Data (rule D7). Each card's own device mark (`reefid-recalc`, `reefid-rv-msg`, `reefid-mx-lost`, `reefid-sorry`, `reefid-<dev>hello-<mode>`, `reefid-board-told`) stays and counts too; the first time this version runs on a device, the marks it holds go to the account signed in then, never to a later one (`progress.js` `toldSeed`, `reefid-told-seeded`: a shared device's next player never takes them). Not signed in: no wait, no tokens, the device marks only, as before. Tested in `tests/notices.test.js` and `tests/pages.smoke.js` `noticesAfterSync` (`SMOKE_ONLY=notices`). Any sheet script stores the record as it comes (no version gate, rule D4).
- **One per subject** (the fish welcome question, Pierre 2026-11-04; docs/FISH_PATH.md "The fish welcome question and the IMR answer"): each subject asks its own, in its own words (`core.js` `SUBJECTS` `start`: the question `q` and, per answer id of `CHOICE.opts`, its answer and line; fish: "How well do you know reef fish?", "I'm new to reef fish", "I'm at IMR (Research Assistant programme)", "I know reef fish well"), with the same answers' variables for now (`CHOICE.opts`: `[id, {p, at, basics}]`; the fish values are to be set with learnsim fish profiles), kept in its own record (`core.js` `startKey`: `reefid-sync-<subject>-start`, so the coral answer stays the corals'), asked on the card before that subject's first quiz question, its fallback window counting that subject's answers. Everything below holds per subject; a subject without a basics module (`SUBJECTS` `pages` without `basics`: no Fish basics yet; `app.js` `basicsDue`) offers no basics after "new" and has no "lower" card. The fish answers are logged but not replayed, so the fish prior applies at once (`model.js` `startP`: no replay to wait for). The admin's player journey shows the corals' record only.
- **First-launch question** (Pierre, 2026-11-03; `core.js` `CHOICE`, `model.js` `startSet`, `startStep`, `startDue`, `startMerge`, `quiz.js` `startAskShow`, `startFbShow`; docs/LEARNING.md "The first-launch question"): "How well do you know corals?", a forced choice among "I'm new to corals", "I had the IMR coral course", "I know corals well" (`app.js` `startOptsHtml`: big buttons, one per row, each with its line), asked as the welcome tour's first step (no Skip, no Next and no arrow key until answered) or, for a player who skipped the tour (or closed it with Escape), on a card before the next photo-quiz question (`startGate`, `.unlock.startq`: the three answers only; Escape goes Home and the quiz asks again). Existing players: asked once the same way when they have fewer than `CHOICE.ASK` (50) answers; with 50 or more never asked, the answer inferred silently from their global level (`core.js` `startInfer`, recorded `via: 'inferred'`; Pierre, 2026-11-03). It sets variables of the existing model, never a coded path (`startV()`): the level the starting ability of a genus never answered is shrunk towards (`core.js` `playerStart(P, skip, p)`) and, for "new", Coral basics offered first (the tour's last button "Coral basics (5 min)", the card's follow-up `startBasicsOffer`). `CHOICE` `at`, the answer from which the offer to open all genera could come, is no longer read since that card went (Pierre, 2026-11-12, Q337). Key **`reefid-sync-coral-start`** (synced with the progress like every `reefid-sync*` key, merged by `startMerge`: the latest answer wins, the fallback cards shown on either device count; a question card left waiting resumes the quiz only once `sync.js` `mergeSaved` has merged everything else (`startResume`); cleared at sign-out; kept across a progress reset, its window restarted): `{c, t, at, via: tour / card / fallback / inferred, w: [[difficulty, guess rate, right, first answer on its genus]…] (up to the last of CHOICE.AT, 60), hd (the opening hold fired in the window), v (the verdict waiting for its card), fb: {'<answer>-lo' / '-hi': [answers, time]}, h: the history, an append-only list of timestamped events (`core.js` `startEvents`: answers given `[c, at, t, via]` and eases `[band below, answers, t, 'ease', the answer eased, the time of the choice event eased]`, an ease applying only while that very choice event is in force (an older ease without the time: bound to the latest choice of its answer at or before it, in its own record), every one kept: the full replay needs each answer's prior, and a compaction would not commute with the union; `START_CAP` 200 is only a safety bound), from which `c`, `at`, `t`, `via` and `ease` (the ease in force on the current answer) are derived (`startNorm`) and the prior at any time (`startPriorAt`: an ease applies from its time until the next answer, to the answer it eased only; a record without h reads as its answer and its ease field), ease: [c, answers, time], wt (the window's epoch: set by a coral progress reset, `startRestart`, so a window saved before it never wins a merge; a reset made here starts a new epoch, one adopted from another device, `sync.js` `mergeSubject`, takes that reset's time and keeps a window already counted since it; a fish reset leaves it)}`; the variables in force are the `ease` band's when set, else the answer's (`startEff`); the prior enters the live ability (`model.js` `startP`) and the replay of this device's pending answers (`sync.js` `replayAdopt`) only once the sync script's replies say v ≥ `core.js` `START_SHEET` (36, the script that replays it; rule D4, Codex review #428); `startMerge` (and `sync.js` `signIn`, for a record made on this device before signing in, and `core.js` `mergeUsers`, the admin's account merge) takes the union of the two histories (`core.js` `startUnion`: one event per kind, time and value, oldest first, the same whichever way round), keeps the window of the record holding the current answer (on the same answer the later epoch `wt`, then the longer window), and drops the planned questions when the variables in force change. **Fallback card** (everyone who answered, also through the tour: they may have picked wrong; never an inferred answer): `quiz.js` `answer` feeds `startStep` the answers after the answer (their difficulty `dm` and guess rate as the ability step takes them, right, whether it was the genus's first answer), up to 60; at 40 and 60 answers only (`CHOICE.AT`) `core.js` `startCheck` compares the global level (the Elo over every answer, with its standard error over the window) with the band the answer predicts (`startBand`: the edges midway between the priors; Pierre, 2026-11-04, after weighing the errors: a true beginner on the harder path is the costliest): `lo`, for "IMR course" and "know well" only, when it is under the floor with one-sided 80% confidence (`CHOICE.ZL`) or the opening hold fired in the window; `hi` with `CHOICE.MET` (10) genera met, when it is above the ceiling with 95% confidence and, as a guard only, the level fitted on the first answers on each genus (`levelFit`) above it too; `startDue` adds once per answer and direction (`fb`, marked when shown), no move back after a move (`startBack`, the silent one included), `lo` only while Coral basics is not done, `hi` only with a higher answer to offer; shown by `quizPhoto` as an out-of-game prompt (`promptOk`, `noOpen`). `lo` is a gentle offer that never labels the player (`.startfb[data-dir=lo]`): "The first genera feel hard?", **Coral basics in 5 minutes** (opens it and moves the variables one band down silently: `model.js` `startEase`) and **No thanks, I know the basics** (changes nothing), two buttons of equal weight; `hi`: "You know more than you said" with the higher answers (`startSet(c, 'fallback')`) or Keep my answer. The answers are kept either way. The admin's player journey shows the answer, how it was given ("inferred" in bold; "changed by you on Players" for Pierre's change, sheet v99, `core.js` `startAdminSet`, whose fallback window restarts from it), each change, the silent ease ("Eased") and the fallback cards (`admin-journey.js` `jStart`). Tests: `tests/start.test.js`, `tests/pages.smoke.js` `startSmoke`. **Rules version 3**: the prior moves the progress, so the full replay applies it at each answer (`core.js` `replayProgress` option `starts`, `startPriorAt`: the answer then in force, from the record's history; sheet v29 reads it from the saved data or an anonymous send, and from v36 keys its stored replay (the Replayed tab's Since column, `reset#fingerprint`, `core.js` `startFp`) by the history too, so a replay made with another history is not reused, and sends that fingerprint with the replay (`fp`): `sync.js` `replayAdopt` adopts it only when it matches the history the device now holds (else the save that follows carries the merged history and brings the right replay), `admin.js` from the saved copy, `sync.js` from the store).
- **Welcome tour** (`tour.js`, 6 steps, the first-launch question first; 8 for a player with two subjects or more, below): each step spotlights `sel` (fallback `alt`), joined with `also` into one frame, plus an optional separate `plus` spotlight (step 3: Practice in the menu). The bubble must never cover a spotlight (Pierre, 2026-11-01): `frame()` scrolls once per step so the target fits in the band between the pinned header and the phone tab bar, beside or above/below the bubble (`.tbub.wide` on wide screens when that is what it takes); a target taller than the band shows its top, or its bottom with `tall: 'end'` (the doors). `arrows: true` (step 3) draws a big bobbing arrow at each spotlight (`arrows()`, below/above it, else beside, never over the bubble or another spotlight; the phone sheet then sits midway between the two spotlights, and on wide screens the bubble stands one arrow away from the target). `html.touring` adds room below the page so the last element can scroll clear. `place()` then puts the bubble on the free side (phones: a sheet above the tab bar, or at the top with `.up`). Never write the genus count in user-facing text: it changes (bonus genera). Any `.unlock` card or offer due while the tour runs (the first genus cards on step 3) waits under it, hidden by `html.touring .unlock`, and its keys let the tour's through; it shows, focused, when the tour closes (an RA, 2026-11-02; `pages.smoke.js` checks it).
