v3 `?stats=1` photo difficulty; v4 `?answers=1` anonymous ids (PR #195); v5 answers carry expected success, ability, new photos, single-view answers count +0.5 harder (PRs #197, #201). v6 progress of any size, stale-write check, input hardening, PIN throttle, secret anonymous-id salt (code review, 2026-11-01). v7 players' reports (`Reports` tab). v8 reports readable by every admin (GitHub token check). v9 `quiz|kN` modes rated with a 1/N guess (deployed 2026-11-01). v10 `Choices` column, `?stats=1` adds `conf` (pair offered / mixed-up counts) (PR #286). **v10 is deployed (Pierre, 2026-11-01 08:10 UTC, verified live: `v: 10`, `conf` in `?stats=1`); external-request permission granted.** The app and admin work with v5 to v10; reports wait in the player's browser until v7 or later is live, and admin Triage needs v8 to read them. Every reply carries `v`, so the app and admin can tell which script is live. **v17** (2026-11-02, self-deploys; Pierre: anonymous players and bots should not rate the photos, players should be encouraged to sign in): photo difficulty and confusions (`stats_`, cache key `stats10`) count only trusted players (`trusted_`: not `~anon`, `TRUST.N` = 10 answers or more, right rate clearly above chance, one-sided z ≥ `TRUST.Z` = 1.645 against each question's guess rate; judged on the whole history at each recompute, so a beginner who gets out of chance counts from then on with all their answers). Every player starts as a beginner, level `PLAYER0` = -1 (about 45% right on an average photo with 4 choices; Pierre: the RAs play from the first day of their course, and players without the IMR course know even less), not as an average player (0), whose misses made the photos look harder. Each answer moves the photos by a weight n / (n + `PHOTO_W.N` = 30) after n answers of the player (Glicko's idea, Glickman 1999, as in Park EDM 2021 and Pelánek 2016's separate uncertainty for students and items: an uncertain level moves the items little, so a beginner's random first answers weigh little), and repeats count against the player's own history with the photo (Duolingo's half-life regression, Settles & Meeder 2016, fits each word's difficulty beside the learner's seen/right/wrong counts with it; PFA): a photo new to the player is expected `PHOTO_W.NEW` = 0.5 harder, one already right twice `PHOTO_W.KNOWN` = 1 easier, the app's own rule (`answer()` `bk`). The photo's answer and right counts are weighed the same way (`p[2] += w`): they damp later steps and are the app's confidence in the rating (`photoDiff`), so zero-weight answers change nothing. A corallites hint left closed is not logged with the answer (not seen when answering), so it is neither rated nor counted as recognised. A first-answer-only rule (Papoušek, Pelánek & Stanislav, EDM 2014, for prior knowledge of geography) was tried and dropped (Pierre): players here learn the corals in the app, so the first answer is the noisiest. Every answer still moves the player's own level (`ras`), from `PLAYER0`. Anonymous answers are kept and count once the device signs in (moved to the name, v12). Names are matched case-blind (`lc_`). `readAnswers` rows carry an anonymous flag (index 11) so `core.js` `trustOf` / `eloOf` / `confusionOf` replay the same rule in the admin (and the admin demo). The sign-in card says the answers of players not signed in don't rate the photos. Admin `{anonDelete: [ids]}` (token) deletes every answer of chosen anonymous ids among the rows without a name (`~anon`, `~deleted`), never a named player's: Players > Not signed in, tick, Delete (test runs; Pierre: no developer submissions in the data). **v16** (2026-11-01, self-deploys): the leaderboard computes each player's % itself from their saved progress (`pctOf_`, a copy of `core.js` `pctOf`, the app's `progPct` formula; over the "By genus" tab's genera), so it is current for everyone without waiting for their next sync and no longer trusts a % sent by the app (the Progress column stays as a fallback). **v15** (2026-11-01, self-deploys): the leaderboard gives a level only from `ELO_MIN` = 200 answers (null before); `GET ?achs=1` (`achs_`, cached 10 minutes) counts the signed-in players holding each achievement from their saved progress (counts only). **v14** (2026-11-01, self-deploys): public leaderboard `GET ?board=1` (`board_`: name, progress %, level from `stats_` `ras`, for every RAs row with answers whose Board column is not 0; progress then level; a row saved before v14 uses learned / total until the next sync; cached 5 minutes); each sync writes the summary's `pct` and `board` into the new RAs columns Progress and Board (header added to an older tab on the first write; a sync without `board` keeps the saved choice); per-player answers and abilities became admin-only (POST `readAnswers` / `readStats` with the token), the admin falling back to the old GETs while an older script answers. **v13** (2026-11-01, self-deploys): the admin player list (`users_`) gives each player's anonymous id (`id`, the one of `?answers=1` and `?stats=1`), so the admin Players page puts names on the anonymous stats. **v12** (2026-11-01, self-deployed): answers of players who never sign in arrive as `{anon, anonAnswers}` under `~anon <random device id>` (`anon_`; app: `ANONQ`, up to 2000, `sendAnon()` at most 100 a request, once 10 answers wait or 20 seconds after the last one (`anonSoon`), on start-up, when back online and when the page is hidden; a refused or failed send is reported as a `sync` event, 2026-11-03; and once per `ANON_RESEND.ID` (`anonResend()`, 2026-11-03, Pierre: RAs who played without a name were missing from the sheet) every answer the device played without an account, `LOG0`, is queued again, the sheet skipping those it already has by id), moved to the name at sign-in (`anon` in the first send, `relabel_`); a copy run on localhost / 127.x / `file:` (tests, `tools/perf.js`, a developer), or in a browser driven by a program on any host (`navigator.webdriver`, 2026-11-03: an agent's test served the app under a made-up host name and the requests sent while its page unloaded escaped the network mocks: 100 made-up answers and 6 events, removed; `tests/net.smoke.js` check 11; rule D1), never sends anonymous answers, reports or events (`LOCAL` in `app.js`; 2026-11-02: 17 anonymous "players" of 5-6 answers each were `tools/perf.js` runs, whose requests passed by the test mocks once the service worker was in; `perf.js` now also mocks the sheet inside the page); flood caps per 6 hours (`over_`: anonymous id 600, all anonymous 6000, per name 3000, new accounts 40, reports 300) answering `busy`, first refusal logged in the **Alerts** tab. See [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md). **v11** (2026-11-01, deployed by Pierre the same day and verified live; it updates itself from then on): admin user management (`users_`: POST `{users}`, `{userData}`, `{userDelete, answers}`, `{userMerge: {from, into, data, at}}`, all behind the admin token check; from v28 a merge saves its copy without `reefid-sync-coral-rv`, so the account's devices get the replay of both histories); a `Removed` tab (name, code hash, into, time) so a device still signed in with a removed name and its old code gets `error: 'gone'` (+ `into`) instead of re-creating the account (the app then asks the player to sign out, and to sign in as `into`; answers sent by a merged name are logged under `into`); and the mode of a duel on a chosen pair (`quiz-duel|A|B`) is kept (v10 dropped it: uppercase refused, so it was rated as a 4-choice answer). **v18** (2026-11-02, self-deploys; Pierre: "réintégrer les données des joueurs anonymes que l'on peut détecter comme étant des vrais joueurs"): devices that never signed in count for photo difficulty and confusions under the same `TRUST` rule as signed-in players (`trusted_` no longer excludes `~anon`); faster ratings (Pierre: the first players are RAs and experts): `PHOTO_W.N` 30 → 10; each photo of `?stats=1` gets a 4th field, the plain count of the answers that rated it (the 2nd is weighed by experience, so a photo answered once weighed under 1 and the admin's "≥1" filter hid it: 111 of the ~300 photos answered); stats cache key `stats11`. **v19** (2026-11-02, self-deploys; Pierre: most RAs will not report problems): `events_` logs what the players' devices report by themselves into an **Events** tab (`EVH`): app errors (`err`: message, file:line, page; only errors from the app's own `.js` files on this site, not scripts that a browser or an extension injects into the page, nor the harmless "ResizeObserver loop" warning: `ownError()` in `sync.js`, Pierre 2026-11-02), photos that still fail after their retries (`photo`), saves that fail while online (`sync`), and the one-tap question's answers (`pulse`: too easy / about right / too hard, with mode, answers this visit, right % of the last 10, overall level, genera met); a random device id (`reefid-dev-id`), never a name; caps 50 per request, 200 per device, 3000 in all per 6 hours, ids deduplicated. Admin `readEvents` (token). App side: `ev()`, `sendEvents()` (queue `reefid-evq`, never from a local copy), `pulseDue()` / `pulseAsk()` (`PULSE`: first after 15 answers, then every 60, at least 8 this visit; counted in answers). Admin **Errors & feedback** `#inbox/todo/errors` (`renderEvents`). **v20** (2026-11-02, self-deploys; Pierre: a renamed test account must not be on the leaderboard): admin `{userBoard: name, hide}` (token) sets the RAs Board column to -1 (hidden by an admin) or 1; a sync keeps -1 whatever the player's app sends, `board_` skips 0 (the player's opt-out) and -1, `users_` gives each player's `board`. Admin Players > a player > Manage: On the leaderboard / Hidden, with Hide / Show (and "hidden" by the name). `{userRename: {from, into}}` (token) moves a record to a new name (RAs row, saved progress, By genus, answers, reports), hidden from the leaderboard; the old name is not marked removed, so its owner signs out and signs in under it to start over; the app cannot open the renamed record (the code hash is tied to the name): an archive, read in the admin (Players > Manage > Rename…; Pierre: his account from the old algorithms kept as "Pierre-test"). v20 also rates a photo-quiz question with 2 or 3 genera in its Choices but no `|kN` in its mode with its real guess rate, 1 / choices (`guess_`, `core.js` `guessOf`): the "Again: A or B?" question after a mix-up was logged plain `quiz` and rated as a 4-choice one (59 of the first 552 answers); Pierre asked for the existing data to be corrected, which this does without rewriting a row; the app now logs it `quiz|k2`; stats cache key `stats12`. **v23** (2026-11-02, self-deploys; Pierre): the load reply carries the player's opening hold (`hold_`, see "Opening hold synced" above); the Answers tab gets Seconds and Enlarged at the end (see "Answer time and enlargement"), and `readAnswers` rows indexes 12-13. Backward compatible both ways: an app before v23 ignores `hold` and sends neither field (blank cells; its gentle-restart answers carry no `|w`, so the sheet's hold counts them, like every row logged before v23); the new app against v22 gets no `hold` (device-only hold, as before) and v22 drops `rt`/`zm` while logging `|w` in the mode as usual (counted out by v23 once live). **v32** (2026-11-04, self-deploys; Pierre): the full replay reads each answer's Seconds (`answerRows_` reads columns A-N, `rowOf_` puts the seconds at index 12) for the answer-time weight of rules version 4 (`core.js` `rtWeight`); the app turns that weight on only from this version (`core.js` `RT_SHEET`). **v31** (2026-11-03, self-deploys; Pierre: "drop the Easy/Medium/Hard classification, implement the hierarchical photo Elo"): `stats_` rates each photo as its genus's offset (`G`, shared by every photo of the genus shown, moved by `GENUS_K` = 0.4 / (1 + its weighed answers / 10) × the surprise × the answer's weight) plus its own residual (moved as before); `?stats=1` serves the total in `photos` and the offsets in `gd`; `core.js` `GENUS_K`, `eloReplay`, `eloOf`, `genusOffsetsOf` in step (`tests/core.test.js`); stats cache `stats15`. The app reads `gd` only from v31 (`model.js` `PGD_SHEET`); with an older script every photo's prior is 0. The genus offset moves the photo memory offsets `mem` that `replay_` uses, so `core.js` appends rules version 3 (`RVS`) and every player is replayed again (Codex P1 on #422). docs/LEARNING.md "Hierarchical photo Elo". **v30** (2026-11-03, self-deploys; Pierre): `pages` events, the pages opened in a visit, under the admin's id of the player, in their own **Pages** tab, read with `readEvents` `pages: 1` ("Pages opened" above). **v28** (2026-11-03, self-deploys; Pierre): the full replay of each player's log by the script itself ("Full replay" above): `core.js` embedded (`CORE_`, `tools/sync_core.py`), `replay_` in the replies to a load, a save and anonymous answers when the app's `rv` is older, the **Replayed** tab, `data.json` read from GitHub for the look-alikes (cached); stats cache key unchanged (`stats_` unchanged but for reading the tab through `answerRows_`). **v27** (2026-11-03, self-deploys; Pierre): typed answers (mode `|t`, the player types the genus) rated with no guess rate (`guess_` 0, as `core.js` `guessOf`), stats cache `stats14`. **v26** (2026-11-03, self-deploys; Pierre): photo memory estimated from the answers (`MEM`, `memBucket_`, `memFam_` in `stats_`; `core.js` `MEM`, `eloReplay`, `photoMemOf` in step, checked by `tests/core.test.js`): an answer on a photo is expected easier by an offset from the player's own history with it (new 0; shown never right, right once, right twice or more), rated in the same replay as the photos and the players from the trusted answers, shrunk to the old fixed rule (`PHOTO_W.NEW` / `KNOWN`, as offsets 0 / −0.5 / −0.5 / −1.5) weighing 10 answers; the photos and the player's level now move against the same expectation (until v25 the level ignored memory); `?stats=1` adds `mem` (the four offsets) and `memn` (trusted answers in each bucket); stats cache `stats13`. The app uses `mem` (`model.js` `PMEM`), the prior with an older sheet. docs/LEARNING.md "Photo memory". **v25** (2026-11-03, self-deploys; Pierre): `oldApp_` logs a request without `av` (an app from before 2026-11-03: photo stats, load and save once the account's code is checked, anonymous answers) in Events as kind `old`, with the name or anonymous id it carries, at most once an hour per sender and 300 in all per 6 hours (Codex review #373); admin Errors & feedback lists them under Old app versions. **v24** (2026-11-03, self-deploys; Codex review #372): the flood caps (`over_`, per anonymous id, all anonymous, per name) count only the answers the Answers tab does not have yet (`freshAnswers_`, which hands the ids it read to `logAnswers_`), so the app's one-time catch-up of anonymous answers or a resend after a lost reply never uses up a device's quota. **v22** (2026-11-02, self-deploys): the leaderboard % (`pctOf_`) follows the app's new progress, `s.pg` (a moving average weighed by what each answer proves; progress saved before it recomputed from its last 10 results, `core.js` `pgOf`), so the board and the app show the same %. **v21** (2026-11-02, self-deploys): a `load` event, once a visit (`loadMark` in `app.js`, `loadSend` in `sync.js`): seconds to the first page drawn (`p0`, `t0`), to the quiz (`qo`) and to its first photo (`q1`), the next page (`nx`), the line measured (`bps`, kbit/s), KB downloaded (`kb`), served by the offline copy (`sw`), Saver (`sv`), theme, window width, installed as an app or in a browser tab (`st`: `display-mode: standalone`, iOS `navigator.standalone`), and the CDN server that sent `data.json` (GitHub Pages' `x-served-by`, in the Message column); sent when the first quiz photo shows, when the app is hidden or 3 min after the first page. Admin Errors & feedback shows the newest 40 with medians (Load times). Purpose: set the loading order and `LINK.SLOW` from the players' real lines (Pierre, 2026-11-02). **v33** (2026-11-04, self-deploys; the rename to IMR Reef ID approved by Pierre on 2026-11-03): the app's storage keys were renamed with the app; a player's saved data is read under the new key names or the old ones (`dataKey_`: progress, reset, achievements), an account merge drops the rules version under its new and its old name, see "Names, storage keys and caches". **v38** (2026-11-04, self-deploys): the Coral Finder tool renamed the Coral ID Key (`#identify/key`; the book keeps its name): a one-shot migration, `ONCE_` `idkey` run by `autoUpdate` once the new code finds itself up to date (`once_`: a Script Property `ONCE_idkey` marks it done, the Deploy log line says how many cells changed), rewrites the tool's old page names to `idkey` / `lab-idkey` in the Pages and Events tabs (Page, Detail) and the Reports tab (Where); the app's page names reach any older script as they are (`pagesClean` takes any name), so nothing waits for v38. The `ONCE_` table is the place for any later one-shot data migration (alpha, rule W3).
- **Pages opened** (sheet v30 `pages`, Pierre 2026-11-03: "most players only play the quiz and never open the genus sheets": does anyone, do beginners more, do sheet readers progress faster?). First-party, no outside service or script. The app keeps a tally of the visit (`sync.js` `pagesRoute`, called at the end of `app.js` `route()` for the page actually drawn, not a link that sent on; pure functions in `core.js` `PAGES`, `pageName`, `pageFrom`, `pagesOpen`, `pagesStop`, `pagesResume`, `pagesTake`, `pagesSummary`): per page name (`home`, `practice.quiz`, `practice.custom`, `learn.genera`, `learn.genus`, `learn.look-alikes`, `learn.compare`, `identify.key`, `progress`, `leaderboard.achievements`, `tools.…`; the address's section and page, its things and filters dropped, since the URL refonte of 2026-11-05; the rows before it hold the old names, `genus`, `quiz`…, until the sheet's one-shot renames them; a page drawn with another subject than the corals on screen carries that subject's prefix, `core.js` `SUBJECTS` `pv`: `fish.practice.quiz`, `fish.home`…, `pageName(h, pre)`, from 2026-11-04, so a fish page is told from a coral one, and switching the subject on the same page (Tools) closes one page and opens the other (`pagesRoute` keys on the hash and the prefix); the corals' prefix is empty; the rows before that date are all read as coral pages, including the admin's fish beta visits, which cannot be told apart; the sheet's `pagesClean` already accepted `.`, so no gate; the admin's Journey reads it back with `pageSubj`, "practice.quiz (Fish)") the times opened and the seconds on screen (only while the app is visible), and per genus sheet `[times, seconds, from, asked]`: `from` how its first opening was reached (`quiz`: the answer's feedback; `learn`: Genera, a sheet, Look-alikes, Compare; `progress`: My progress, achievements, leaderboard; `other`), from the page before it or, for the quiz feedback's link that opens a new tab on a computer, from the link clicked (`reefid-pv-link`, 30 s); `asked` whether the genus had been asked to the player before (`P[g].ok + ko`). When the app is hidden (`visibilitychange`), `pagesSend` queues one `pages` event (`ev`, `reefid-evq`) with the summary (`r`, `g`, capped at 30 pages and 30 genera), the visit id `vi` (random a page load) and the answers so far `na` (`answersSoFar`), and starts a fresh tally (coming back adds another summary with the same `vi`; the page still in view counts 0 times). Each summary is queued with its owner (`o`, `core.js` `pagesOwner`: `n:<name>` signed in, `a:<anonymous answer id>` else; Codex review #423), kept on the device: a request (`sync.js` `evBatch`, `core.js` `pagesBatch`) sends only the summaries of the player active now, without `o`, and drops from the queue any queued for someone else, so a summary left waiting (offline, a failed send) is never sent under the next player; at sign-in the device's anonymous summaries become the account's (`pagesOwn`, as its answers do), and sign-out drops the summaries still queued. `sendEvents` adds to a request holding one the player's id: `eanon` (the device's anonymous answer id, `reefid-anon-id`) or, signed in, `ename`/`epin`. The sheet (`events_`, `evWho_`) stores it as the admin's id of the player (`aid_('~anon ' + id)` or `aid_(name)` after checking the code; '' on a wrong code), never the name or the raw id, in the Message column of a **Pages** tab of its own (same columns as Events), the summary cleaned by `core.js` `pagesClean` in Detail. The Pages tab follows the answers (Codex review #423, `pagesMove_`, `pagesDrop_`): when a device's anonymous answers are claimed at sign-in (`anon` in the first send) their summaries move to the account's id, and an admin rename, merge or delete moves them as it moves the answers (to the new name, the merged name, or the answers' `~deleted` id); a delete with the answers and `anonDelete` delete them (never a named player's). `POST {readEvents: 1, pages: 1, token}` reads the last 10,000 rows of that tab (`PAGES_READ`), so errors and load times never push them out of Events' 3,000-row window. **The app waits for script v30** (`core.js` `PAGES.SHEET`, rule D4): nothing is queued until a reply said `v` >= 29 (`sync.js` `sheetSeen`, kept in `reefid-sheet-v`, from the photo stats, events and anonymous answers replies); like every event, never sent from a local copy. Admin (`admin.js` `loadPages`, `pagesRows`, `sheetsOf`; the read is kept 5 minutes, an error 30 s, read again when Stops or a Journey is drawn after that (`pagesFresh`, which redraws once when a new read arrives, never on a cached one: no render / read loop; Codex review #423), and ↻ on Activity reads it again): Analyses › Learning › Stops › Genus sheets opened (`healthSheets`: by experience band, answers so far under 50, 50-300, over 300, the players and visits and the share that opened at least one sheet; Without me drops your visits) and the Journey's Genus sheets opened (per genus times, time, first opened before or after the genus was first asked, from where; the pages they spent most time on; `#players/list/journey/<name>` and `#players/list/anon/<id>`); before v30 both say which version they wait for. `admin.html?demo` serves a Pages tab of made-up visits. Privacy: `privacy.html` "Which pages you open", About › Privacy.
- **First page from Home** (sheet v124 `fromhome`; ROADMAP "Load order, further steps" (c), Pierre 2026-11-02, reopened 2026-11-09): which page players open first from Home, so the loading tiers ("Load order in tiers") can follow real use. `app.js` `fromHome`, called by `route()` before it draws a page, sends one event a visit (`ev`, the Events tab), the first time a Home it drew is left for another page: the page name (`core.js` `pageName`) in the message column, `{s, w, tr}` in the detail: the seconds since Home was first drawn, whether the page's tier was still on its way (the player saw the loading bar), whether the welcome tour ran this visit (its own steps, taken while it is on screen, are not counted). Sent only once the script reports `FROMHOME_SHEET` (rule D4). The `load` event's `nx` (the first page after the first page drawn, sheet v21) is close but is lost when that event leaves first (at the first quiz photo or after 3 min) and counts the tour's steps. `tests/fromhome.test.js`, `tests/sync.test.js`.
- **Full replay** (sheet v28, Pierre 2026-11-03: progress recalculated from the whole answer log, automatically, never by a human click, now and whenever the progress engine changes; docs/LEARNING.md "Full replay"). `core.js` is embedded at the end of `Code.gs` between `// BEGIN core.js (generated by tools/sync_core.py, do not edit)` and `// END core.js`, inside a function that gives it its own `module` (so its names stay out of the script's globals, where `TRUST`, `PHOTO_W`, `MEM`, `PLAYER0` are Code.gs's own; reached as `CORE_`); `tools/sync_core.py --fix` writes it, `--check` runs in CI; the self-updater installs it with `Code.gs` (one file). Code.gs keeps its own `pctOf_` and the Elo of `stats_` (`CORE_` is used for the replay only). Requests carry `rv`, the rules version this device applied (`sync.js` `rvApplied`, key `reefid-sync-coral-rv`, synced with the progress): `GET ?…&rv=N&pt=T`, `POST {…, rv, pt}` (save) and `POST {anon, anonAnswers, rv, pt, reset}` (a device that never signed in). Signing in to an existing account takes the account's `reefid-sync-coral-rv` (0 when its data has none), never the device's (`sync.js` `signIn`). When `rv` < `CORE_.RV` (`core.js` `RVS`), `replay_` replays the player's rows of the Answers tab since their last reset (`core.js` `replayProgress`, with `stats_` `mem` and `conf`, and the genera's `look` and `fam` from `data.json` on GitHub main, `gen_`, cached 6 hours as `gen1`; none when it cannot be read) and the reply carries `replay: {rv, n, rec, ids}` (`rec`: the replayed record per genus and `~level`, replayed with the app's rules of the day, the known mix-ups term of rules version 2 included (`core.js` `CF_LOGGED`: the app's own term taken out of the `e` logged from then on); with per genus `lm`, the time of the last miss that left its progress below the learned mark's keep rule, `core.js` `mxKeeps`, so `replayedOf` drops a mark passed before it, as the game does; `ids`: the ids of the rows replayed from the request's `pt` on, the time of the oldest answer the device still queues, and at least the last 500, so a lost reply's answers still queued are never counted twice: Codex review #399). The anonymous request also carries `reset` (the device's `reefid-sync-coral-reset`): its replay counts only the answers since (Codex review #399). A reply to a save or an anonymous send is adopted only if the device's reset is still the one the request carried (a reset made while it was on its way: the next reply brings the replay from the new reset). On adoption the device's merge baseline (`SYNC.base`) becomes the replayed record without its own uncounted answers, and `mergeSaved` keeps the device's progress over a saved copy whose `reefid-sync-coral-rv` is older than the device's (Codex review #399). Stored in the **Replayed** tab: Name (or `~anon <id>`), Rules (rv), Rows (n), Since (the reset, ms), Updated, Record (JSON in 45,000-character chunks from column F); reused while the rules version, the row count and the reset are the same. The player's Progress data / RAs copy is never written there. Admin delete, merge and rename drop the player's Replayed row. The Answers tab is read once per request (`answerRows_`, shared by `hold_`, `stats_` and `replay_`). An app without `rv` (before v28) gets no replay; an app against an older script gets none and keeps its own progress. **The app waits for script v28** (`core.js` `RV_SHEET`, rule D4): `sync.js` `replayAdopt` adopts a replay only from a reply whose `v` is 28 or more, and the admin shows replayed values (`admin.js` `replayOn`: Players' Progress column and "↻ saved" marks, the Journey's Progress) only when its `readAnswers` or `users` reply says `v` >= 28; before, both do exactly what they did (the sheet's Learned column, the Journey's learned genera from the saved copy). The `rv` field the app's requests carry is ignored by an older script. `admin.html?demo&v=27` answers as an older script (`tests/admin.smoke.js`). The app's side: `sync.js` `replayAdopt` (docs/LEARNING.md). A saved copy whose `reefid-sync-coral-rv` is below the device's (an app from before the replay saved it, or a copy older than the replay) is not merged; the device's version goes back to the copy's, so its next save (`syncPull` asks for one) gets the replay of the whole log again. Except the copy this device saved or loaded last, unchanged since (the reply's `rev` is still `SYNC.srv`; `mergeSaved` `mine`): already part of its progress, it is never merged nor taken for an older app's, whatever its `reefid-sync-coral-rv`; and a replay adopted from a save's reply is followed at once by one more save carrying the adopted version (`syncOnce`), so the saved copy reaches the rules version and the replays stop (Codex review #399: a full replay at every sync, the saved version left at 0). The reply to an anonymous send is ignored when the device signed in or changed anonymous id while it was on its way (`sendAnon` keeps the id it sent). After an adoption the merge baseline `SYNC.base` is the sheet's replay itself (`core.js` `replayBase`), so this device's unsent answers stay on top at the next merge; a reset this device made that the sheet's copy lacks, or a reset from another device while answers after it are unsent here, also sends the rules version back to 0, so the save that follows brings the replay since the reset.
- **Other subjects' replays** (ROADMAP "Fish progress replayed from the answer log", Pierre 2026-11-08): a subject with `SUBJECTS` `replayed` (fish; `core.js` `replayedSubjects`) is replayed by the same `replay_` from its own rows (`subjRows_`, from the same read of the Answers tab as `answerRows_`), with its own units (`replayed.gen` over its first pool file from GitHub main: `core.js` `fishReplayGen` over `fish.json`, the families' and the scientific genera's `look` and family line as the app's `fishGen` and `fishGenera` build them, `tests/fishreplay.test.js`; cached 6 hours as `sgen1:fish`) and none of `stats_`'s photo numbers (the corals' only; every fish answer is logged with its scoring record and replays as the game scored it). Asked per subject by the rules version the app applied to it (each subject its own list: the corals' `RVS`, fish `FISH_RVS`, `core.js` `rvsOf`, `rvOf`, so a fish replay never makes a coral device take its replay again): `POST {…, rvs: {fish: rv}}`, `GET ?…&rv_fish=N`, an anonymous send with `sreset` and `sstart` ({subject: the device's reset and first-launch record}); a signed-in player's reset and first-launch answer are the subject's keys of their data (`reefid-sync-fish-reset`, `reefid-sync-fish-start`). The reply carries `replays: {fish: {rv, n, rec, ids, fp}}` beside the corals' `replay`; an app that sends no `rvs` gets none. Stored in the **Replayed Fish** tab (`rplTab_`, the same columns; from v117 a tab found empty, made by hand or by anything but `sheet_`, gets its header there too and `find_` reads no name in an empty tab: until then the real sheet's empty, headerless "Replayed Fish" made every fish replay throw before it was stored or sent, so no fish player was replayed, `tests/fishadopt.test.js`; from v117 too, a record kept for a player whose key is a member every plain object inherits, `constructor` or `__proto__`, is never reused but made again, `core.js` `replayProgress` having replayed none of their answers before, Codex review #812), dropped with the corals' rows when an account is deleted, merged or archived, or a device's answers are claimed (`rplDrop_`). The app (`sync.js`) asks for it once a reply said the script replays it (`core.js` `REPLAYS_SHEET`; `rvsAsk`, `rv_fish` on the load) and adopts it as the corals' (`replayAdoptS`, run as that subject, with its rules version `reefid-sync-fish-rv` (`rvKey`), its reset as the cutoff, its units from `UNITS` `gen` and none of the corals' photo numbers; `mergeSubject` takes its rules version into the merge as the corals', `rpOn`), with no floor since fish rules version 2 (rule D9, Pierre, 2026-11-09, Q330: a bar may go down; until then the sheet floored the first replay against the account's saved bars, kept with the record as `{P, F}` in "Replayed Fish", which `Code.gs` `rplRec_` still reads) and its own rules versions (`FISH_RVS`; a card told as `fish:<version>`, `progress.js` `rvId`, none from fish rules version 2). A refused save's fish answers (`SYNC.lg`, or a save whose fate is unknown) are not merged while the fish replay cannot be adopted (its units not in yet, or no replay in the reply): the fish rules version goes back to 0 and the replay of its whole log is adopted once it can (`mergeSubject`, Codex review #779; the corals the same way since 2026-11-09). Before that script version fish merges exactly as before. The log is the only reference for progress (rule D9, Pierre 2026-11-09, Q322, Q330; rules version 10, fish rules version 2, sheet v127): `core.js` `replayedOf` and `replayBase` take the replay's record and answer count of a unit also when the device holds more answers there, and a unit the log has no answer on loses the answers the device counts on it (the level's answer count keeps the larger of the two: a follow-up, branch `claude/level-count-from-log`); no floor; no card for the recalculation (rule D5); and the app never adopts a replay of a rules version newer than its own (`replayAdoptS`, `rvOf`), which it could not hold (LEARNING.md "The log, the only reference for progress"). A device that never signed in does not ask yet; signing in drops the device's fish rules version as the corals' (`signIn`), and sign-out clears it.
- **The leaderboard follows the rules** (sheet v118; Pierre, 2026-11-09, rule D8: "whenever progress is recalculated, the leaderboard must be recalculated automatically, even if people do not reconnect"). For every subject with a board and rules versions (`core.js` `rvsOf`: the corals' `RVS`, fish `FISH_RVS`, any future subject's `replayed.rvs`; no per-subject code), `board_` ranks a player whose saved copy applied an older version (its `reefid-sync-<subject>-rv` key below `rvOf`) by the progress and level their app will hold once it adopts the replay: `core.js` `replayAdopted` (the one `sync.js` `replayAdoptS` adopts with: `replayedOf` over the saved copy, and for a subject replayed with a floor the units kept from the copy floored and the floor's typed opportunities kept) of the record the subject's Replayed tab keeps for today's rules, row count and reset (`boardRpl_`, the same test as `replay_`'s reuse); the level is the replayed one too (`replayedOf`: the replay's ability, the larger answer count), from `ELO_MIN` answers as before. No record kept yet, or a player up to date: the saved copy's, as before. The board reads only (one read of the Answers tab and of each Replayed tab, only once a player behind is met; nothing fetched, no lock) and stays cached 5 minutes. The records are made ahead by the existing 15-minute trigger (`autoUpdate`, once it finds the script up to date, after the one-shot migrations: `boardWarm_`): every listed player (opt-out, admin hide and archive left out) behind the rules whose record is missing or stale gets the same replay `replay_` makes (`rplMake_`, outside the script lock, data.json / fish.json / the photo stats fetched there if not cached), all written under one short lock (`rplPut_`), each only if, read again under the lock, nothing it was made from changed meanwhile: the account still there under the same name and code and listed, the same saved data, the same answers of that subject since its reset, its Replayed row as read (Codex review #810, rule D7: an account deleted while its replay was made never gets its row back; else dropped, made again at the next run if still needed); one player's failure never stops the others' (and the board falls back to the saved copy for them); every map keyed by a player name has no prototype, `core.js` `replayProgress`'s per-player map too (a player named `constructor` is an entry like any other); new replays started only within `BOARD_WARM_MS` (150 s) of the run, the rest at the next run; a board that got one is dropped from the cache and the Deploy log counts them. The player's own next sync reuses that very record (`replay_`), so the board shows what their app then shows. After a merge that appends a rules version, the boards follow within about 30 minutes (the self-update, then the next run). `tests/board.test.js`.
- The app keeps unsent answers (max 2,000) until the server confirms `log: true`.
- Tests: `node tests/run.js` (every `tests/*.test.js` under `node --test`, in the machine slot of `tests/slot.js`, rule W24; CI runs `node --test tests/*.test.js`) runs `tools/sync/Code.gs` against an in-memory sheet (`tests/gas.js`), and checks `core.js` (`merge3`, `mergeTabs`, `mergeProgress`, `accountOf`, `eloOf` against `stats_`, `confusionOf` against `stats_` `conf`, `confScore`, `pairConf`); `tests/photos.test.js` checks the photo rules on the real data and curation. `tests/pages.smoke.js` (CI job `pages`, Playwright) opens the main routes, two malformed links and `admin.html?readonly` at 390 and 1280 px, external hosts cut off, and fails on any page error; it also signs in against a sync script answered inside the page (a wrapped `fetch`, the script's hosts mapped to 127.0.0.1, nothing reaches the sheet): a name taken with another code, "Is it you?" on a device with answers of its own and on a fresh one, No (nothing saved or sent), Yes (signed in and merged), a new name (no question), and the tour's sign-in step. It also checks the sheet's full replay adopted by a stale signed-in device (`replayAdoptCheck`: the replayed record with its own unsent answer on top, sent, not adopted twice; a version with a `msg` shows its card once at load, after the update note due at the same load (`RECALC`), one without nothing; `SMOKE_ONLY=replay`, formerly `PAGES_ONLY=replay`, runs that check alone). `tests/replay.test.js` replays a device's log of typed and picked answers (exam included) and finds the record the app scored live; `tests/sync.test.js` checks `replay_` (the embedded engine is `core.js`, stored once, replayed again on a new rules version or new rows, the reset, anonymous devices, the saved copy untouched). `tests/draw.smoke.js` (same job) runs the real `photoSet()` and `distractors()` in the page for every genus at 4 player levels and checks one observation per question, no multi photo, bonus genera Research Grade only, and the asked number of traps among 3 distinct choices. `tests/admin.smoke.js` opens `admin.html?demo` at 390 and 1280 px (after each step it waits for no demo call in flight, `admin-demo.js` `window.DEMO_PENDING`, and no "Loading…" note, over three frames, not a fixed time), every view of the 9 tabs and every old address (each must land on its tab, in the tab row and the phone menu, and its view, with no sideways scroll), checks that each tab and group reopens its view used last (after a reload too), To do › All (Health's line first, reports, errors), the Where to look banner, Put back in Hidden and the genus page's view filter, then opens a player, merges two names, deletes one, checks the reports in To do, Skip and Reject in Candidates and Publish, and fails if a call reaches the sync script or GitHub or a real admin storage key is written.

## Security model

What is public, what protects what (code review, 2026-11-01).
- **Everything in the repo is public**, including the admin page code and the sync URL. The admin password only unlocks the token stored in that browser; it protects nothing on its own.
- **The GitHub token is the real key**: fine-grained, this repo only, Contents read/write, 90-day expiry. Kept encrypted in `localStorage` (`reefid-admin-vault`); the unlocked token lives in `sessionStorage` only. `ppericard.github.io` is shared by every Pages site of the account, so never add third-party scripts: the CSP (`<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy">` in `index.html` and `admin.html`) allows scripts from this site only, inline scripts by hash (`tests/csp.test.js` fails if a hash is stale). Add a host to `img-src` / `connect-src` there when a new external source is needed. `index.html` and `admin.html` also allow `https://www.fishbase.se` (and the older `https://www.fishbase.de`) in `img-src` (the FishBase fish candidates and sheet photo, shown remote; `tests/csp.test.js` checks every candidate host is allowed). The R2 bucket (`https://pub-934250f83b884a4f890497f2027ce251.r2.dev`) is in `img-src` (the photos) and `connect-src` (any `fetch()` of a photo; the service worker's own requests follow no page CSP).
- **Templates**: every data string goes through `esc()` (escapes `& < > " '`); links from data go through `safeUrl()` (https only).
- **Sync sheet**: anyone can call the Apps Script URL. A name + 4-digit code only prevents accidental overwrites; v6 throttles wrong codes, never writes a user string as a formula, and keeps anonymous ids unguessable (secret salt). RA data is low-sensitivity (first names and quiz answers).
- **GitHub Action** (`approved-photos.yml`, `contents: write`): downloads photos only from iNaturalist hosts (`tools/fetch_approved.py` `HOSTS`); its `fish` job only reads the iNaturalist API (`tools/fetch_fish.py --refill`, the project's User-Agent of `tools/inat.py`, no personal email) and downloads no photo.
- **Photos to R2** (`photos-r2.yml` and the last job of `approved-photos.yml` (its `r2` job), `tools/r2_sync.sh`; Pierre, 2026-11-03; both in the concurrency group `photos-r2`): `img/` copied to the Cloudflare R2 bucket (public address `https://pub-934250f83b884a4f890497f2027ce251.r2.dev`, CORS for the site and localhost) with rclone (the official release, pinned to one version in `tools/r2_sync.sh` and checked against its SHA-256 before it runs; never the runner's apt package) and an R2 API token limited to that bucket (Object Read & Write), from the repo's secrets; files compared by checksum; **nothing is ever deleted from the bucket** (`rclone copy`, since step 2: the photos with an online original live there only), and the run fails if an R2-only photo lacks one of its four files. The `photos` job of `approved-photos.yml` uploads the photos `tools/fetch_approved.py` downloaded (`tools/r2_sync.sh r2-upload`, which fails without the secrets) before its commit. The app and the admin read the photos from the bucket ("Photos").

## Tools (`tools/`)

Python 3 scripts, run from the repo root. Many were one-off steps of the original build and read a scratchpad that no longer exists (`make_site.py`, `build_photos.py`, `apply_levels.py`, `build_lecture_more.py`, `coral_finder_book/*`): they now live in `tools/archive/` (kept for reference, cannot be re-run); edit `data.json` directly instead, and write it with `tools/datajson.py`.

Still useful / re-runnable:
| Script | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `timings.js` | The sheet's `Timings` tab summed up (Progress sync, "`Timings` tab"): read the tab read-only through the Google Sheets connector (range `Timings!A1:G`, saved as the JSON it returns) or as a CSV, then `node tools/timings.js <file>` (`--json`, `--day` / `--week`): per operation the weighted count, median, p90, max ms and most rows read, the trend over time, and the quota lines (6 min an execution, 90 min of triggers a day). Pure functions exported for `tests/timings.test.js`. |
| `sync_core.py` | Copies `core.js` into `tools/sync/Code.gs` between its BEGIN / END markers (`--fix`), or checks the copy is current (`--check`, CI): the sync script replays progress with the app's own engine ("Full replay" under Progress sync). After a `--fix`, add the sheet changes file (`tools/sheet_version.py`), never a `VERSION` bump. |
| `photos.py` | The four photo sizes (`SIZES`, `save()`, `remove()`); where a photo lives (`online()`: R2 only, `gitonly()`: git, `every()`, `path()`: a local file, from R2 if need be, `url()`); fills missing smaller files of `img/`, `--check` (CI), `--r2-list` (`r2_sync.sh`). |
| `r2_move.py` | Photos out of the repo, step 2: lists what leaves `img/` (online original) and what stays, by kind, owner and genus, with sizes; `--verify` checks each file on R2 byte for byte; `--apply` removes from `img/` the files R2 holds identically. |
| `fetch_approved.py`, `fetch_local.py` | Candidate download/refill (run by the GitHub Action; `fetch_local.py <Genus…>` to limit, `--plan` to see offline what it would refill). `fetch_local.py` searches the iNaturalist taxon `taxonomy.json` gives each genus (`taxonomy.py` `inat_taxon`). `fetch_approved.py --games` (the Action passes it) also downloads any photo of Millepora and the bonus genera still remote (`r: 1`, as `build_extra.py` imports them): every game photo is hosted like the others (2026-11-03: their 80 photos downloaded). The other extra Dauin genera (Coral ID Key only) stay on iNaturalist. |
| `cand_gaps.py` | Game genera short of candidates waiting (rule W20); Key-only genera are left out. Asks iNaturalist as `fetch_local.py` does (its `get`, paced with retries; the genus taxon from `taxonomy.json`). |
| `photo_quality.py` | Automatic quality → `quality.json`. |
| `photo_metrics.py` | Objective metrics (sharpness, size, colour cast) of live photos and iNat candidates → `metrics.json` (auto-sort tie-breakers and sanity flags; `--live`, `--cand`, `--calib` against Pierre's ratings). |
| `find_dups.py` | Near-duplicates → `dups.json` (re-run after adding photos). |
| `tag_origin.py` | `loc` tag (dauin/ph/world) on iNat photos. |
| `judging_manifest.py`, `judging_import.py`, `judging_cand.py` | Outside photo-judging agent package (`judging/`); `--check` validates. |
| `apply_id_tips.py` | `tools/id_tips.txt` → `gen[g].qt`. |
| `apply_imr_corrections.py` | Four IMR wordings corrected by Pierre after the plain sentences' sources (2026-11-03): Seriatopora, Lobactis, Heliofungia, Herpolitha (lecture notes, review slides, mnemonic, `sum`, `check` → `info`; the ID tips in `id_tips.txt`). |
| `apply_some_species.py` | Traits of some species only (`gen[g].trs`): Favites `pali`, Goniastrea `plo` (species present in the Philippines by rule C6; its header gives the sources and why Dipsastraea gets none). |
| `apply_favites.py` | Favites and its neighbours (Pierre, 2026-11-04, after a research report): sourced "Other sources" notes on Favites and Goniastrea (species recorded in the Philippines only, rule C6), Favites `sum` and size class `s3`, *F. magnistellata* restored in the slide 65 line, `spell` for Diploastreidae. Its header lists what was checked (iNaturalist counts, Corals of the World, WoRMS). |
| `apply_plain_sheets.py` | The plain sentence of every genus (`gen` and `x.gen`) → `plain` (re-runnable; `build_extra.py` keeps it on a rebuild). Its sources per genus: `tools/plain_sources.json`. |
| `apply_imr_forms.py` | IMR growth form definitions into `forms.json`. |
| `game_forms.js` | Game photos with no growth form (`node tools/game_forms.js`), and `--apply FILE` to write a growth-form pass's guesses into `forms.json` `gf` / `gunsure` (never over Pierre's curation `gf`). |
| `build_extra.py` | Extra Dauin genera for the Coral ID Key (`data.json` `x`). |
| `apply_book_rewrite.py`, `book_rewrite.json` | The Coral Finder texts (criteria, summaries, look-alike sources, Coral ID Key box labels) that followed the book too closely, reworded in our own words (Pierre, 2026-11-08; chosen by comparing each text with an OCR of its cited pages, outside the repo: a 6-word run shared, or 80% of its words in the book's order). The map keeps only hashes of the old wording and of its 5-word runs, never book text; `tests/booktext.test.js` fails when any of these fields shares a 5-word run with the old wording again (`--check`: exit 1 when a listed text has its old wording). `build_extra.py` holds the same new wording for the bonus genera. |
| `apply_bonus.py` | Bonus genera in games: marks photos of non-Research-Grade observations `gid: 1` (asks iNat) and moves their multi-coral photos to `more`. Its list is `core.js` `BENTHIC` + `BONUS` (`tools/genera.py`; Millepora's photos follow the same rule); re-run after `build_extra.py`. |
| `inat.py` | The one HTTP client of every tool that asks iNaturalist (rule C7; also WoRMS for `build_fish.py` and `taxonomy.py`): `get(url)` waits `PACE` after the last request to that host (1 s iNaturalist, 0.3 s WoRMS), sends the project's User-Agent (`UA`: `coral-id-imr/1.0 (IMR Reef ID)`, no personal address or account), retries a 429, a 5xx, a network error or a cut reply after `WAITS` (5, 15, 30, 60, 120 s; Retry-After when given, at most 300 s), never another 4xx; `missing=True` reads a 204 / 404 as None. The batched counts: `leaf_counts` (`species_counts` for many taxa, exact where iNaturalist's leaf counting allows, else None to ask alone) and `observer_counts` (the distinct observers of the taxa with few observations counted from their observations read together, `observations/observers` for the others), used by `presence.py`, `build_fish.py`, `fish_commonness.py`; `chunks` for `id=` / `taxon_id=` lists. Tests: `tests/inat.test.js` with a made-up iNaturalist (`tests/inatstub.py`). |
| `genera.py` | Shared helper: the game genera and the Key-only ones (`game_genera`, `key_only`, as `core.js` `gameOf`), read from `core.js` `BENTHIC` / `BONUS`, and `cap_sheet()` (`CAP.sheet`). |
| `apply_covers.py`, `apply_look_sources.py`, `apply_sources.py`, `apply_review_slides.py`, `apply_cmp.py`, `apply_multi.py` | One-time content passes, idempotent; document how fields were built. |
| `commonness.py` | How common each game genus is near Dauin (iNaturalist, read only: verifiable observations and distinct observers within 40 km, plus the old name of a genus whose `crit`/`imr` lines say "formerly X") → `cm` on its `data.json` record (geometric mean). Prints the table; `--dry` writes nothing. Re-run to update (Pierre, 2026-11-03). |
| `fish_refs.py` | The fish units' reference photos (iNaturalist covers and every taxon-page photo passing the game photo rules, FishBase's photos) → `tools/fish_refs.json`, by species, and `away`: the taxon-page photos outside the Indo-West Pacific, listed only with their rough region (`region()`; `--relabel` labels the list again after a change to it, offline) (Q230); judged ones join the game photos ("Fish candidates", Reference photos). |
| `fish_draw_sim.js` | The fish draw's easy share in the real app: `model.js` `photoSet` drawn per entry and stage of a player's level, the share of photos under ease 4 (`--photos FILE`, `--calib JSON`, `--entries`, `--draws`; Playwright, in the machine slot); docs/LEARNING.md "The fish draw in big pools". |
| `fetch_fish.py` | Fish candidate photos from iNaturalist → `fish-cand.json` ("Fish candidates" above; `--plan` offline, `--only`, `--circle`, `--cap`; `--rep` tags the candidates that are their taxon's iNaturalist cover, 30 taxa a request; `--refill` refills the short species only and remembers the ones searched to the end in `tools/fish_refill.json`, Q171, run by the Candidate photos workflow). |
| `fetch_fishbase.py` | Fish candidate photos from FishBase → `fish-cand.json`, after `fetch_fish.py` ("Fish candidates" above): `--fishbase DIR` reads the rfishbase parquet snapshot (pyarrow) into `tools/fishbase_src.json`, then offline (`--plan`, `--only`, `--circle`, `--cap`); also the FishBase source of the sheet-only photos (`fish_photos.py --sheet`). |
| `judging_fish.py` | The fish candidate pass on the fish rubric (judging/FISH.md "Candidates"): contact sheets, `--check`, import into `suggest.json` `fish`. |
| `presence.py`, `presence_checklists.json` | The presence rule, defined once (rule C8): `near_dauin`, `ph_confirmed`, the checklists (FishBase Table 1 for the Philippines, Corals of the World's Philippine ecoregions, derived name lists of 2026-11-04), `species_presence` / `confirmed_species` per genus. Read by `build_fish.py`, `fetch_local.py`, `coral_presence.py`. Checklist names are matched through the species rows of `taxonomy.json` (`taxonomy.py` `aliases`: the name WoRMS accepts now and iNaturalist's spelling), for the subjects the file holds (corals now). A Corals of the World name mapped by decision rather than by WoRMS carries its reason in `cotw.decided` (Pierre, 2026-11-05, Q50: *Plesiastrea versipora* of the Philippine lists = *P. peroni*, Juszkiewicz et al. 2022; `presence.json` picks it up at the next `coral_presence.py` run). |
| `taxonomy.py`, `taxonomy.json` | The build-time reference of names (Pierre, 2026-11-05, Q49; [taxonomy study](reviews/2026-11-05-coral-taxonomy-study.md) section 5, small form): for every `data.json` genus, its role, the app's own wording copied verbatim (`app`: family, the IMR lecture's "formerly…" lines; never corrected, rule C1), WoRMS (id, status, family, accepted species) and iNaturalist (id, family, the species it lacks or spells otherwise); the former genus names (hand-listed, plus WoRMS's genus synonyms still met on iNaturalist or in a checklist or photo name); species rows where they pay (WoRMS moves into an app genus whose old name iNaturalist knows, Corals of the World names and photo names, served or candidates (waiting ones included), WoRMS writes otherwise); the watch list (Q51: the Favites revision and *F. irregularis*, the unnamed genus inside *Fungia fungites*, Tubipora's lineages, *Cyphastrea agassizi*: reported when WoRMS or iNaturalist moves, never applied); a dated log of the changes each refresh found. **The app never loads it.** One section per subject (`SUBJECTS` in the tool: corals now, the fish later), one record per line. WoRMS batched (50 names a call), iNaturalist in bulk (the Scleractinia genera and species, 500 a page) and paced (~1 request/s, User-Agent, 429 / 5xx retries); replies cached in `tools/.cache/taxonomy.json`, so a rerun is offline and identical (`--offline`, `--refresh`, `--dry`); `--check` (offline, `tests/taxonomy.test.js`) checks the layout, `names.json` in step and the copied wording against `data.json`. Monthly, and before a content PR that touches names or families. **Its readers** (ROADMAP "Taxonomy reference", Pierre 2026-11-05, Q49): `inat_taxon(genus)`, the iNaturalist taxon `fetch_local.py`, `cand_gaps.py` and `coral_presence.py` search for a genus (`gen.<genus>.inat.id`: no search by name, no homonym guard; a genus the file does not hold, or with an inactive taxon, is skipped and said so; another taxon only by Pierre's decision); `aliases(name)`, through which `presence.py` matches checklist names (a species row brings the name WoRMS accepts now and iNaturalist's spelling of it). **`names.json`**, written with the file (and alone, offline, by `--names`): the admin's extract, `{v, doc, coral: {date, sp: {name: {now, iname?, inat?}}, gen: {former genus: genus now}}}` (the species rows, and the former genus names that are no longer a genus of their own); the admin loads it (`side('names.json', ADM0.id)`), never `taxonomy.json`, and `presence.js` `nameFlag(p, nm)` puts a neutral **WoRMS: <name>** tag next to the source of a photo or candidate whose name (`t`) is a former name, a former genus or iNaturalist's spelling of a name WoRMS writes otherwise (`admin.js` `nameTag`, in `srcTag`; its tooltip says which). Information only: nothing is renamed (rule C1). A candidate fetched after the last refresh gets its row at the next one. |
| `coral_presence.py` | `presence.json`: the presence rule per coral photo species, for the admin's "out of rule" flag (`presence.js`; "Presence of coral species" above). |
| `apply_loc_fix.py` | Hand corrections of a photo's `loc` (2026-11-04: five Anacropora photos from Sabah labelled ph → world). |
| `build_fish.py` | Fish content: `tools/fish_source.json` + WoRMS and iNaturalist (cached in `tools/.cache/`) → `fish.json` (see "Fish content" above); `--commonness` re-applies the species order offline. |
| `fish_common.py` | The curated common names per fish species (1 to 3, written into `fish.json` `cn` by the build; `tools/fish_common.json`, inputs `tools/fish_common_src.json`): `--inat` (batched, ~1 request/s), `--fishbase DIR` (the rfishbase parquet tables, pyarrow), then offline; `--check`. docs/FISH_PATH.md "Common names". |
| `fish_commonness.py` | The fish species' commonness index within their entry (field counts near Dauin, IMR 24, iNaturalist observers in three rings; inputs `tools/fish_commonness.json`, `tools/fish_commonness_inat.json`), applied to `fish.json` by `build_fish.py`. `--fetch` asks iNaturalist (batched, ~1 request/s, cached); with entry ids, prints each species' raw values. docs/FISH_PATH.md "Species order: the commonness index". |
| `datajson.py` | Shared helper: the one `data.json` writer (`load()`, `dump(d, path)`, one genus per line). Every script above that writes `data.json` uses it. Run alone it rewrites the file in that layout; `--check` is the PR check. |
| `excluded.py`, `excluded.json` | Shared helper: excluded photos (imports skip them, `drop_excluded` keeps what it removes in `excluded.json`, `restore_unexcluded` puts back what Pierre restored). `apply_excluded_archive.py` seeds `excluded.json` from git history; `--files` takes a restored lecture photo's file back from it. |
| `apply_ratios.py` | Photo shape `ar` into `data.json` (quiz layout before photos load); run after adding photos (the Action runs it too). |
| `apply_no_book.py` | Takes every Coral Finder book crop record out of `data.json` and `cand.json` (`gen` `book`, the key boxes' `ph`; Pierre, 2026-11-08); re-runnable, `tests/nobook.test.js` guards it. |
| `curplay.py` | The players' copy of the curation, `curation-play.json`, written at deploy (`pages.yml`, on the staged copy, before the stamps) and by the smokes (`tests/calm.js`): `curation.json` without what the app never reads ("Front end", the service worker) |
| `apply_skeletons.py` | The coral skeleton photos (rule P6, Pierre, 2026-11-09): `skel: 1` on every record `judging/skeletons.json` lists, in `data.json` and `cand.json`, and on no other (`--check`: exit 1 when they disagree); `core.js` `applyCuration` drops them, the admin (`own`, `rawCands`) and `fetch_local.py` (`colonies`, waiting) skip them (`tests/skeletons.test.js`). Re-run after editing the list or rebuilding records (`build_extra.py`). |
| `stamp_versions.py` | Version numbers stamped at deploy (`pages.yml`, on the staged copy): every `?v=` a content hash, `LAB_V` and the `sw.js` Lab list one hash of the Lab files, `sw.js` `V` a hash of its `SHELL`, the build number in the pages; PRs never edit them (rule W15). |
| `check_versions.py` | Cache-busting consistency, in the source and in a stamped copy (`--dir`): one `?v=` per file everywhere, `sw.js` `SHELL` lists what `index.html` and `app.js` load, the Lab list matches `LAB.files` and `LAB_V`. No bumps: the deploy stamps the numbers. In the source it also runs `sheet_version.py`'s check (`--sheet-base REF` on a PR, CI passing the merge ref's `HEAD^1`; `--main` on `main` or a merge train). |
| `sheet_version.py` | The sheet script's `VERSION`, assigned at merge (rule D3): `--check` (a PR waiting for its version carries one `tools/sync/changes/<branch>.md` and leaves `VERSION` and the header line as on its base; an assigned one is the base plus one entry; `--main`, no changes file left, no gate above `VERSION`, no bare `vNEXT`) and `--assign` (the integrator, at merge: `VERSION` + 1, the entry into the header line under the Manila date and into the version history, every gate the PR adds or changes against `main` and every bare `vNEXT` set to it, the file removed; `--dry-run`); `tests/sheetversion.test.js`. |
| `train.py` | The merge train (rule W26): `python3 tools/train.py [--dry-run] N…` checks each PR (open, on `main`, no draft, no `needs-pierre`, green on its head, no sheet changes file), builds `main` plus their heads in a temporary worktree (the sheet rules checked by `main`'s copy of `sheet_version.py`: no PR code runs), pushes `train/<UTC time>`, waits for Checks and Smokes on it, squash-merges each PR at its tested head in order (stopping if `main` moved) and compares `main`'s tree with the train's. `gh api` GET and PUT only, no force-push; `tests/train.test.js` (a stand-in `gh`, a bare origin). |
| `stack.json`, `stack_check.py` | The technology stack's pins (Pierre, 2026-11-05: the whole stack on the latest stable, rule W9): one item per runtime, tool, runner image, data snapshot or external API whose version the repository chooses and no package manager tracks (Node, Python, the runner label, Prettier, Playwright, Pillow, rclone, the FishBase snapshot, the iNaturalist and GitHub REST API versions, the Apps Script runtime), with where each is written and where its latest stable is looked up. `--check` (offline, the PR check) fails when a pin in the repository differs from `stack.json`; `--latest` looks every item up at its source and prints what is behind (informational, exit 0; `--all`, `--json`). `tools/brief.py` shows the `--latest` count once a Manila day (main's copy, 3 s at most, cached in `tools/.cache/stack-latest.json`). GitHub Actions are Dependabot's (`.github/dependabot.yml`). |

## Reference material (outside the repo)

- IMR Lecture 6 (2026_L6_CoralID.pptx) and a Google Slides copy, plus the Coral Finder 2022 PDF, are on Pierre's Google Drive (IMR folder). Slide text and presenter notes were extracted into `data.json` (`imr`, `mn`, `sl`).
- Pierre's iNaturalist: user `ppericard`, coral taxon 47532.

## Run and deploy

- Run: `python3 -m http.server 8000` at the repo root, open <http://localhost:8000> (`admin.html` for the back-office; without a token use `admin.html?demo`, made-up data, or `admin.html?readonly`, the real public curation). Sync and photo Elo talk to the live Apps Script even locally.
- Deploy: `.github/workflows/pages.yml` publishes current `main` as a static artifact, with one `pages` concurrency group and `cancel-in-progress: true`. Settings > Pages must use **GitHub Actions**; the workflow fails early while the legacy branch source is selected. Every workflow and its triggers, incident evidence and CLI recovery: [PAGES.md](PAGES.md).
- Pierre's Publish pushes `curation.json` to `main`, triggering both Deploy Pages and Candidate photos (which also runs on a push changing `suggest.json`, `tools/fish_judge.json`, `tools/fish_refs.json` or `tools/fish_game.py`). The latter commits `data.json`, `cand.json`, `excluded.json`, `fish-game.json` and `img/` (and, in its `fish` job, `fish-cand.json` and `tools/fish_refill.json`) using `GITHUB_TOKEN`; its completion triggers Deploy Pages again so bot changes are published too.
- The service worker serves the cached shell: the deploy stamps every `?v=` and `sw.js` `V` from content hashes (`tools/stamp_versions.py`, rule W15), so a shell change reaches the players without anyone bumping a number. `curation.json` is fetched with `no-cache` (from the kept copy first at start-up, see above).
- Pushes to `main` never run Checks (the admin's Publish, the Candidate photos bot): Deploy Pages first checks that every tracked JSON file parses (`tools/check_json.py`, also the Checks step on pull requests) and the `data.json` layout (`tools/datajson.py --check`), so a broken commit fails the deploy and the last good site stays live; fix it with a new commit.
- A completed Actions run can leave a stale Pages backend lock. Cancel the blocking **Pages deployment** as well as any stuck Actions run, then dispatch `pages.yml` on `main`; see [PAGES.md](PAGES.md#manual-recovery). Repeatedly rerunning the blocking legacy SHA is not the fix.
- `.gitattributes`: every text file stored with LF (`* text=auto eol=lf`); the machine-written JSON (`cand.json`, `fish-cand.json`, `fish-game.json`, `suggest.json`) marked `linguist-generated`, so GitHub collapses their diffs (`data.json`, the hand-edited source of truth, stays shown); `docs/`, `tests/`, `tools/`, `judging/`, `.github/` and `.claude/` are `export-ignore`, so the deploy's `git archive` leaves them out of the site (nothing it serves loads them: `tests/artifact.test.js`; the sync script reads `tools/sync/Code.gs` from raw.githubusercontent.com). `.gitignore` also keeps out `node_modules/` (a Playwright install by hand) and `.DS_Store`.

## Players' reports (2026-11-01)

In every game (Quiz, Custom quiz, Look-alike duels, Memory, Reveal), once the answer is out, the feedback shows a discreet "⚑ Report a problem" (`repBtn()` in `sync.js`; Lab games call it if present). It opens a small dialog (`reportDialog()`): Wrong answer (with a "Right genus, if you know" field, suggestions from the 39 genera), Several corals, Bad photo, Other (in the current subject's words, `SUBJECTS` `one` and `several`: with Fish on, "Right family, if you know" with the entries' IMR names, never their teaching ids, and "Several fish species"), an optional note, and "Your name" when the player is not signed in to the sync. A report carries the photos of the question (one observation), the expected genus, the answer given and the game. Reports queue in `localStorage` `reefid-reports` and are sent by `sendReports()` (on send, start-up and when back online) to `Code.gs` v7; an older script leaves them queued. Admin To do (views All and Reports; script v8) lists them first ("Reports and feedback", `renderTriage` `repCard`), with the reporter's name (a `?` after it = not verified by a sync code). **One action bar on every report** (Pierre, 2026-11-08, Q309): its **status**, one click each (Undo the safety net): **Done** (resolved; `curation.json` `seen` `rep:<id>`, so the player who sent it is thanked, Q66), **Stand-by** and **Dismiss** (`curation.json` **`reps`** `{id: 'wait'|'drop'}`, never in `seen`: no thanks), and Reopen once handled; through the draft and Publish (Publish counts them as report statuses), open reports first, Stand-by below them (muted), Done and Dismissed folded with their counts (`REP_STATUS`, `repStatus`, `repStatusSet`, `repsOrder` in **`reports.js`**, admin only, `tests/reports.test.js`); then the tools the report carries what they need for: **Journey** (the reporter is on the players' list: their journey in the report's subject), **Open genus / Open family** (the unit's admin page: a coral genus page, or a fish family's Photos › Tree page, also for a fish genus or species id), **Open page** (the app page it was sent from), and on a report with no photo **Attach a photo** (the photos of its unit, a unit picker first when it names none; the photo picked shows with its tools, kept in this tab only, never saved). Its photos carry every tool of their page (Q309; Pierre, 2026-11-09, on a fish report whose photo had only Remove / Not this / Open in Families: "the same tools"): a coral photo its genus page's tile (`coralRepTile`, `tile0` without the drag handle: quality, growth form, view, ★ Cover, Remove, Crop with the rotation, Not this genus, ×), a fish photo its family page's, one renderer and one binder for both (`fishTile`, `bindFishTiles` in `admin.js`; `fishRepTile` reads whether it is in play, featured or the cover as the family page does, `fuFam` once per draft, under the species it is in play for and that species' family, `fishFamOf`, a photo moved with Not this into another family included): Feature, ★ Cover, Remove, Not this (a candidate photo), Crop (with the rotation, `admin-fish.js` `fishCrop`), Tags (its Ease, phase and the rubric's tag rows and flags, `fishRows` / `bindFishRows` as on the fish Candidates card, unfolded on that tile only, which on a family page then takes a whole row on a phone and two columns from 600 px), ×, enlarge in place, plus Open in Tree; a count of Other species in view (code `m`, several species on one photo; the Count row reads One fish, Same species, Other species in view, Unclear, each with its tooltip, codes unchanged) or Unclear set on a photo in play also takes it out of play in the same Undo step (rule P3: the game never plays such a photo by itself, `autosort.js` `fishSure`, `tools/fish_game.py` `judged_sure`); a fish flag's tile the same, the tools that take the photo out clearing the flag, plus Done. A reason names the organism in its subject's words (`reports.js` `repWhyText`: "Several corals", "Several fish species", "Wrong family", as the player's dialog put it; the sheet keeps the key, so old reports read the same). What a report is about, whatever it was sent from: `reports.js` `repCtx` (its source: game, photo, sheet, marks, page; its unit, the expected one, else the unit whose sheet its page shows: a fish species id the script does not keep, a feedback sent from a sheet; its photos; its page; its subject: the unit's, its photos', its page's, or none). **The list follows the Corals | Fish switch** (rule A4): each side lists its subject's reports and those about no subject (Home, About, the leaderboard), and counts its own open ones (the switch, the Inbox, To do and its views: `repsOpen(S)`, `todoHere`). Send feedback from a game carries the question's unit and photos (`app.js` `fbCtx`, `quiz.js` `qNow`), as a report does; the sheet already keeps them (no script change). Since v8 the same dialog also opens from "⚑ Report" in the caption of any enlarged photo (lightbox, not while a game hides the genus; reasons Wrong genus, Several corals, Bad photo, Other), "⚑ Report a problem with this sheet" at the bottom of a genus sheet (Wrong information, Bad photo, Other) and "Send feedback" at the end of the ? help of every page and on About and credits (Wrong information, Bug, Suggestion, Other; a page footer until 2026-11-03); `REP_WHY[kind]`, and every report carries the page it came from (`w`, sheet column `Where`). Nothing is ever changed automatically from a report. Pierre's own ⚑ flag (`flag.js`) is separate and stays admin-only. It is one brick for every subject: it names a photo by the `data-f` its page puts on its `<img>` (the game, the lightbox, remote thumbnails), else by its file under `img/`, and asks the subject's data what that name is (`app.js` `UNITS` `photo(f)`, the current subject first): its id in that subject's curation (fish: `core.js` `fishPid`) and what the flag records (corals: genus and view; fish: the photo's name, its unit, species and view). `localStorage` `reefid-photo-flags` holds `{f: id, s: subject, ..., where, at}`; the admin's `importFlags` puts each into its subject's part of `curation.json` (`SUBJECTS` `admin.cur`: `flag` at the top level for the corals, `fish.flag` for fish), never another's, and To do › Reports lists the subject on screen's flags. A fish flag's × writes `fish.exclude` under the photo's name (`fishPhotos` drops its other copies by `fishPid`), its Remove `fish.seen`; each, and Done, clears it.
